We also report the ratio of the median length of the confidence intervals of a particular estimator to that of the NA estimator in the corresponding parentheses. Generally speaking, the confidence intervals of the TSLS and adjusted estimators (L, NL, F, NP, and R) are 20%-30% shorter, in terms of the median, than that of the NA estimator.

Most observations uncovered in DGP (i) carry forward to DGP (ii). Two new patterns emerge. First, the powers of the L, S, NL, F, NP, SNP, and R estimators are much higher than those of the NA and TSLS estimators. Second, the ratio of the median length of the confidence intervals of the TSLS estimator is as wide as that of the NA estimator, whereas the confidence intervals of the adjusted estimators (L, NL, F, NP, and R) become 25%-40% shorter, in terms of the median, than that of the NA estimator. This is probably because the true specifications for
Y_{i}(a)
become more nonlinear.

We now consider DGP (iii). In this setting, only the NA and R estimators are feasible. When
n=200
, both estimators have the correct sizes but the R estimator has considerably higher power. When
n=400
, the sizes of these two estimators remain relatively unchanged, while their powers improve with a diverging gap. The confidence intervals of the R estimator are 60%-65% shorter, in terms of the median, than that of the NA estimator.

In Section
Q
of the Online Supplement, we simulate data with heterogeneous
\pi(s)
. We find that all estimators except TSLS have their empirical rejection rates close to the nominal size of 5% under the null. TSLS, on the other hand, has around 15% rejection rate when
n=1200
. This indicates the TSLS estimator is inconsistent when
\pi(s)
is heterogeneous, in line with Theorem
2.1
.

6.4
Practical Recommendation

If researchers want to use parametric adjustments without tuning parameters, we recommend the F estimator, which is guaranteed to be weakly more efficient than TSLS, L, and NL estimators. Regressors
\Psi_{i,s}
can include linear, quadratic and interaction terms of the original covariates. If researchers want to achieve the SEB by using sieve bases and/or the dimension of covariates is high relative to the sample size, we recommend the R estimator.

7
Empirical Application

Banking the unbanked is considered to be the first step toward broader
financial inclusion -- the focus of the World Bank’s Universal Financial
Access 2020
initiative.
13
13

13

https://www.worldbank.org/en/topic/financialinclusion/brief/achieving-universal-financial-access-by-2020

In a field experiment with a CAR design,
Dupas
et al. 2018
examined the
impact of expanding access to basic saving accounts for rural households
living in three countries: Uganda, Malawi, and Chile. In particular, apart
from the intent-to-treat effects for the whole sample, they also studied the
local average treatment effects for the households who actively used the accounts. This section presents an application of our regression adjusted estimators to the same dataset to examine the LATEs of opening bank accounts on savings balance– a central outcome of interest in their study.

We focus on the experiment conducted in Uganda. The sample consists of 2,160
households who were randomized with a CAR design. Specifically, within each of
41 strata formed by gender, occupation, and bank branch, half of households
were randomly allocated to the treatment group, the other half to the control
one. Households in the treatment group were then offered a voucher to open
bank accounts with no financial costs. However, not every treated household
ever opened and used the saving accounts for deposit. In fact, among those households with treatment assignment, only 41.87% of them opened the accounts and made at least
one deposit within 2 years. Subject compliance is therefore imperfect in this experiment.

The randomization design apparently satisfies statements (i), (ii) and (iii) of Assumption
1
. The target fraction of treatment assignment is 1/2. Because
\max_{s\in\mathcal{S}}|\frac{B_{n}(s)}{n(s)}|\approx 0.056
,
it is plausible to claim that Assumption
1
(iv) is also
satisfied. Since households in the control group need to pay for the fees of
opening accounts while the treated ones bear no financial costs, no-defiers
statement in Assumption
1
(v) holds plausibly in this case.

One of the key analyses in
Dupas
et al. 2018
is to estimate the treatment
effects on savings for active users -- households who actually opened the
accounts and made at least one deposit within 2 years. We follow their
footprints to estimate the same LATEs at savings balance.
14
14

14

Savings
balance includes savings in formal financial intuitions, mobile money, cash at
home or in secret place, savings in ROSCA/VSLA, savings with friends/family,
other cash savings, total formal savings, total informal savings, and total
savings (See
Dupas
et al. 2018
for details). We use data from the first follow-up survey and exclude other cash savings because only 2% of the households in the sample reported having it.
To maintain
comparability, for each outcome variable, we also keep
X_{i}
similar to those
used in
Dupas
et al. 2018
for our adjusted estimators.
15
15

15

The
description of these estimators is similar to that in Section
6
.
Except for savings in formal financial institutions, mobile money, and total
formal savings,
X_{i}
includes baseline value for the outcome of interest, baseline value of total income, and a dummy for missing observations. For savings in formal financial institutions,
mobile money, and total formal savings, since their baseline values are all
zero, we set
X_{i}
as the baseline value of total savings, baseline value of total income, and a dummy for missing observations.
Due to the low dimension of covariates used in the regression adjustments, we focus on the performance of the methods “NA”, “TSLS”, “L”, “NL”, and “F”.

Table
5
presents the LATE estimates and their standard errors
(in parentheses) estimated by these methods.
16
16

16

For each outcome variable, we filter out the observations with missing values of outcome variables or the strata with less than 10 observations. The total trimmed observations are less than 10% of the whole sample in
Dupas
et al. 2018
).
These results lead to four observations. First, consistent with the theoretical and simulation results, the standard errors for the LATE estimates with regression adjustments are
lower than those without adjustments. This observation holds for all the
outcome variables and all the regression adjustment methods. Over the eight
outcome variables, the standard errors estimated by regression adjustments are
on average around 8% lower than those without adjustment. In particular, when
the outcome variable is total informal savings, the standard errors obtained
via the further improvement adjustment – “F” method is about 18% lower
than those without adjustment. This means that regression adjustments,
with the similar covariates used in
Dupas
et al. 2018
, can achieve sizable
efficiency gains in estimating the LATEs.

\justify

Notes: The table reports the LATE estimates of opening bank accounts on saving
stocks. NA, TSLS, L, NL, and F stand for the no-adjustment, TSLS, optimal
linear, nonlinear (logistic), further efficiency improving,
respectively.
n
is the number of households. Standard errors are in parentheses.

Second, the standard errors for the regression-adjusted LATE estimates are
mostly lower than those obtained by the usual TSLS procedure. Especially, when the outcome variables are mobile money and total informal savings, the standard errors obtained via “F” method are about 7.1% and 5%, respectively, lower than those by TSLS. When the outcome variable is savings in friends/family, the standard error estimated by the optimal linear adjustment – “L” method is around 6.7% lower than that obtained by TSLS. This means that, compared with our regression-adjusted methods, TSLS is generally less efficient to estimate the LATEs under CAR.

Third, the standard errors for the LATE estimates with regression adjustments
are similar in terms of magnitude. This implies that all the regression
adjustments achieve similar efficiency gain in this case.

Finally, as in
Dupas
et al. 2018
, for the households who actively use bank
accounts, we find that reducing the cost of opening a bank account can
significantly increase their savings in formal institutions. We also observe
the evidence of crowd-out – mainly moving cash from saving at home to saving
in bank.

Appendix A
Covariate-Adaptive Treatment Assignment Rules

Example 4

(SRS)
.

Let
A_{k}
be a Bernoulli random variable, independent of
\{S_{i}\}_{i=1,\neq k}^{n}
and
\{A_{i}\}_{i=1}^{k-1}
, with success rate
\pi(s)
when
S_{k}=s
for
k=1,\ldots,n
. That is,

\mathbb{P}\left(A_{k}=1\big|\{S_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n},\{A_{i}\}_{i=1}^{k-1}\right)=\mathbb{P}(A_{k}=1|S_{k})=\pi(S_{k}).

Example 5

(WEI)
.

This design was first proposed by
Wei 1978
. Let

n_{k-1}(S_{k})=\sum_{i=1}^{k-1}1\{S_{i}=S_{k}\}
,
B_{k-1}(S_{k})=\sum_{i=1}^{k-1}\left(A_{i}-\frac{1}{2}\right)1\{S_{i}=S_{k}\}
, and

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(A_{k}=1\big|\{S_{i}\}_{i=1}^{k},\{A_{i}\}_{i=1}^{k-1}\right)=f\biggl(\frac{2B_{k-1}(S_{k})}{n_{k-1}(S_{k})}\biggr),

where
f(\cdot)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}[-1,1]\mapsto[0,1]
is a pre-specified non-increasing
function satisfying
f(-x)=1-f(x)
. Here,
\frac{B_{0}(S_{1})}{n_{0}(S_{1})}
and
B_{0}(S_{1})
are understood to be zero.

Example 6

(BCD)
.

The treatment status is determined sequentially for
1\leq k\leq n
as

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(A_{k}=1|\{S_{i}\}_{i=1}^{k},\{A_{i}\}_{i=1}^{k-1}\right)=\begin{cases}\frac{1}{2}&\text{if }B_{k-1}(S_{k})=0\\
\lambda&\text{if }B_{k-1}(S_{k})<0\\
1-\lambda&\text{if }B_{k-1}(S_{k})>0,\end{cases}

where
B_{k-1}(s)
is defined as above and
\frac{1}{2}<\lambda\leq 1
.

Example 7

(SBR)
.

For each stratum,
\lfloor\pi(s)n(s)\rfloor
units are
assigned to treatment and the rest are assigned to control.

Appendix B
The S Estimator in
Ansel
et al. 2018

Ansel
et al. 2018
propose a LATE estimator adjusted with extra covariates.
It takes the form

\displaystyle\hat{\tau}_{S}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\frac{\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\hat{p}(s)(\hat{\gamma}_{1s}^{Y}-\hat{\gamma}_{0s}^{Y}+(\hat{\nu}_{1s}^{Y}-\hat{\nu}_{0s}^{Y})^{\top}\bar{X}_{s})}{\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\hat{p}(s)(\hat{\gamma}_{1s}^{D}-\hat{\gamma}_{0s}^{D}+(\hat{\nu}_{1s}^{D}-\hat{\nu}_{0s}^{D})^{\top}\bar{X}_{s})},

where
\hat{p}(s)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=n(s)/n
,
\bar{X}_{s}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\frac{1}{n\hat{p}(s)}\sum_{i\in[n]}X_{i}1\{S_{i}=s\}
, and
(\hat{\gamma}_{as}^{Y},\hat{\gamma}_{as}^{D},\hat{\nu}_{as}^{Y},\hat{\nu}_{as}^{D})
for
a=0,1
are the estimated coefficients of the four sets of stratum-specific regressions using only the
s
stratum:

\displaystyle(1-A_{i})Y_{i}=(1-A_{i})(\gamma_{0s}^{Y}+X_{i}^{\top}\nu_{0s}^{Y}+e_{0i}^{Y}),\quad A_{i}Y_{i}=A_{i}(\gamma_{1s}^{Y}+X_{i}^{\top}\nu_{1s}^{Y}+e_{1i}^{Y})

\displaystyle(1-A_{i})D_{i}=(1-A_{i})(\gamma_{0s}^{D}+X_{i}^{\top}\nu_{0s}^{D}+e_{0i}^{D}),\quad A_{i}D_{i}=A_{i}(\gamma_{1s}^{D}+X_{i}^{\top}\nu_{1s}^{D}+e_{1i}^{D}).

Interpret
(\hat{\gamma}_{aS_{i}}^{Y},\hat{\gamma}_{aS_{i}}^{D},\hat{\nu}_{aS_{i}}^{Y},\hat{\nu}_{aS_{i}}^{D})
for
a=0,1
as the estimated coefficients of the four sets of stratum-specific regressions using only the
S_{i}
stratum.

Under Assumption
1
and Assumption
2
of our paper,
Ansel
et al. 2018
show that
\hat{\tau}_{S}
is a consistent estimator of
\tau
, asymptotically normal, and the most efficient among the estimators studied in their paper (
\pi(s)
can be heterogenous across strata). To define the explicit expression for the asymptotic variance of
\hat{\tau}_{S}
, denoted as
\sigma_{S}^{2}
, we need to introduce addition notation. For
s\in\mathcal{S}
, let
\tilde{X}_{is}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=X_{i}-\mathbb{E}(X_{i}|S_{i}=s)
,

\displaystyle\rho_{iS_{i}}(1)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\frac{Y_{i}(D_{i}(1))-D_{i}(1)\tau-X_{i}^{\top}\nu_{1S_{i}}^{YD}}{\pi(S_{i})}+X_{i}^{\top}(\nu_{1S_{i}}^{YD}-\nu_{0S_{i}}^{YD})

\displaystyle\rho_{iS_{i}}(0)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\frac{Y_{i}(D_{i}(0))-D_{i}(0)\tau-X_{i}^{\top}\nu_{0S_{i}}^{YD}}{1-\pi(S_{i})}-X_{i}^{\top}(\nu_{1S_{i}}^{YD}-\nu_{0S_{i}}^{YD})

\displaystyle\nu_{1s}^{YD}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\left[\mathbb{E}(\tilde{X}_{is}\tilde{X}_{is}^{\top}|S_{i}=s)\right]^{-1}\mathbb{E}\mathinner{\biggl(\tilde{X}_{is}\mathinner{\Bigl[Y_{i}\mathinner{\bigl(D_{i}(1)\bigr)}-D_{i}(1)\tau\Bigr]}|S_{i}=s\biggr)},

\displaystyle\nu_{0s}^{YD}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\left[\mathbb{E}(\tilde{X}_{is}\tilde{X}_{is}^{\top}|S_{i}=s)\right]^{-1}\mathbb{E}\mathinner{\biggl(\tilde{X}_{is}\mathinner{\Bigl[Y_{i}\mathinner{\bigl(D_{i}(0)\bigr)}-D_{i}(0)\tau\Bigr]}|S_{i}=s\biggr)}.

\displaystyle\sigma_{S1}^{2}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\mathbb{E}\mathinner{\biggl[\pi(S_{i})\mathinner{\Bigl\{\rho_{iS_{i}}(1)-\mathbb{E}[\rho_{iS_{i}}(1)|S_{i}]\Bigr\}}^{2}\biggr]}

\displaystyle\sigma_{S0}^{2}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\mathbb{E}\mathinner{\biggl[\mathinner{\bigl(1-\pi(S_{i})\bigr)}\mathinner{\Bigl\{\rho_{iS_{i}}(0)-\mathbb{E}[\rho_{iS_{i}}(0)|S_{i}]\Bigr\}}^{2}\biggr]}

\displaystyle\sigma_{S2}^{2}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\mathbb{E}\mathinner{\biggl[\mathinner{\Bigl(\mathbb{E}\mathinner{\bigl[Y_{i}(D_{i}(1))-Y_{i}(D_{i}(0))-\tau(D_{i}(1)-D_{i}(0))|S_{i}\bigr]}\Bigr)}^{2}\biggr]}.

In addition, define

\displaystyle\hat{\rho}_{iS_{i}}(1)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\frac{Y_{i}-D_{i}\hat{\tau}_{S}-X_{i}^{\top}\hat{\nu}_{1S_{i}}^{YD}}{\hat{\pi}(S_{i})}+X_{i}^{\top}(\hat{\nu}_{1S_{i}}^{YD}-\hat{\nu}_{0S_{i}}^{YD})

\displaystyle\hat{\rho}_{iS_{i}}(0)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\frac{Y_{i}-D_{i}\hat{\tau}_{S}-X_{i}^{\top}\hat{\nu}_{0S_{i}}^{YD}}{1-\hat{\pi}(S_{i})}-X_{i}^{\top}(\hat{\nu}_{1S_{i}}^{YD}-\hat{\nu}_{0S_{i}}^{YD})

\displaystyle\hat{\sigma}_{S1}^{2}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}A_{i}\mathinner{\biggl[\hat{\rho}_{iS_{i}}(1)-\frac{1}{n_{1}(S_{i})}\sum_{j\in I_{1}(S_{i})}\hat{\rho}_{jS_{j}}(1)\biggr]}^{2}

\displaystyle\hat{\sigma}_{S0}^{2}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}(1-A_{i})\mathinner{\biggl[\hat{\rho}_{iS_{i}}(0)-\frac{1}{n_{0}(S_{i})}\sum_{j\in I_{0}(S_{i})}\hat{\rho}_{jS_{j}}(0)\biggr]}^{2}

\displaystyle\hat{\sigma}_{S2}^{2}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}\mathinner{\biggl(\frac{1}{n_{1}(S_{i})}\sum_{j\in I_{1}(S_{i})}(Y_{j}-\hat{\tau}_{S}D_{j})-\frac{1}{n_{0}(S_{i})}\sum_{j\in I_{0}(S_{i})}(Y_{j}-\hat{\tau}_{S}D_{j})\biggr)}^{2}

\displaystyle\hat{\sigma}_{S}^{2}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\frac{\hat{\sigma}_{S1}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{S0}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{S2}^{2}}{\mathinner{\bigl(\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\hat{p}(s)(\hat{\gamma}_{1s}^{D}-\hat{\gamma}_{0s}^{D}+(\hat{\nu}_{1s}^{D}-\hat{\nu}_{0s}^{D})^{\top}\bar{X}_{s})\bigr)}^{2}}

where
\hat{\nu}_{aS_{i}}^{YD}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\hat{\nu}_{aS_{i}}^{Y}-\hat{\tau}_{S}\hat{\nu}_{aS_{i}}^{D}
for
a=0,1
.

Theorem B.1
.

Suppose Assumptions
1
and
2
hold. Then,

(i)

\displaystyle\sigma_{S}^{2}=\frac{\sigma_{S1}^{2}+\sigma_{S0}^{2}+\sigma_{S2}^{2}}{(\mathbb{E}[D_{i}(1)-D_{i}(0)])^{2}}.

(B.1)

(ii)

\hat{\sigma}_{S}^{2}\xrightarrow{p}\sigma_{S}^{2}.

It can be shown that
\sigma_{Sa}^{2}\geq\underline{\sigma}_{a}^{2}
for
a=0,1
and
\sigma_{S2}^{2}=\underline{\sigma}_{2}^{2}
, where the
inequalities are strict except special cases such as
\mathbb{E}(Y_{i}(D_{i}(a))-D_{i}(a)\tau|X_{i},S_{i}=s)
is linear in
X_{i}
, and
\underline{\sigma}_{a}^{2}
for
a=0,1,2
are defined in Theorem
4.1
. This implies
in general, the S estimator is not semiparametrically most efficient.

Theorem B.2
.

Suppose that Assumptions
1
and
2
hold. Moreover, suppose that

\pi(s)
is the same across
s\in\mathcal{S}
. Then
\hat{\tau}_{S}
is more
efficient than
\hat{\tau}_{TSLS}
in the sense that
\sigma_{S}^{2}\leq\sigma_{TSLS}^{2}
.

Theorem
B.2
could be deduced from Theorem
5.3
. Both
\hat{\tau}_{TSLS}
and
\hat{\tau}_{S}
use linear adjustments of
X_{i}
, but Theorem
B.2
states that
\hat{\tau}_{S}
is more efficient than
\hat{\tau}_{TSLS}
. In the discussion following Theorem
5.3
, we further show that
\hat{\tau}_{S}
achieves the minimum asymptotic variance among the class of estimators with linear adjustments. On the other hand, nonlinear adjustments may be more efficient than the optimal linear adjustment.

Appendix C
Implementation Details for Sieve and Lasso Regressions

Sieve regressions.

We provide more details on the sieve basis. Recall
\mathring{\Psi}_{i,n}\equiv(b_{1,n}(x),\cdots,b_{h_{n},n}(x))^{\top}
, where
\{b_{h,n}(\cdot)\}_{h\in[h_{n}]}
are
h_{n}
basis functions of a linear sieve space,
denoted as
\mathcal{B}
. Given that all the elements of vector
X
are
continuously distributed, the sieve space
\mathcal{B}
can be constructed as follows.

1.

For each element
X^{(l)}
of
X
,
l=1,\cdots,d_{x}
, where
d_{x}

denotes the dimension of vector
X
, let
\mathcal{B}_{l}
be the univariate
sieve space of dimension
J_{n}
. One example of
\mathcal{B}_{l}
is the
linear span of the
J_{n}
dimensional polynomials given by

\mathcal{B}_{l}=\biggl\{\sum_{k=0}^{J_{n}}\alpha_{k}x^{k},x\in\text{Supp}(X^{(l)}),\alpha_{k}\in\mathbb{R}\biggr\};

Another example is the linear span of
r
-order splines with
J_{n}
nodes
given by

\mathcal{B}_{l}=\biggl\{\sum_{k=0}^{r-1}\alpha_{k}x^{k}+\sum_{j=1}^{J_{n}}b_{j}[\max(x-t_{j},0)]^{r-1},x\in\text{Supp}(X^{(l)}),\alpha_{k},b_{j}\in\mathbb{R}\biggr\},

where the grid
-\infty=t_{0}\leq t_{1}\leq\cdots\leq t_{J_{n}}\leq t_{J_{n}+1}=\infty
partitions
\text{Supp}(X^{(l)})
into
J_{n}+1
subsets

I_{j}=[t_{j},t_{j+1})\cap\text{Supp}(X^{(l)})
,
j=1,\cdots,J_{n}-1
,

I_{0}=(t_{0},t_{1})\cap\text{Supp}(X^{(l)})
, and
I_{J_{n}}=(t_{J_{n}},t_{J_{n}+1})\cap\text{Supp}(X^{(l)})
.

2.

Let
\mathcal{B}
be the tensor product of
\{\mathcal{B}_{l}\}_{l=1}^{d_{x}}
, which is defined as a linear space spanned by the functions

\prod_{l=1}^{d_{x}}g_{l}
, where
g_{l}\in\mathcal{B}_{l}
. The dimension
of
\mathcal{B}
is then
K\equiv d_{x}J_{n}
if
\mathcal{B}_{l}
is
spanned by
J_{n}
dimensional polynomials.

We refer interested readers to
Hirano
et al. 2003
and
Chen 2007
for more details
about the implementation of sieve estimation. Given the sieve basis, we can
compute the
\{\hat{\mu}^{b}(a,s,X_{i})\}_{a=0,1,b=D,Y,s\in\mathcal{S}}
following (
5.16
).

Lasso regressions.

We follow the estimation procedure and the choice of tuning parameter proposed
by
Belloni et al. 2017
. We provide details below for completeness. Recall

\varrho_{n,a}(s)=c\sqrt{n_{a}(s)}F_{N}^{-1}(1-1/(p_{n}\log(n_{a}(s))))
.
We set
c=1.1
following
Belloni et al. 2017
. We then implement the following
algorithm to estimate
\hat{\theta}_{a,s}^{R}
and
\hat{\beta}_{a,s}^{R}
:

(i)

Let
\hat{\sigma}_{h}^{Y,(0)}=\frac{1}{n_{a}(s)}\sum_{i\in I_{a}(s)}(Y_{i}-\bar{Y}_{a,s})^{2}\mathring{\Psi}_{i,n,h}^{2}
and
\hat{\sigma}_{h}^{D,(0)}=\frac{1}{n_{a}(s)}\sum_{i\in I_{a}(s)}(D_{i}-\bar{D}_{a,s})^{2}\mathring{\Psi}_{i,n,h}^{2}
for
h\in[p_{n}]
, where
\bar{Y}_{a,s}=\frac{1}{n_{a}(s)}\sum_{i\in I_{a}(s)}Y_{i}
and
\bar{D}_{a,s}=\frac{1}{n_{a}(s)}\sum_{i\in I_{a}(s)}D_{i}
. Estimate

\displaystyle\hat{\theta}_{a,s}^{R,0}=

\displaystyle\argmin_{t}\frac{-1}{n_{a}(s)}\sum_{i\in I_{a}(s)}\biggl(Y_{i}-\mathring{\Psi}_{i,n}^{\top}t\biggr)^{2}+\frac{\varrho_{n,a}(s)}{n_{a}(s)}\sum_{h\in[p_{n}]}\hat{\sigma}_{h}^{Y,(0)}|t_{h}|,

\displaystyle\hat{\beta}_{a,s}^{R,0}=

\displaystyle\argmin_{b}\frac{-1}{n_{a}(s)}\sum_{i\in I_{a}(s)}\biggl[D_{i}\log(\lambda(\mathring{\Psi}_{i,n}^{\top}b))+(1-D_{i})\log(1-\lambda(\mathring{\Psi}_{i,n}^{\top}b))\biggr]

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{\varrho_{n,a}(s)}{n_{a}(s)}\sum_{h\in[p_{n}]}\hat{\sigma}_{h}^{D,(0)}|b_{h}|.

(ii)

For
k=1,\cdots,K
, obtain
\hat{\sigma}_{h}^{Y,(k)}=\sqrt{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}(\mathring{\Psi}_{i,n,h}\hat{\varepsilon}_{i}^{Y,(k)})^{2}}
, where
\hat{\varepsilon}_{i}^{Y,(k)}=Y_{i}-\mathring{\Psi}_{i,n}^{\top}\hat{\theta}_{a,s}^{R,k-1}
and
\hat{\sigma}_{h}^{D,(k)}=\sqrt{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}(\mathring{\Psi}_{i,n,h}\hat{\varepsilon}_{i}^{D,(k)})^{2}}
, where
\hat{\varepsilon}_{i}^{D,(k)}=D_{i}-\lambda(\mathring{\Psi}_{i,n}^{\top}\hat{\beta}_{a,s}^{R,k-1})
. Estimate

\displaystyle\hat{\theta}_{a,s}^{R,k}=

\displaystyle\argmin_{t}\frac{-1}{n_{a}(s)}\sum_{i\in I_{a}(s)}\mathinner{\Bigl(Y_{i}-\mathring{\Psi}_{i,n}^{\top}t\Bigr)}^{2}+\frac{\varrho_{n,a}(s)}{n_{a}(s)}\sum_{h\in[p_{n}]}\hat{\sigma}_{h}^{Y,(k-1)}|t_{h}|,

\displaystyle\hat{\beta}_{a,s}^{R,k}=

\displaystyle\argmin_{b}\frac{-1}{n_{a}(s)}\sum_{i\in I_{a}(s)}\mathinner{\Bigl[D_{i}\log(\lambda(\mathring{\Psi}_{i,n}^{\top}b))+(1-D_{i})\log(1-\lambda(\mathring{\Psi}_{i,n}^{\top}b))\Bigr]}

\displaystyle\qquad+

\displaystyle\frac{\varrho_{n,a}(s)}{n_{a}(s)}\sum_{h\in[p_{n}]}\hat{\sigma}_{h}^{D,(k-1)}|b_{h}|.

(iii)

Let
\hat{\theta}_{a,s}^{R}=\hat{\theta}_{a,s}^{R,K}
and
\hat{\beta}_{a,s}^{R}=\hat{\beta}_{a,s}^{R,K}
.

Appendix D
Regression Adjustment under Full Compliance

In this section, we briefly discuss the regression adjustment under full compliance. We aim to construct consistent and efficient estimators for the average treatment effect (ATE). Under full compliance, we have
D(a)=a
for
a=0,1
so that
D=A
. The estimator
\hat{\mu}^{D}(a,s,x)=a
is correctly specified. Then, our proposed estimator of ATE is

\displaystyle\hat{\tau}_{ATE}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}\left[\frac{A_{i}(Y_{i}-\hat{\mu}^{Y}(1,S_{i},X_{i}))}{\hat{\pi}(S_{i})}-\frac{(1-A_{i})(Y_{i}-\hat{\mu}^{Y}(0,S_{i},X_{i}))}{1-\hat{\pi}(S_{i})}+\hat{\mu}^{Y}(1,S_{i},X_{i})-\hat{\mu}^{Y}(0,S_{i},X_{i})\right],

(D.1)

where
\hat{\mu}^{Y}(a,s,x)
is an estimator of the working model
\overline{\mu}^{Y}(a,s,x)
.

The optimal linear adjustment is
\hat{\mu}^{Y}(a,s,X_{i})=\Psi_{i,s}^{\top}\hat{\theta}_{a,s}^{L}
, where

\displaystyle\dot{\Psi}_{i,a,s}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\Psi_{i,s}-\frac{1}{n_{a}(s)}\sum_{i\in I_{a}(s)}\Psi_{i,s}

\displaystyle\hat{\theta}_{a,s}^{L}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\mathinner{\biggl(\frac{1}{n_{a}(s)}\sum_{i\in I_{a}(s)}\dot{\Psi}_{i,a,s}\dot{\Psi}_{i,a,s}^{\top}\biggr)}^{-1}\mathinner{\biggl(\frac{1}{n_{a}(s)}\sum_{i\in I_{a}(s)}\dot{\Psi}_{i,a,s}Y_{i}\biggr)}.

We can show that such an adjustment achieves the minimal variance of the ATE estimator that is adjusted by linear functions of
\Psi_{i,s}
.

Let
\mathring{\Psi}_{i,n}
contains sieve bases of
X_{i}
. Then, the nonparametric adjustment can be written as
\hat{\mu}^{Y}(a,s,X_{i})=\mathring{\Psi}_{i,n}^{\top}\hat{\theta}_{a,s}^{NP}
, where

\displaystyle\hat{\theta}_{a,s}^{NP}=\mathinner{\biggl(\frac{1}{n_{a}(s)}\sum_{i\in I_{a}(s)}\mathring{\Psi}_{i,n}\mathring{\Psi}_{i,n}^{\top}\biggr)}^{-1}\mathinner{\biggl(\frac{1}{n_{a}(s)}\sum_{i\in I_{a}(s)}\mathring{\Psi}_{i,n}Y_{i}\biggr)}.

Last, suppose
\mathring{\Psi}_{i,n}
contains high-dimensional regressors of
X_{i}
. Then, the regularized adjustment can be written as
\hat{\mu}^{Y}(a,s,X_{i})=\mathring{\Psi}_{i,n}^{\top}\hat{\theta}_{a,s}^{R}
,
where

\displaystyle\hat{\theta}_{a,s}^{R}=

\displaystyle\argmin_{t}\frac{-1}{n_{a}(s)}\sum_{i\in I_{a}(s)}\mathinner{\bigl(Y_{i}-\mathring{\Psi}_{i,n}^{\top}t\bigr)}^{2}+\frac{\varrho_{n,a}(s)}{n_{a}(s)}||\hat{\Omega}^{Y}t||_{1},

\{\varrho_{n,a}(s)\}_{a=0,1,s\in\mathcal{S}}
are tuning parameters,
\hat{\Omega}^{Y}=\text{diag}(\hat{\omega}_{1}^{Y},\cdots,\hat{\omega}_{p_{n}}^{Y})
is a diagonal matrix of data-dependent penalty loadings as defined in Section

C
. Under similar conditions as in Assumptions
8
and
9
, we can show that the ATE estimator with both the nonparametric and regularized adjustments achieves the semiparametric efficiency bound.

Appendix E
Proof of Theorem
2.1

We define
\hat{\sigma}_{TSLS,naive}^{2}
as

\displaystyle\hat{\sigma}_{TSLS,naive}^{2}=e_{1}^{\top}[S_{\bar{Z},\bar{X}}^{\top}S_{\bar{Z},\bar{Z}}^{-1}S_{\bar{Z},\bar{X}}]^{-1}\left[S_{\bar{Z},\bar{X}}^{\top}S_{\bar{Z},\bar{Z}}^{-1}\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\bar{Z}_{i}\bar{Z}_{i}^{\top}\hat{\varepsilon}_{i}^{2})\right)S_{\bar{Z},\bar{Z}}^{-1}S_{\bar{Z},\bar{X}}\right][S_{\bar{Z},\bar{X}}^{\top}S_{\bar{Z},\bar{Z}}^{-1}S_{\bar{Z},\bar{X}}]^{-1}e_{1},

where
\bar{X}_{i}=(D_{i},\{1\{S_{i}=s\}\}_{s\in\mathcal{S}},X_{i}^{\top})^{\top}

\bar{Z}_{i}=(A_{i},\{1\{S_{i}=s\}\}_{s\in\mathcal{S}},X_{i}^{\top})^{\top}
,
S_{\bar{Z},\bar{Z}}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}\bar{Z}_{i}\bar{Z}_{i}^{\top}
,

S_{\bar{Z},\bar{X}}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}\bar{Z}_{i}\bar{X}_{i}^{\top}
,
e_{1}
is a vector with its first element being one and the rest being zero,
\hat{\varepsilon}_{i}=Y_{i}-\hat{\tau}_{TSLS}D_{i}-\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\hat{\alpha}_{s,TSLS}1\{S_{i}=s\}-X_{i}^{\top}\hat{\delta}_{TSLS}
, and
(\hat{\tau}_{TSLS},\hat{\alpha}_{s,TSLS},\hat{\delta}_{TSLS})
are the usual TSLS estimators.

Next, we define
\sigma_{TSLS}^{2}
and
\sigma_{TSLS,naive}^{2}
. Let
\mathbb{X}_{i}=(X_{i}^{\top},\{1\{S_{i}=s\}\}_{s\in\mathcal{S}})^{\top}
,

\displaystyle\sigma_{TSLS}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{\sigma_{TSLS,0}^{2}+\sigma_{TSLS,1}^{2}+\sigma_{TSLS,2}^{2}+\sigma_{TSLS,3}^{2}}{(\mathbb{E}(D_{i}(1)-D_{i}(0)))^{2}},

\displaystyle\sigma_{TSLS,1}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbb{E}\mathinner{\bigl[Y_{i}(D_{i}(1))-D_{i}(1)\tau-\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\lambda^{*}-\mathbb{E}[Y_{i}(D_{i}(1))-D_{i}(1)\tau-\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\lambda^{*}|S_{i}]\bigr]}^{2}}{\pi}

\displaystyle\sigma_{TSLS,0}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbb{E}\mathinner{\bigl[Y_{i}(D_{i}(0))-D_{i}(0)\tau-\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\lambda^{*}-\mathbb{E}[Y_{i}(D_{i}(0))-D_{i}(0)\tau-\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\lambda^{*}|S_{i}]\bigr]}^{2}}{1-\pi},

\displaystyle\sigma_{TSLS,2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\mathinner{\bigl[Y(D(1))-Y(D(0))-(D(1)-D(0))\tau|S_{i}\bigr]}\right]^{2},

\displaystyle\sigma_{TSLS,3}^{2}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left\{\gamma(S_{i})\mathinner{\biggl(\mathbb{E}\mathinner{\biggl[\frac{Y_{i}(D_{i}(1))-D_{i}(1)\tau-\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\lambda^{*}}{\pi}+\frac{Y_{i}(D_{i}(0))-D_{i}(0)\tau-\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\lambda^{*}}{1-\pi}\bigg|S_{i}\biggr]}\biggr)}^{2}\right\},

\displaystyle\lambda^{*}

\displaystyle=\left(\mathbb{E}\mathbb{X}_{i}\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\right)^{-1}\mathbb{E}\mathbb{X}_{i}\left[\pi(Y_{i}(D_{i}(1))-D_{i}(1)\tau)+(1-\pi)(Y_{i}(D_{i}(0))-D_{i}(0)\tau)\right].

Furthermore, define

\displaystyle\sigma_{TSLS,naive}^{2}=\frac{\sigma_{TSLS,0}^{2}+\sigma_{TSLS,1}^{2}+\sigma_{TSLS,2}^{2}+\tilde{\sigma}_{TSLS,3}^{2}}{(\mathbb{E}(D_{i}(1)-D_{i}(0)))^{2}},\quad\text{where}

\displaystyle\tilde{\sigma}_{TSLS,3}^{2}=\mathbb{E}\left\{\pi(1-\pi)\mathinner{\biggl(\mathbb{E}\mathinner{\biggl[\frac{Y_{i}(D_{i}(1))-D_{i}(1)\tau-\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\lambda^{*}}{\pi}+\frac{Y_{i}(D_{i}(0))-D_{i}(0)\tau-\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\lambda^{*}}{1-\pi}\bigg|S_{i}\biggr]}\biggr)}^{2}\right\}.

By definition,
\sigma_{TSLS}^{2}\leq\sigma_{TSLS,naive}^{2}
. The inequality is strict if
\gamma(s)<\pi(1-\pi)
.

Define
\tilde{A}_{i}
as the residual from the regression of
A_{i}
on

X_{i}
and
\{1\{S_{i}=s\}\}_{s\in\mathcal{S}}
. Then, we have

\displaystyle\hat{\tau}_{TSLS}=\frac{\sum_{i\in[n]}\tilde{A}_{i}Y_{i}}{\sum_{i\in[n]}\tilde{A}_{i}D_{i}}=\frac{\sum_{i\in[n]}\mathinner{\bigl(A_{i}-\pi(S_{i})\bigr)}Y_{i}+\sum_{i\in[n]}R_{i}Y_{i}}{\sum_{i\in[n]}\mathinner{\bigl(A_{i}-\pi(S_{i})\bigr)}D_{i}+\sum_{i\in[n]}R_{i}D_{i}},

where
R_{i}=\tilde{A_{i}}-(A_{i}-\pi(S_{i}))
. We first suppose that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}R_{i}Y_{i}=o_{p}(1)\quad\text{and}\quad\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}R_{i}D_{i}=o_{p}(1).

(E.1)

In addition, we note that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}(A_{i}-\pi(S_{i}))Y_{i}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}A_{i}(1-\pi(S_{i}))Y_{i}(D_{i}(1))-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}(1-A_{i})\pi(S_{i})Y_{i}(D_{i}(0)).

For the first term on the RHS of the above display, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}A_{i}(1-\pi(S_{i}))Y_{i}(D_{i}(1))

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}A_{i}(1-\pi(S_{i}))(Y_{i}(D_{i}(1))-\mathbb{E}(Y_{i}(D_{i}(1))|S_{i}))+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}A_{i}(1-\pi(S_{i}))\mathbb{E}(Y_{i}(D_{i}(1))|S_{i})

\displaystyle=o_{p}(1)+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}\pi(S_{i})(1-\pi(S_{i}))\mathbb{E}(Y_{i}(D_{i}(1))|S_{i})+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}B_{n}(s)(1-\pi(s))\mathbb{E}(Y_{i}(D_{i}(1))|S_{i}=s)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\pi(S_{i})(1-\pi(S_{i}))\mathbb{E}(Y_{i}(D_{i}(1))|S_{i})+o_{p}(1),

(E.2)

where the second equality is by conditional Chebyshev’s inequality using the
facts that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\mathinner{\biggl[\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}A_{i}(1-\pi(S_{i}))(Y_{i}(D_{i}(1))-\mathbb{E}(Y_{i}(D_{i}(1))|S_{i}))\bigg|\{A_{i},S_{i}\}_{i\in[n]}\biggr]}=0

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\mathinner{\biggl[\mathinner{\biggl(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}A_{i}(1-\pi(S_{i}))(Y_{i}(D_{i}(1))-\mathbb{E}(Y_{i}(D_{i}(1))|S_{i}))\biggr)}^{2}\bigg|\{A_{i},S_{i}\}_{i\in[n]}\biggr]}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\frac{n_{1}(s)(1-\pi(s))^{2}\mathbb{E}(Y^{2}(D(1))|S_{i}=s)}{n^{2}}=o_{p}(1),

and the third equality is by Assumption
1
(iv) and the
usual LLN. For the same reason, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}(1-A_{i})\pi(S_{i})Y_{i}(D_{i}(0))\overset{p}{\longrightarrow}\mathbb{E}\pi(S_{i})(1-\pi(S_{i}))\mathbb{E}(Y_{i}(D_{i}(0))|S_{i}),

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}A_{i}(1-\pi(S_{i}))D_{i}(1)\overset{p}{\longrightarrow}\mathbb{E}\pi(S_{i})(1-\pi(S_{i}))\mathbb{E}(D_{i}(1)|S_{i}),

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}(1-A_{i})\pi(S_{i})D_{i}(0)\overset{p}{\longrightarrow}\mathbb{E}\pi(S_{i})(1-\pi(S_{i}))\mathbb{E}D_{i}(0)|S_{i}),

and

\displaystyle\hat{\tau}_{TSLS}\overset{p}{\longrightarrow}\frac{\mathbb{E}\pi(S_{i})(1-\pi(S_{i}))(\mathbb{E}(Y_{i}(D_{i}(1))|S_{i})-\mathbb{E}(Y_{i}(D_{i}(0))|S_{i}))}{\mathbb{E}\pi(S_{i})(1-\pi(S_{i}))(\mathbb{E}(D_{i}(1)|S_{i})-\mathbb{E}(D_{i}(0)|S_{i}))}.

Therefore, it is only left to show (
E.1
). Let
\mathbb{X}_{i}=(X_{i}^{\top},\{1\{S_{i}=s\}\}_{s\in\mathcal{S}})^{\top}
,
\hat{\theta}
be
the OLS coefficient of regressing
A_{i}
on
\mathbb{X}_{i}
, and
\theta=(0_{d_{x}}^{\top},\{\pi(s)\}_{s\in\mathcal{S}})^{\top}
, where
d_{x}
is
the dimension of
X_{i}
. Then, we have
R_{i}=-\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}(\hat{\theta}-\theta)
. In order to show (
E.1
), it suffices to show

\hat{\theta}\overset{p}{\longrightarrow}\theta
, or equivalently,
\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}\mathbb{X}_{i}(A_{i}-\pi(S_{i}))\overset{p}{\longrightarrow}0
. We note that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}\mathbb{X}_{i}(A_{i}-\pi(S_{i}))=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}(\mathbb{X}_{i}-\mathbb{E}(\mathbb{X}_{i}|S_{i}))(A_{i}-\pi(S_{i}))+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}\mathbb{E}(\mathbb{X}_{i}|S_{i})(A_{i}-\pi(S_{i}))

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}(\mathbb{X}_{i}-\mathbb{E}(\mathbb{X}_{i}|S_{i}))A_{i}(1-\pi(S_{i}))-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}(\mathbb{X}_{i}-\mathbb{E}(\mathbb{X}_{i}|S_{i}))(1-A_{i})\pi(S_{i})+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\mathbb{E}(\mathbb{X}_{i}|S_{i}=s)B_{n}(s)

\displaystyle=o_{p}(1),

(E.3)

where the last equality holds following the similar argument in (
E.2
).
This concludes the proof of the first statement.

For the second statement, let
\mathbb{X}_{i}=(X_{i}^{\top},\{1\{S_{i}=s\}\}_{s\in\mathcal{S}})^{\top}
,

\displaystyle\hat{\theta}=\mathinner{\biggl(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}\mathbb{X}_{i}\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\biggr)}^{-1}\mathinner{\biggl(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}\mathbb{X}_{i}A_{i}\biggr)},

\widetilde{A}_{i}=A_{i}-\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\hat{\theta}
, and
\theta=(0_{d_{x}}^{\top},\pi,\cdots,\pi)^{\top}
. Then, we have

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}(\hat{\tau}_{TSLS}-\tau)=\frac{\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i\in[n]}\widetilde{A}_{i}(Y_{i}-D_{i}\tau)}{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}\widetilde{A}_{i}D_{i}}.

By the same argument in the proof of the first statement of Theorem

2.1
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}\widetilde{A}_{i}D_{i}\overset{p}{\longrightarrow}\pi(1-\pi)\mathbb{E}(D(1)-D(0)).

Next, we turn to the numerator. We have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i\in[n]}\widetilde{A}_{i}(Y_{i}-D_{i}\tau)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i\in[n]}(A_{i}-\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\theta-\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}(\hat{\theta}-\theta))(Y_{i}-D_{i}\tau)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i\in[n]}(A_{i}-\pi)(Y_{i}-D_{i}\tau)-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}(Y_{i}-D_{i}\tau)\mathinner{\biggl(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}\mathbb{X}_{i}\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\biggr)}^{-1}\mathinner{\biggl(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i\in[n]}\mathbb{X}_{i}(A_{i}-\pi)\biggr)}.

where the second equality uses the facts that
\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\theta=\pi
and

\displaystyle\hat{\theta}-\theta

\displaystyle=\mathinner{\biggl(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}\mathbb{X}_{i}\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\biggr)}^{-1}\mathinner{\biggl(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}\mathbb{X}_{i}(A_{i}-\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\theta)\biggr)}=\mathinner{\biggl(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}\mathbb{X}_{i}\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\biggr)}^{-1}\mathinner{\biggl(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}\mathbb{X}_{i}(A_{i}-\pi)\biggr)}.

We first consider the joint convergence of
\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i\in[n]}(A_{i}-\pi)(Y_{i}-D_{i}\tau)
and
\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i\in[n]}\mathbb{X}_{i}(A_{i}-\pi)
. Let
\lambda_{1}
be a scalar and

\lambda_{2}\in\Re^{d_{x}}
. Then, it suffices to consider the weak
convergence of
\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i\in[n]}(A_{i}-\pi)(\lambda_{1}(Y_{i}-D_{i}\tau)+\lambda_{2}^{\top}\mathbb{X}_{i})
. Let
\varpi_{i}=\lambda_{1}(Y_{i}-D_{i}\tau)+\lambda_{2}^{\top}\mathbb{X}_{i}
and

\varpi_{i}(a)=\lambda_{1}(Y_{i}(D_{i}(a))-D_{i}(a)\tau)+\lambda_{2}^{\top}\mathbb{X}_{i}
. Note that
\varpi_{i}=A_{i}\varpi_{i}(1)+(1-A_{i})\varpi_{i}(0)
. We have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i\in[n]}(A_{i}-\pi)\varpi_{i}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i\in[n]}\left[A_{i}(1-\pi)\varpi_{i}(1)-(1-A_{i})\pi\varpi_{i}(0)\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i\in[n]}\left[A_{i}(1-\pi)(\varpi_{i}(1)-\mathbb{E}(\varpi_{i}(1)|S_{i}))-(1-A_{i})\pi(\varpi_{i}(0)-\mathbb{E}(\varpi_{i}(0)|S_{i}))\right]

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i\in[n]}\left[A_{i}(1-\pi)\mathbb{E}(\varpi_{i}(1)|S_{i})-(1-A_{i})\pi\mathbb{E}(\varpi_{i}(0)|S_{i})\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i\in[n]}\left[A_{i}(1-\pi)(\varpi_{i}(1)-\mathbb{E}(\varpi_{i}(1)|S_{i}))-(1-A_{i})\pi(\varpi_{i}(0)-\mathbb{E}(\varpi_{i}(0)|S_{i}))\right]

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}B_{n}(s)\left[(1-\pi)\mathbb{E}(\varpi_{i}(1)|S_{i}=s)+\pi\mathbb{E}(\varpi_{i}(0)|S_{i}=s)\right]+\frac{\pi(1-\pi)}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i\in[n]}\mathbb{E}(\varpi_{i}(1)-\varpi_{i}(0)|S_{i})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i\in[n]}\left[A_{i}(1-\pi)(\varpi_{i}(1)-\mathbb{E}(\varpi_{i}(1)|S_{i}))-(1-A_{i})\pi(\varpi_{i}(0)-\mathbb{E}(\varpi_{i}(0)|S_{i}))\right]

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}B_{n}(s)\left[(1-\pi)\mathbb{E}(\varpi_{i}(1)|S_{i}=s)+\pi\mathbb{E}(\varpi_{i}(0)|S_{i}=s)\right]

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{\pi(1-\pi)}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i\in[n]}\left(\mathbb{E}(\varpi_{i}(1)-\varpi_{i}(0)|S_{i})-\mathbb{E}(\varpi_{i}(1)-\varpi_{i}(0))\right)

\displaystyle\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{N}(0,\Sigma^{2}),

(E.4)

where

\displaystyle\Sigma^{2}

\displaystyle=(1-\pi)\pi\left[(1-\pi)\mathbb{E}\mathinner{\bigl[\varpi_{i}(1)-\mathbb{E}(\varpi_{i}(1)|S_{i})\bigr]}^{2}+\pi\mathbb{E}\mathinner{\bigl[\varpi_{i}(0)-\mathbb{E}(\varpi_{i}(0)|S_{i})\bigr]}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathbb{E}\mathinner{\biggl[\gamma(S_{i})\mathinner{\Bigl(\mathbb{E}\mathinner{\bigl[(1-\pi)\varpi_{i}(1)+\pi\varpi_{i}(0)|S_{i}\bigr]}\Bigr)}^{2}\biggr]}+\pi^{2}(1-\pi)^{2}\mathbb{E}\mathinner{\Bigl(\mathbb{E}\mathinner{\bigl[\varpi_{i}(1)-\varpi_{i}(0)|S_{i}\bigr]}\Bigr)}^{2},

the last convergence in distribution is by a similar argument in the proof of

Bugni
et al. 2018
and the fact that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(\varpi_{i}(1)-\varpi_{i}(0))=\lambda_{1}\mathbb{E}(Y_{i}(D_{i}(1))-Y_{i}(D_{i}(1))-(D_{i}(1)-D_{i}(0))\tau)=0.

Thus (
E.4
) implies both
\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i\in[n]}(A_{i}-\pi)(Y_{i}-D_{i}\tau)
and
\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i\in[n]}\mathbb{X}_{i}(A_{i}-\pi)
are
O_{p}(1)
. In addition, let
\hat{\lambda}=\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}\mathbb{X}_{i}\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\right)^{-1}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}\mathbb{X}_{i}(Y_{i}-D_{i}\tau)
. We can
show

\displaystyle\hat{\lambda}\overset{p}{\longrightarrow}\lambda^{*}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\left(\mathbb{E}\mathbb{X}_{i}\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\right)^{-1}\mathbb{E}\mathbb{X}_{i}\left[\pi(Y_{i}(D_{i}(1))-D_{i}(1)\tau)+(1-\pi)(Y_{i}(D_{i}(0))-D_{i}(0)\tau)\right].

Therefore, by letting
\lambda_{1}=1
and
\lambda_{2}=\lambda^{*}
, we
have

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}(\hat{\tau}_{TSLS}-\tau)\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma_{TSLS}^{2}),

where

\displaystyle\sigma_{TSLS}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{\sigma_{TSLS,0}^{2}+\sigma_{TSLS,1}^{2}+\sigma_{TSLS,2}^{2}+\sigma_{TSLS,3}^{2}}{(\mathbb{E}(D_{i}(1)-D_{i}(0)))^{2}},

\displaystyle\sigma_{TSLS,0}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbb{E}\mathinner{\bigl[Y_{i}(D_{i}(0))-D_{i}(0)\tau-\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\lambda^{*}-\mathbb{E}[Y_{i}(D_{i}(0))-D_{i}(0)\tau-\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\lambda^{*}|S_{i}]\bigr]}^{2}}{1-\pi},

\displaystyle\sigma_{TSLS,1}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbb{E}\mathinner{\bigl[Y_{i}(D_{i}(1))-D_{i}(1)\tau-\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\lambda^{*}-\mathbb{E}[Y_{i}(D_{i}(1))-D_{i}(1)\tau-\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\lambda^{*}|S_{i}]\bigr]}^{2}}{\pi},

\displaystyle\sigma_{TSLS,2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\mathinner{\bigl[Y(D(1))-Y(D(0))-(D(1)-D(0))\tau|S_{i}\bigr]}\right]^{2},

\displaystyle\sigma_{TSLS,3}^{2}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left\{\gamma(S_{i})\mathinner{\biggl(\mathbb{E}\mathinner{\biggl[\frac{Y_{i}(D_{i}(1))-D_{i}(1)\tau-\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\lambda^{*}}{\pi}+\frac{Y_{i}(D_{i}(0))-D_{i}(0)\tau-\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\lambda^{*}}{1-\pi}\bigg|S_{i}\biggr]}\biggr)}^{2}\right\}.

To see the second result, we note that
\bar{X}_{i}=(D_{i},\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top})^{\top}
and
\bar{Z}_{i}=(A_{i},\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top})^{\top}
. Denote
\breve{Z}_{i}=(\tilde{A}_{i},\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top})^{\top}
. Then, we have

\displaystyle e_{1}^{\top}[S_{\bar{X},\bar{Z}}S_{\bar{Z},\bar{Z}}^{-1}S_{\bar{Z},\bar{X}}]^{-1}

\displaystyle=[S_{\bar{X},\breve{Z}}S_{\breve{Z},\breve{Z}}^{-1}S_{\breve{Z},\bar{X}}]^{-1}

\displaystyle=e_{1}^{\top}\biggl\{\begin{pmatrix}\sum_{i\in[n]}D_{i}\tilde{A}_{i}/n&\sum_{i\in[n]}D_{i}\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}/n\\
0&\sum_{i\in[n]}\mathbb{X}_{i}\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}/n\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\sum_{i\in[n]}\tilde{A}_{i}^{2}/n&0\\
0&\sum_{i\in[n]}\mathbb{X}_{i}\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}/n\end{pmatrix}^{-1}

\displaystyle\times\begin{pmatrix}\sum_{i\in[n]}D_{i}\tilde{A}_{i}/n&0\\
\sum_{i\in[n]}D_{i}\mathbb{X}_{i}/n&\sum_{i\in[n]}\mathbb{X}_{i}\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}/n\end{pmatrix}\biggr\}^{-1}

\displaystyle=e_{1}^{\top}\begin{pmatrix}\frac{(\sum_{i\in[n]}D_{i}\tilde{A}_{i}/n)^{2}}{(\sum_{i\in[n]}\tilde{A}_{i}^{2}/n)}+\sum_{i\in[n]}D_{i}\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}/n\left[\sum_{i\in[n]}\mathbb{X}_{i}\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}/n\right]^{-1}\sum_{i\in[n]}D_{i}\mathbb{X}_{i}/n&\sum_{i\in[n]}D_{i}\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}/n\\
\sum_{i\in[n]}D_{i}\mathbb{X}_{i}/n&\sum_{i\in[n]}\mathbb{X}_{i}\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}/n\end{pmatrix}^{-1}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\longrightarrow}}[\pi(1-\pi)]^{-1}(\mathbb{E}(D(1)-D(0)))^{-2}\begin{pmatrix}1&-\gamma_{D}^{\top}\end{pmatrix}.

and

\displaystyle S_{\bar{X},\bar{Z}}S_{\bar{Z},\bar{Z}}\bar{Z}_{i}

\displaystyle=S_{\bar{X},\breve{Z}}S_{\breve{Z},\breve{Z}}\breve{Z}_{i}

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}\sum_{i\in[n]}D_{i}\tilde{A}_{i}/n&\sum_{i\in[n]}D_{i}\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}/n\\
0&\sum_{i\in[n]}\mathbb{X}_{i}\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}/n\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\sum_{i\in[n]}\tilde{A}_{i}^{2}/n&0\\
0&\sum_{i\in[n]}\mathbb{X}_{i}\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}/n\end{pmatrix}^{-1}\begin{pmatrix}\tilde{A}_{i}\\
\mathbb{X}_{i}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}(\sum_{i\in[n]}D_{i}\tilde{A}_{i}/n)(\sum_{i\in[n]}\tilde{A}_{i}^{2}/n)^{-1}&(\sum_{i\in[n]}D_{i}\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}/n)(\sum_{i\in[n]}\mathbb{X}_{i}\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}/n)^{-1}\\
0&I\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\tilde{A}_{i}\\
\mathbb{X}_{i}\end{pmatrix},

where
\gamma_{D}=(\mathbb{E}\mathbb{X}_{i}\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top})^{-1}\mathbb{E}(\mathbb{X}_{i}(\pi D_{i}(1)+(1-\pi)D_{i}(0)))
. Further note that

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}(\sum_{i\in[n]}D_{i}\tilde{A}_{i}/n)(\sum_{i\in[n]}\tilde{A}_{i}^{2}/n)^{-1}&(\sum_{i\in[n]}D_{i}\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}/n)(\sum_{i\in[n]}\mathbb{X}_{i}\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}/n)^{-1}\\
0&I\end{pmatrix}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\longrightarrow}}\begin{pmatrix}\mathbb{E}(D(1)-D(0))&\gamma_{D}^{\top}\\
0&I\end{pmatrix}

and

\displaystyle\hat{\lambda}_{TSLS}\equiv\begin{pmatrix}\hat{\alpha}_{1,TSLS}\\
\vdots\\
\hat{\alpha}_{S,TSLS}\\
\hat{\theta}_{TSLS}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle=(\sum_{i\in[n]}\mathbb{X}_{i}\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}/n)^{-1}(\sum_{i\in[n]}\mathbb{X}_{i}(Y_{i}-D_{i}\hat{\tau}_{TSLS})/n)

\displaystyle=\hat{\lambda}+(\sum_{i\in[n]}\mathbb{X}_{i}\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}/n)^{-1}(\sum_{i\in[n]}\mathbb{X}_{i}D_{i}/n)(\tau-\hat{\tau}_{TSLS})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\longrightarrow}}\lambda^{*}.

Then, we have

\displaystyle\hat{e}_{i}=e_{i}-D_{i}(\hat{\tau}_{TSLS}-\tau)-\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}(\hat{\lambda}_{TSLS}-\lambda^{*}),

where
e_{i}=Y_{i}-D_{i}\tau-\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\lambda^{*}
. In addition, as shown above, we have
\tilde{A}_{i}=A_{i}-\pi-\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}(\hat{\theta}-\theta)
and
\hat{\theta}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\longrightarrow}}\theta
. This implies,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}\hat{e}_{i}^{2}\begin{pmatrix}\tilde{A}_{i}^{2}&\tilde{A}_{i}\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\\
\tilde{A}_{i}\mathbb{X}_{i}&\mathbb{X}_{i}\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}e_{i}^{2}\begin{pmatrix}(A_{i}-\pi)^{2}&(A_{i}-\pi)\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\\
(A_{i}-\pi)\mathbb{X}_{i}&\mathbb{X}_{i}\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\end{pmatrix}+o_{P}(1)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}\left[A_{i}(Y_{i}(D_{i}(1))-D_{i}(1)\tau-\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\lambda^{*})^{2}+(1-A_{i})(Y_{i}(D_{i}(0))-D_{i}(0)\tau-\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\lambda^{*})^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\times\begin{pmatrix}(A_{i}-\pi)^{2}&(A_{i}-\pi)\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\\
(A_{i}-\pi)\mathbb{X}_{i}&\mathbb{X}_{i}\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\end{pmatrix}+o_{P}(1)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}A_{i}\begin{pmatrix}(1-\pi)^{2}(Y_{i}(D_{i}(1))-D_{i}(1)\tau-\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\lambda^{*})^{2}&(1-\pi)(Y_{i}(D_{i}(1))-D_{i}(1)\tau-\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\lambda^{*})^{2}\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\\
(1-\pi)(Y_{i}(D_{i}(1))-D_{i}(1)\tau-\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\lambda^{*})^{2}\mathbb{X}_{i}&(Y_{i}(D_{i}(1))-D_{i}(1)\tau-\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\lambda^{*})^{2}\mathbb{X}_{i}\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}(1-A_{i})\begin{pmatrix}\pi^{2}(Y_{i}(D_{i}(0))-D_{i}(0)\tau-\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\lambda^{*})^{2}&-\pi(Y_{i}(D_{i}(0))-D_{i}(0)\tau-\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\lambda^{*})^{2}\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\\
-\pi(Y_{i}(D_{i}(0))-D_{i}(0)\tau-\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\lambda^{*})^{2}\mathbb{X}_{i}&(Y_{i}(D_{i}(0))-D_{i}(0)\tau-\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\lambda^{*})^{2}\mathbb{X}_{i}\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\end{pmatrix}+o_{P}(1).

For the first term on the RHS of the above display, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}A_{i}\begin{pmatrix}(1-\pi)^{2}(Y_{i}(D_{i}(1))-D_{i}(1)\tau-\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\lambda^{*})^{2}&(1-\pi)(Y_{i}(D_{i}(1))-D_{i}(1)\tau-\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\lambda^{*})^{2}\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\\
(1-\pi)(Y_{i}(D_{i}(1))-D_{i}(1)\tau-\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\lambda^{*})^{2}\mathbb{X}_{i}&(Y_{i}(D_{i}(1))-D_{i}(1)\tau-\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\lambda^{*})^{2}\mathbb{X}_{i}\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}A_{i}\biggl\{\begin{pmatrix}(1-\pi)^{2}(Y_{i}(D_{i}(1))-D_{i}(1)\tau-\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\lambda^{*})^{2}&(1-\pi)(Y_{i}(D_{i}(1))-D_{i}(1)\tau-\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\lambda^{*})^{2}\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\\
(1-\pi)(Y_{i}(D_{i}(1))-D_{i}(1)\tau-\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\lambda^{*})^{2}\mathbb{X}_{i}&(Y_{i}(D_{i}(1))-D_{i}(1)\tau-\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\lambda^{*})^{2}\mathbb{X}_{i}\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle-\mathbb{E}\left[\begin{pmatrix}(1-\pi)^{2}(Y_{i}(D_{i}(1))-D_{i}(1)\tau-\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\lambda^{*})^{2}&(1-\pi)(Y_{i}(D_{i}(1))-D_{i}(1)\tau-\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\lambda^{*})^{2}\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\\
(1-\pi)(Y_{i}(D_{i}(1))-D_{i}(1)\tau-\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\lambda^{*})^{2}\mathbb{X}_{i}&(Y_{i}(D_{i}(1))-D_{i}(1)\tau-\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\lambda^{*})^{2}\mathbb{X}_{i}\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\end{pmatrix}\bigg|S_{i}\right]\biggr\}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}(A_{i}-\pi)\mathbb{E}\left[\begin{pmatrix}(1-\pi)^{2}(Y_{i}(D_{i}(1))-D_{i}(1)\tau-\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\lambda^{*})^{2}&(1-\pi)(Y_{i}(D_{i}(1))-D_{i}(1)\tau-\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\lambda^{*})^{2}\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\\
(1-\pi)(Y_{i}(D_{i}(1))-D_{i}(1)\tau-\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\lambda^{*})^{2}\mathbb{X}_{i}&(Y_{i}(D_{i}(1))-D_{i}(1)\tau-\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\lambda^{*})^{2}\mathbb{X}_{i}\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\end{pmatrix}\bigg|S_{i}\right]

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}\pi\mathbb{E}\left[\begin{pmatrix}(1-\pi)^{2}(Y_{i}(D_{i}(1))-D_{i}(1)\tau-\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\lambda^{*})^{2}&(1-\pi)(Y_{i}(D_{i}(1))-D_{i}(1)\tau-\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\lambda^{*})^{2}\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\\
(1-\pi)(Y_{i}(D_{i}(1))-D_{i}(1)\tau-\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\lambda^{*})^{2}\mathbb{X}_{i}&(Y_{i}(D_{i}(1))-D_{i}(1)\tau-\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\lambda^{*})^{2}\mathbb{X}_{i}\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\end{pmatrix}\bigg|S_{i}\right]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\longrightarrow}}\pi\mathbb{E}\left[\begin{pmatrix}(1-\pi)^{2}(Y_{i}(D_{i}(1))-D_{i}(1)\tau-\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\lambda^{*})^{2}&(1-\pi)(Y_{i}(D_{i}(1))-D_{i}(1)\tau-\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\lambda^{*})^{2}\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\\
(1-\pi)(Y_{i}(D_{i}(1))-D_{i}(1)\tau-\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\lambda^{*})^{2}\mathbb{X}_{i}&(Y_{i}(D_{i}(1))-D_{i}(1)\tau-\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\lambda^{*})^{2}\mathbb{X}_{i}\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\end{pmatrix}\right]

\displaystyle\equiv\Omega_{1}.

To see the convergence in probability in the above display, we note that

\displaystyle A_{i}\biggl\{\begin{pmatrix}(1-\pi)^{2}(Y_{i}(D_{i}(1))-D_{i}(1)\tau-\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\lambda^{*})^{2}&(1-\pi)(Y_{i}(D_{i}(1))-D_{i}(1)\tau-\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\lambda^{*})^{2}\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\\
(1-\pi)(Y_{i}(D_{i}(1))-D_{i}(1)\tau-\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\lambda^{*})^{2}\mathbb{X}_{i}&(Y_{i}(D_{i}(1))-D_{i}(1)\tau-\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\lambda^{*})^{2}\mathbb{X}_{i}\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle-\mathbb{E}\left[\begin{pmatrix}(1-\pi)^{2}(Y_{i}(D_{i}(1))-D_{i}(1)\tau-\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\lambda^{*})^{2}&(1-\pi)(Y_{i}(D_{i}(1))-D_{i}(1)\tau-\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\lambda^{*})^{2}\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\\
(1-\pi)(Y_{i}(D_{i}(1))-D_{i}(1)\tau-\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\lambda^{*})^{2}\mathbb{X}_{i}&(Y_{i}(D_{i}(1))-D_{i}(1)\tau-\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\lambda^{*})^{2}\mathbb{X}_{i}\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\end{pmatrix}\bigg|S_{i}\right]\biggr\}

is independent and conditionally mean zero given
(A^{(n)},S^{(n)})
. Therefore, by the conditional Chebyshev’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}A_{i}\biggl\{\begin{pmatrix}(1-\pi)^{2}(Y_{i}(D_{i}(1))-D_{i}(1)\tau-\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\lambda^{*})^{2}&(1-\pi)(Y_{i}(D_{i}(1))-D_{i}(1)\tau-\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\lambda^{*})^{2}\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\\
(1-\pi)(Y_{i}(D_{i}(1))-D_{i}(1)\tau-\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\lambda^{*})^{2}\mathbb{X}_{i}&(Y_{i}(D_{i}(1))-D_{i}(1)\tau-\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\lambda^{*})^{2}\mathbb{X}_{i}\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle-\mathbb{E}\left[\begin{pmatrix}(1-\pi)^{2}(Y_{i}(D_{i}(1))-D_{i}(1)\tau-\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\lambda^{*})^{2}&(1-\pi)(Y_{i}(D_{i}(1))-D_{i}(1)\tau-\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\lambda^{*})^{2}\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\\
(1-\pi)(Y_{i}(D_{i}(1))-D_{i}(1)\tau-\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\lambda^{*})^{2}\mathbb{X}_{i}&(Y_{i}(D_{i}(1))-D_{i}(1)\tau-\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\lambda^{*})^{2}\mathbb{X}_{i}\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\end{pmatrix}\bigg|S_{i}\right]\biggr\}

\displaystyle=o_{P}(1).

Also, by Assumption
2
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}(A_{i}-\pi)\mathbb{E}\left[\begin{pmatrix}(1-\pi)^{2}(Y_{i}(D_{i}(1))-D_{i}(1)\tau-\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\lambda^{*})^{2}&(1-\pi)(Y_{i}(D_{i}(1))-D_{i}(1)\tau-\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\lambda^{*})^{2}\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\\
(1-\pi)(Y_{i}(D_{i}(1))-D_{i}(1)\tau-\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\lambda^{*})^{2}\mathbb{X}_{i}&(Y_{i}(D_{i}(1))-D_{i}(1)\tau-\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\lambda^{*})^{2}\mathbb{X}_{i}\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\end{pmatrix}\bigg|S_{i}\right]

\displaystyle=o_{P}(1).

Last, by the usual Law of Large numbers for i.i.d. data, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}\pi\mathbb{E}\left[\begin{pmatrix}(1-\pi)^{2}(Y_{i}(D_{i}(1))-D_{i}(1)\tau-\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\lambda^{*})^{2}&(1-\pi)(Y_{i}(D_{i}(1))-D_{i}(1)\tau-\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\lambda^{*})^{2}\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\\
(1-\pi)(Y_{i}(D_{i}(1))-D_{i}(1)\tau-\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\lambda^{*})^{2}\mathbb{X}_{i}&(Y_{i}(D_{i}(1))-D_{i}(1)\tau-\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\lambda^{*})^{2}\mathbb{X}_{i}\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\end{pmatrix}\bigg|S_{i}\right]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\longrightarrow}}\Omega_{1}.

Similarly, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}(1-A_{i})\begin{pmatrix}\pi^{2}(Y_{i}(D_{i}(0))-D_{i}(0)\tau-\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\lambda^{*})^{2}&-\pi(Y_{i}(D_{i}(0))-D_{i}(0)\tau-\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\lambda^{*})^{2}\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\\
-\pi(Y_{i}(D_{i}(0))-D_{i}(0)\tau-\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\lambda^{*})^{2}\mathbb{X}_{i}&(Y_{i}(D_{i}(0))-D_{i}(0)\tau-\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\lambda^{*})^{2}\mathbb{X}_{i}\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\longrightarrow}}(1-\pi)\mathbb{E}\left[\begin{pmatrix}\pi^{2}(Y_{i}(D_{i}(0))-D_{i}(0)\tau-\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\lambda^{*})^{2}&-\pi(Y_{i}(D_{i}(0))-D_{i}(0)\tau-\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\lambda^{*})^{2}\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\\
-\pi(Y_{i}(D_{i}(0))-D_{i}(0)\tau-\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\lambda^{*})^{2}\mathbb{X}_{i}&(Y_{i}(D_{i}(0))-D_{i}(0)\tau-\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\lambda^{*})^{2}\mathbb{X}_{i}\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\end{pmatrix}\right]

\displaystyle\equiv\Omega_{0}.

Consequently, we have

\displaystyle\hat{\sigma}_{TSLS,naive}^{2}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\longrightarrow}}\frac{\begin{pmatrix}1&-\gamma_{D}^{\top}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\mathbb{E}(D(1)-D(0))&\gamma_{D}^{\top}\\
0&I\end{pmatrix}(\Omega_{1}+\Omega_{0})\begin{pmatrix}\mathbb{E}(D(1)-D(0))&\gamma_{D}^{\top}\\
0&I\end{pmatrix}^{\top}\begin{pmatrix}1\\
-\gamma_{D}\end{pmatrix}}{[\pi(1-\pi)]^{2}[\mathbb{E}(D(1)-D(0))]^{4}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\pi^{-1}\mathbb{E}(Y_{i}(D_{i}(1))-D_{i}(1)\tau-\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\lambda^{*})^{2}+(1-\pi)^{-1}\mathbb{E}(Y_{i}(D_{i}(1))-D_{i}(1)\tau-\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\lambda^{*})^{2}}{[\mathbb{E}(D(1)-D(0))]^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\sigma_{TSLS,0}^{2}+\sigma_{TSLS,1}^{2}+\sigma_{TSLS,2}^{2}+\tilde{\sigma}_{TSLS,3}^{2}}{(\mathbb{E}(D_{i}(1)-D_{i}(0)))^{2}}.

For the last result, by the proof Theorem
3.1
with
\overline{\mu}^{b}(a,s,x)=0
for
a=0,1
and
b=D,Y
and
\pi(s)=\pi
, we have

\displaystyle\sigma_{NA}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{\sum_{s\in S}\frac{p(s)}{\pi}Var(Y(D(1))-\tau D(1)|S=s)+\sum_{s\in S}\frac{p(s)}{1-\pi}Var(Y(D(0))-\tau D(0)|S=s)}{\mathbb{P}(D(1)>D(0))^{2}}

\displaystyle+\frac{Var(\mathbb{E}[W_{i}-Z_{i}|S_{i}]-\tau\left(\mathbb{E}[D_{i}(1)-D_{i}(0)|S_{i}]\right))}{\mathbb{P}(D(1)>D(0))^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbb{E}\frac{1}{\pi}Var(Y(D(1))-\tau D(1)|S)+\frac{1}{1-\pi}Var(Y(D(0))-\tau D(0)|S)}{\mathbb{P}(D(1)>D(0))^{2}}+\frac{\sigma_{TSLS,2}^{2}}{\mathbb{P}(D(1)>D(0))^{2}}.

Then, we have
\sigma_{NA}^{2}<\sigma_{TSLS}^{2}
if and only if

\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\pi}Var(Y(D(1))-\tau D(1)|S)+\frac{1}{1-\pi}Var(Y(D(0))-\tau D(0)|S)\right]<\sigma_{TSLS,0}^{2}+\sigma_{TSLS,1}^{2}+\sigma_{TSLS,3}^{2},

which is equivalent to

\displaystyle 2\left[\frac{\mathbb{E}cov(Y_{i}(D_{i}(1))-D_{i}(1)\tau,\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\lambda^{*}|S)}{\pi}+\frac{\mathbb{E}cov(Y_{i}(D_{i}(0))-D_{i}(0)\tau,\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\lambda^{*}|S)}{1-\pi}\right]\leq\frac{\mathbb{E}Var(\mathbb{X}_{i}^{\top}\lambda^{*}|S)}{\pi(1-\pi)}+\sigma_{TSLS,3}^{2}.

Appendix F
Proof of Theorem
3.1

Let

\displaystyle G

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\mathbb{E}\mathinner{\Bigl[\mathinner{\bigl(Y(1)-Y(0)\bigr)}\mathinner{\bigl(D(1)-D(0)\bigr)}\Bigr]},

\displaystyle H

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\mathbb{E}\mathinner{\bigl[D(1)-D(0)\bigr]},

\displaystyle\hat{G}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}\left[\frac{A_{i}(Y_{i}-\hat{\mu}^{Y}(1,S_{i},X_{i}))}{\hat{\pi}(S_{i})}-\frac{(1-A_{i})(Y_{i}-\hat{\mu}^{Y}(0,S_{i},X_{i}))}{1-\hat{\pi}(S_{i})}+\hat{\mu}^{Y}(1,S_{i},X_{i})-\hat{\mu}^{Y}(0,S_{i},X_{i})\right],

\displaystyle\hat{H}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}\left[\frac{A_{i}(D_{i}-\hat{\mu}^{D}(1,S_{i},X_{i}))}{\hat{\pi}(S_{i})}-\frac{(1-A_{i})(D_{i}-\hat{\mu}^{D}(0,S_{i},X_{i}))}{1-\hat{\pi}(S_{i})}+\hat{\mu}^{D}(1,S_{i},X_{i})-\hat{\mu}^{D}(0,S_{i},X_{i})\right].

Then, we have

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}(\hat{\tau}-\tau)

\displaystyle=\sqrt{n}\mathinner{\biggl(\frac{\hat{G}}{\hat{H}}-\frac{G}{H}\biggr)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\hat{H}}\sqrt{n}(\hat{G}-G)-\frac{G}{\hat{H}H}\sqrt{n}(\hat{H}-H)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\hat{H}}\mathinner{\biggl[\sqrt{n}(\hat{G}-G)-\tau\sqrt{n}(\hat{H}-H)\biggr]}.

(F.1)

Next, we divide the proof into three steps. In the first step, we obtain the
linear expansion of
\sqrt{n}(\hat{G}-G)
. Based on the same argument, we can
obtain the linear expansion of
\sqrt{n}(\hat{H}-H)
. In the second step, we
obtain the linear expansion of
\sqrt{n}(\hat{\tau}-\tau)
and then prove
the asymptotic normality. In the third step, we show the consistency of

\hat{\sigma}
. The second result in the Theorem is obvious given the
semiparametric efficiency bound derived in Theorem
4.1
.

Step 1.
We have

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}(\hat{G}-G)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i\in[n]}\biggl[\frac{A_{i}(Y_{i}-\hat{\mu}^{Y}(1,S_{i},X_{i}))}{\hat{\pi}(S_{i})}-\frac{(1-A_{i})(Y_{i}-\hat{\mu}^{Y}(0,S_{i},X_{i}))}{1-\hat{\pi}(S_{i})}

\displaystyle+\hat{\mu}^{Y}(1,S_{i},X_{i})-\hat{\mu}^{Y}(0,S_{i},X_{i})\biggr]-\sqrt{n}G

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathinner{\biggl[\hat{\mu}^{Y}(1,S_{i},X_{i})-\frac{A_{i}\hat{\mu}^{Y}(1,S_{i},X_{i})}{\hat{\pi}(S_{i})}\biggr]}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathinner{\biggl[\frac{(1-A_{i})\hat{\mu}^{Y}(0,S_{i},X_{i})}{1-\hat{\pi}(S_{i})}-\hat{\mu}^{Y}(0,S_{i},X_{i})\biggr]}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{A_{i}Y_{i}}{\hat{\pi}(S_{i})}-\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{(1-A_{i})Y_{i}}{1-\hat{\pi}(S_{i})}-\sqrt{n}G

\displaystyle=\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}R_{n,1}+R_{n,2}+R_{n,3},

where

\displaystyle R_{n,1}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathinner{\biggl[\hat{\mu}^{Y}(1,S_{i},X_{i})-\frac{A_{i}\hat{\mu}^{Y}(1,S_{i},X_{i})}{\hat{\pi}(S_{i})}\biggr]},

\displaystyle R_{n,2}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathinner{\biggl[\frac{(1-A_{i})\hat{\mu}^{Y}(0,S_{i},X_{i})}{1-\hat{\pi}(S_{i})}-\hat{\mu}^{Y}(0,S_{i},X_{i})\biggr]},

\displaystyle R_{n,3}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{A_{i}Y_{i}}{\hat{\pi}(S_{i})}-\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{(1-A_{i})Y_{i}}{1-\hat{\pi}(S_{i})}-\sqrt{n}G.

Lemma
P.1
shows that

\displaystyle R_{n,1}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathinner{\biggl(1-\frac{1}{\pi(S_{i})}\biggr)}A_{i}\tilde{\mu}^{Y}(1,S_{i},X_{i})+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(1-A_{i})\tilde{\mu}^{Y}(1,S_{i},X_{i})+o_{p}(1),

\displaystyle R_{n,2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathinner{\biggl(\frac{1}{1-\pi(S_{i})}-1\biggr)}(1-A_{i})\tilde{\mu}^{Y}(0,S_{i},X_{i})-\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}A_{i}\tilde{\mu}^{Y}(0,S_{i},X_{i})+o_{p}(1),

\displaystyle R_{n,3}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{1}{\pi(S_{i})}\tilde{W}_{i}A_{i}-\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{1-A_{i}}{1-\pi(S_{i})}\tilde{Z}_{i}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathinner{\bigl(\mathbb{E}[W_{i}-Z_{i}|S_{i}]-\mathbb{E}[W_{i}-Z_{i}]\bigr)}.

This implies

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}(\hat{G}-G)

\displaystyle=\biggl\{\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left[\left(1-\frac{1}{\pi(S_{i})}\right)\tilde{\mu}^{Y}(1,S_{i},X_{i})-\tilde{\mu}^{Y}(0,S_{i},X_{i})+\frac{\tilde{W}_{i}}{\pi(S_{i})}\right]A_{i}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left[\left(\frac{1}{1-\pi(S_{i})}-1\right)\tilde{\mu}^{Y}(0,S_{i},X_{i})+\tilde{\mu}^{Y}(1,S_{i},X_{i})-\frac{\tilde{Z}_{i}}{1-\pi(S_{i})}\right](1-A_{i})\biggr\}

\displaystyle+\left\{\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathinner{\bigl(\mathbb{E}[W_{i}-Z_{i}|S_{i}]-\mathbb{E}[W_{i}-Z_{i}]\bigr)}\right\}+o_{p}(1).

(F.2)

Similarly, we can show that

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}(\hat{H}-H)

\displaystyle=\biggl\{\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left[\left(1-\frac{1}{\pi(S_{i})}\right)\tilde{\mu}^{D}(1,S_{i},X_{i})-\tilde{\mu}^{D}(0,S_{i},X_{i})+\frac{\tilde{D}_{i}(1)}{\pi(S_{i})}\right]A_{i}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left[\left(\frac{1}{1-\pi(S_{i})}-1\right)\tilde{\mu}^{D}(0,S_{i},X_{i})+\tilde{\mu}^{D}(1,S_{i},X_{i})-\frac{\tilde{D}_{i}(0)}{1-\pi(S_{i})}\right](1-A_{i})\biggr\}

\displaystyle+\left\{\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathinner{\bigl(\mathbb{E}[D_{i}(1)-D_{i}(0)|S_{i}]-\mathbb{E}[D_{i}(1)-D_{i}(0)]\bigr)}\right\}+o_{p}(1),

(F.3)

where
\tilde{D}_{i}(a)=D_{i}(a)-\mathbb{E}(D_{i}(a)|S_{i})
for
a=0,1

and
\tilde{\mu}^{D}(0,s,X_{i})=\overline{\mu}^{D}(0,s,X_{i})-\mathbb{E}(\overline{\mu}^{D}(0,S_{i},X_{i})|S_{i}=s).

Combining (
F.1
), (
F.2
), and (
F.3
), we obtain the linear
expansion for
\hat{\tau}
as

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}(\hat{\tau}-\tau)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\hat{H}}\left[\sqrt{n}(\hat{G}-G)-\tau\sqrt{n}(\hat{H}-H)\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\hat{H}}\left[\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Xi_{1}(\mathcal{D}_{i},S_{i})A_{i}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Xi_{0}(\mathcal{D}_{i},S_{i})(1-A_{i})+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Xi_{2}(S_{i})\right]+o_{p}(1),

where
\mathcal{D}_{i}=\{Y_{i}(1),Y_{i}(0),D_{i}(1),D_{i}(0),X_{i}\}
,

\displaystyle\Xi_{1}(\mathcal{D}_{i},S_{i})

\displaystyle=\left[\left(1-\frac{1}{\pi(S_{i})}\right)\tilde{\mu}^{Y}(1,S_{i},X_{i})-\tilde{\mu}^{Y}(0,S_{i},X_{i})+\frac{\tilde{W}_{i}}{\pi(S_{i})}\right]

\displaystyle-\tau\left[\left(1-\frac{1}{\pi(S_{i})}\right)\tilde{\mu}^{D}(1,S_{i},X_{i})-\tilde{\mu}^{D}(0,S_{i},X_{i})+\frac{\tilde{D}_{i}(1)}{\pi(S_{i})}\right],

\displaystyle\Xi_{0}(\mathcal{D}_{i},S_{i})

\displaystyle=\left[\left(\frac{1}{1-\pi(S_{i})}-1\right)\tilde{\mu}^{Y}(0,S_{i},X_{i})+\tilde{\mu}^{Y}(1,S_{i},X_{i})-\frac{\tilde{Z}_{i}}{1-\pi(S_{i})}\right]

\displaystyle-\tau\left[\left(\frac{1}{1-\pi(S_{i})}-1\right)\tilde{\mu}^{D}(0,S_{i},X_{i})+\tilde{\mu}^{D}(1,S_{i},X_{i})-\frac{\tilde{D}_{i}(0)}{1-\pi(S_{i})}\right],

\displaystyle\Xi_{2}(S_{i})

\displaystyle=\mathinner{\bigl(\mathbb{E}[W_{i}-Z_{i}|S_{i}]-\mathbb{E}[W_{i}-Z_{i}]\bigr)}-\tau\left[\mathbb{E}[D_{i}(1)-D_{i}(0)|S_{i}]-\mathbb{E}[D_{i}(1)-D_{i}(0)]\right].

Step 2.
Lemma
P.2
implies that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Xi_{1}(\mathcal{D}_{i},S_{i})A_{i}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma_{1}^{2}),\quad\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Xi_{0}(\mathcal{D}_{i},S_{i})(1-A_{i})\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma_{0}^{2}),\quad\text{and}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Xi_{2}(S_{i})\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma_{2}^{2}),

and the three terms are asymptotically independent, where

\displaystyle\sigma_{1}^{2}=\mathbb{E}\pi(S_{i})\Xi_{1}^{2}(\mathcal{D}_{i},S_{i}),\quad\sigma_{0}^{2}=\mathbb{E}(1-\pi(S_{i}))\Xi_{0}^{2}(\mathcal{D}_{i},S_{i}),\quad\text{and}\quad\sigma_{2}^{2}=\mathbb{E}\Xi_{2}^{2}(S_{i}).

This further implies
\hat{H}\overset{p}{\longrightarrow}H
and

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}(\hat{\tau}-\tau)\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{N}\left(0,\frac{\sigma_{1}^{2}+\sigma_{0}^{2}+\sigma_{2}^{2}}{H^{2}}\right),

Step 3.
We aim to show the consistency of
\hat{\sigma}^{2}
. First
note that

\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Xi_{H,i}=\hat{H}\overset{p}{\longrightarrow}H=\mathbb{E}(D_{i}(1)-D_{i}(0)).

In addition, Lemma
P.3
shows.

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}A_{i}\hat{\Xi}_{1}^{2}(\mathcal{D}_{i},S_{i})\overset{p}{\longrightarrow}\sigma_{1}^{2},\quad\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(1-A_{i})\hat{\Xi}_{0}^{2}(\mathcal{D}_{i},S_{i})\overset{p}{\longrightarrow}\sigma_{0}^{2},\quad\text{and}\quad\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\hat{\Xi}_{2}^{2}(\mathcal{D}_{i},S_{i})\overset{p}{\longrightarrow}\sigma_{2}^{2}.

This implies
\hat{\sigma}^{2}\overset{p}{\longrightarrow}\sigma^{2}
.

Appendix G
Proof of Theorem
4.1

Without loss of generality, we assume
A_{i}=\phi_{i}(\{S_{i}\}_{i\in[n]},U)
, where
\phi_{i}(\cdot)
is a deterministic function and
U
is a
random variable (vector) with density
P_{U}(\cdot)
and is independent of
everything else in the data. Further denote
\mathcal{Y}_{i}(a)=\{Y_{i}(D_{i}(a)),D_{i}(a),X_{i}\}
. We consider parametric submodels indexed
by a generic parameter
\theta
. The likelihoods of
S_{i}
evaluated at
s

and
\mathcal{Y}_{i}(a)
given
S_{i}=s
evaluated at
\overline{y}
are
written as
f_{S}(s;\theta)
and
f_{\mathcal{Y}(a)|S}(\overline{y}|s;\theta)

for
a=0,1
, respectively. The density of
U
does not depend on
\theta
. Let

\theta_{n}=\theta^{*}+h/\sqrt{n}
, where
\theta^{*}
indexes the true
underlying DGP.

By Assumption
1
, the joint likelihood of
\{Y_{i},X_{i},S_{i},A_{i}\}_{i\in[n]}
under
\theta
can be written as

\displaystyle P_{U}(u)\Pi_{i\in[n]}\left[f_{S}(s_{i};\theta)\Pi_{a=0,1}f_{\mathcal{Y}(a)|S}(\tilde{y}_{i}(a)|s_{i};\theta)^{1\{\phi_{i}(s_{1},\cdots,s_{n},u)=a\}}\right]

where
(x_{i},y_{i}(d_{i}(a)),d_{i}(a),u,s_{i})
are the realizations

(X_{i},Y_{i}(D_{i}(a)),D_{i}(a),U,S_{i})
for
i\in[n]
and
\tilde{y}_{i}(a)=\{y_{i}(d_{i}(a)),d_{i}(a),x_{i}\}
. We make the following
regularity assumptions with respect to the submodel.

Assumption 10
.

(i)

Suppose
f_{S}(s;\theta)
and
f_{\mathcal{Y}(a)|S}(\overline{y}|s;\theta)
for
a=0,1
are differentiable in quadratic mean at
\theta^{*}
with score functions
g_{s}(S_{i})
and
g_{a}(\mathcal{Y}_{i}(a)|S_{i})
for
a=0,1
, respectively, such that

\displaystyle\dot{f}_{\mathcal{Y}(a)|S}(\overline{y}|s;\theta)=\frac{\partial\log(f_{\mathcal{Y}(a)|S}(\overline{y}|s;\theta))}{\partial\theta},\quad\dot{f}_{S}(s;\theta)=\frac{\partial\log(f_{S}(s;\theta))}{\partial\theta},

\displaystyle\dot{f}_{\mathcal{Y}(a)|S}(\mathcal{Y}_{i}(a)|S_{i};\theta^{*})=g_{a}(\mathcal{Y}_{i}(a)|S_{i}),\quad\text{and}\quad\dot{f}_{S}(S_{i};\theta^{*})=g_{s}(S_{i}).

(ii)

Suppose
\dot{f}_{\mathcal{Y}(a)|S}(\overline{y}|s;\theta)
and
\dot{f}_{S}(s;\theta)
are continuous at
\theta^{*}
so that there exist a
sequence
t_{n}=o(1)
and a function
L_{a}(\mathcal{Y}_{i}(a),S_{i})
such
that

\displaystyle|\dot{f}_{\mathcal{Y}(a)|S}(\mathcal{Y}_{i}(a)|S_{i};\theta^{*}+h/\sqrt{n})-g_{a}(\mathcal{Y}_{i}(a)|S_{i})|+|\dot{f}_{S}(S_{i};\theta^{*}+h/\sqrt{n})-g_{s}(S_{i})|\leq t_{n}L_{a}(\mathcal{Y}_{i}(a),S_{i})

and
\mathbb{E}|Y_{i}(D_{i}(a))L_{a}(\mathcal{Y}_{i}(a),S_{i})|<\infty
for

a=0,1
.

(iii)

Suppose there exists a constant
C>0
such that

\displaystyle\max_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\mathbb{E}\mathinner{\Bigl[\left.\mathinner{\!\bigl\lvert\underline{\Xi}_{1}(\mathcal{D}_{i},S_{i})g_{1}(\mathcal{Y}_{i}(1)|S_{i})\bigr\rvert}+\mathinner{\!\bigl\lvert\underline{\Xi}_{0}(\mathcal{D}_{i},S_{i})g_{0}(\mathcal{Y}_{i}(0)|S_{i})\bigr\rvert}\right|S_{i}=s\Bigr]}

\displaystyle\leq C

\displaystyle\max_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\mathbb{E}\mathinner{\Bigl[\left.\underline{\Xi}_{1}(\mathcal{D}_{i},S_{i})^{2}+\underline{\Xi}_{0}(\mathcal{D}_{i},S_{i})^{2}\right|S_{i}=s\Bigr]}

\displaystyle\leq C,

where
\underline{\Xi}_{1}(\mathcal{D}_{i},S_{i})
,
\underline{\Xi}_{0}(\mathcal{D}_{i},S_{i})
and
\underline{\Xi}_{2}(S_{i})
are defined as
\Xi_{1}(\mathcal{D}_{i},S_{i})
,
\Xi_{0}(\mathcal{D}_{i},S_{i})
and
\Xi_{2}(S_{i})
in (
3.7
)–(
3.9
),
respectively, with the researcher-specified working model
\overline{\mu}^{b}(a,s,x)
equal to the true specification
\mu^{b}(a,s,x)
for all

(a,b,s,x)\in\{0,1\}\times\{D,Y\}\times\mathcal{SX}
.

We denote
\tau(\theta)=\mathbb{E}_{\theta}(Y_{i}(1)-Y_{i}(0)|D_{i}(1)>D_{i}(0))
, where
\mathbb{E}_{\theta}(\cdot)
means the expectation
is taken with the parametric submodel indexed by
\theta
. We further denote

\mathbb{E}(\cdot)=\mathbb{E}_{\theta^{*}}(\cdot)
, which is the expectation
with respect to the true DGP.

Proof of Theorem
4.1
.
Following the same argument in

Armstrong 2022
, in order to show the semiparametric efficiency bound, we only need
to show (1) local asymptotic normality of the log likelihood ratio for the
parametric submodel with tangent set of the form

\displaystyle\mathbb{T}=\begin{pmatrix}&\Psi(\mathcal{D}_{i},S_{i},A_{i})=g_{s}(S_{i})+A_{i}g_{1}(\mathcal{Y}_{i}(1)|S_{i})+(1-A_{i})g_{0}(\mathcal{Y}_{i}(0)|S_{i})\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\\
&\mathbb{E}\mathinner{\bigl[g_{s}^{2}(S_{i})+\sum_{a=0,1}g_{a}^{2}(\mathcal{Y}_{i}(a)|S_{i})\bigr]}<\infty,\mathbb{E}g_{s}(S_{i})=0,\mathbb{E}(g_{a}(\mathcal{Y}_{i}(a)|S_{i})|S_{i})=0,\\
&\mathbb{E}(g_{1}(\mathcal{Y}_{i}(1)|S_{i})|X_{i},S_{i})=\mathbb{E}(g_{0}(\mathcal{Y}_{i}(0)|S_{i})|X_{i},S_{i})\end{pmatrix}.

(G.1)

and (2)
\sqrt{n}(\tau(\theta^{*}+h/\sqrt{n})-\tau(\theta^{*}))=\langle\tilde{\Psi},\Psi\rangle_{\bar{\mathbb{P}}}h+o(1)
, where

\tilde{\Psi}(\mathcal{D}_{i},S_{i},A_{i})
is the efficient score defined as

\displaystyle\tilde{\Psi}(\mathcal{D}_{i},S_{i},A_{i})=\mathinner{\bigl[\underline{\Xi}_{2}(S_{i})+A_{i}\underline{\Xi}_{1}(\mathcal{D}_{i},S_{i})+(1-A_{i})\underline{\Xi}_{0}(\mathcal{D}_{i},S_{i})\bigr]}/\mathbb{E}[D_{i}(1)-D_{i}(0)]

(G.2)

and
\langle\tilde{\Psi},\Psi\rangle_{\bar{\mathbb{P}}}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}\mathbb{E}\tilde{\Psi}(\mathcal{D}_{i},S_{i},A_{i})\Psi(\mathcal{D}_{i},S_{i},A_{i})
is the inner product w.r.t. measure
\bar{\mathbb{P}}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}\mathbb{P}_{i}
. We establish these two results
in two steps.

Step 1.

Denote
\theta_{n}=\theta^{*}+h/\sqrt{n}
where
\theta^{*}
is fixed and

\mathbb{P}_{n,h}
as the joint distribution of
\{Y_{i},X_{i},S_{i},A_{i}\}_{i\in[n]}
under
\theta_{n}
. The log likelihood ratio for

\theta_{n}
against
\theta^{*}
is given by

\displaystyle\ell_{n,h}=\sum_{i\in[n]}\tilde{\ell}_{s}(S_{i};\theta_{n})+\sum_{a=0,1}\sum_{i\in[n]}1\{A_{i}=a\}\tilde{\ell}_{\mathcal{Y}(a)|S}(\mathcal{Y}_{i}|S_{i};\theta_{n}),

where
\mathcal{Y}_{i}=(Y_{i},D_{i},X_{i})
,
\tilde{\ell}_{s}(S_{i};\theta_{n})=\log\left(\frac{f_{S}(S_{i};\theta_{n})}{f_{S}(S_{i};\theta^{*})}\right)
, and
\tilde{\ell}_{\mathcal{Y}(a)|S}(\mathcal{Y}_{i}|S_{i};\theta_{n})=\log\left(\frac{f_{\mathcal{Y}(a)|S}(\mathcal{Y}_{i}|S_{i};\theta_{n})}{f_{\mathcal{Y}(a)|S}(\mathcal{Y}_{i}|S_{i};\theta^{*})}\right)
for
a=0,1
. Then,
Armstrong 2022
shows

\ell_{n,h}
converges in distribution to a
\mathcal{N}(-h^{\prime}\tilde{I}^{*}h/2,h^{\prime}\tilde{I}^{*}h)
law under
\theta^{*}
where
\tilde{I}^{*}
is the limit of

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\theta^{*}}g_{s}^{2}(S_{i})+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}\sum_{a=0,1}1\{A_{i}=a\}\mathbb{E}_{\theta^{*}}\left[g_{a}^{2}(\mathcal{Y}_{i}(a)|S_{i})|S_{i}\right].

and the score for this parametric submodel can be written as

\displaystyle\Psi(\mathcal{D}_{i},S_{i},A_{i})=g_{s}(S_{i})+A_{i}g_{1}(\mathcal{Y}_{i}(1)|S_{i})+(1-A_{i})g_{0}(\mathcal{Y}_{i}(0)|S_{i}).

(G.3)

We note that by definition, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}g_{s}(S_{i})=0\quad\text{and}\quad\mathbb{E}(g_{a}(\mathcal{Y}_{i}(a)|S_{i})|S_{i})=0.

In addition, we have the equality that, for an arbitrary function
h(\cdot)

of
X
such that
\mathbb{E}h^{2}(X)<\infty
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\theta}(h(X)|S)=\int_{x}h(x)f_{X|S}(x|S;\theta)\text{d}x

\displaystyle=\int_{x}h(x)\mathinner{\biggl[\int_{y(d(a)),d(a)}f_{Y(D(a)),D(a)|X,S}(y(d(a)),d(a)|x,S;\theta)\text{d}y(d(a))\text{d}d(a)\biggr]}f_{X|S}(x|S;\theta)\text{d}x

\displaystyle=\int_{y(d(a)),d(a),x}h(x)f_{\mathcal{Y}(a)|S}(y(d(a)),d(a),x|S;\theta)\text{d}y(d(a))\text{d}d(a)\text{d}x

(G.4)

for
a=0,1
, where

is the

(Y(D(a)),D(a),X)
given
S
for

. We note that,
for

,

\displaystyle\frac{\partial f_{\mathcal{Y}(a)|S}(y(d(a)),d(a),x|S;\theta^{*})}{\partial\theta}

\displaystyle=f_{\mathcal{Y}(a)|S}(y(d(a)),d(a),x|S;\theta^{*})g_{a}(\mathcal{Y}(a)|S).

Therefore, taking derivatives of
\theta
in (

) and evaluating the

, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\mathinner{\bigl[h(X)g_{1}(\mathcal{Y}(1)|S)|S\bigr]}=\mathbb{E}\mathinner{\bigl[h(X)g_{0}(\mathcal{Y}(0)|S)|S\bigr]},

which implies

. Therefore,

Step 2.

We have

\displaystyle\tau(\theta)=\frac{\mathbb{E}_{\theta}(Y_{i}(D_{i}(1))-Y_{i}(D_{i}(0)))}{\mathbb{E}_{\theta}(D_{i}(1)-D_{i}(0))}.

By the mean-value theorem, we have

\displaystyle\tau(\theta^{*}+h/\sqrt{n})-\tau(\theta^{*})

\displaystyle=\frac{\partial\tau(\theta)}{\partial\theta}\bigg|_{\theta=\tilde{\theta}}\frac{h}{\sqrt{n}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\partial\tau(\theta)}{\partial\theta}\bigg|_{\theta=\theta^{*}}\frac{h}{\sqrt{n}}+\left[\frac{\partial\tau(\theta)}{\partial\theta}\bigg|_{\theta=\tilde{\theta}}-\frac{\partial\tau(\theta)}{\partial\theta}\bigg|_{\theta=\theta^{*}}\right]\frac{h}{\sqrt{n}}.

Let
G(\theta)=\mathbb{E}_{\theta}\mathinner{\bigl[Y(D(1))-Y(D(0))\bigr]}
,
H(\theta)=\mathbb{E}_{\theta}\mathinner{\bigl[D(1)-D(0)\bigr]}
,
G=G(\theta^{*})
, and
H=H(\theta^{*})
. Note that
\tau(\theta)=G(\theta)/H(\theta)
and
\tau=G/H
.
Then, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\partial G(\theta)}{\partial\theta}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\theta}\mathinner{\bigl[Y(D(1))(\dot{f}_{\mathcal{Y}(1)|S}(\mathcal{Y}(1)|S;\theta)+\dot{f}_{S}(S;\theta)\bigr]}-\mathbb{E}_{\theta}\mathinner{\bigl[Y(D(0))(\dot{f}_{\mathcal{Y}(0)|S}(\mathcal{Y}(0)|S;\theta)+\dot{f}_{S}(S;\theta))\bigr]}

\displaystyle\frac{\partial H(\theta)}{\partial\theta}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\theta}\mathinner{\bigl[D(1)(\dot{f}_{\mathcal{Y}(1)|S}(\mathcal{Y}(1)|S;\theta)+\dot{f}_{S}(S;\theta))\bigr]}-\mathbb{E}_{\theta}\mathinner{\bigl[D(0)(\dot{f}_{\mathcal{Y}(0)|S}(\mathcal{Y}(0)|S;\theta)+\dot{f}_{S}(S;\theta))\bigr]}.

Therefore by Assumption
10
we can find a constant
L
such that

\displaystyle\biggl|\frac{\partial\tau(\theta)}{\partial\theta}\bigg|_{\theta=\tilde{\theta}}-\frac{\partial\tau(\theta)}{\partial\theta}\bigg|_{\theta=\theta^{*}}\biggr|

\displaystyle=\biggl|\frac{H(\tilde{\theta})\frac{\partial G(\tilde{\theta})}{\partial\theta}-G(\tilde{\theta})\frac{\partial H(\tilde{\theta})}{\partial\theta}}{H^{2}(\tilde{\theta})}-\frac{H(\theta^{*})\frac{\partial G(\theta^{*})}{\partial\theta}-G(\theta^{*})\frac{\partial H(\theta^{*})}{\partial\theta}}{H^{2}(\theta^{*})}\biggr|

\displaystyle\leq t_{n}L.

This implies

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}(\tau(\theta^{*}+h/\sqrt{n})-\tau(\theta^{*}))=\frac{\partial\tau(\theta)}{\partial\theta}\bigg|_{\theta=\theta^{*}}h+o(1).

(G.5)

In addition, following the calculation by
Frölich 2007
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\tau(\theta)}{\partial\theta}\bigg|_{\theta=\theta^{*}}=\frac{\left[\frac{\partial G(\theta)}{\partial\theta}-\tau\frac{\partial H(\theta)}{\partial\theta}\right]\bigg|_{\theta=\theta^{*}}}{H}

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[(Y(D(1))-\tau D(1))(g_{1}(\mathcal{Y}(1)|S)+g_{s}(S))\right]}{H}-\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[(Y(D(0))-\tau D(0))(g_{0}(\mathcal{Y}(0)|S)+g_{s}(S))\right]}{H},

where for notation simplicity, we write
\mathbb{E}_{\theta^{*}}
as

\mathbb{E}
. Let

\displaystyle\Gamma(X,S)

\displaystyle=\biggl[\pi(S)(\mathbb{E}(Z|X,S)-\mathbb{E}(Z|S))+\left(1-\pi(S)\right)(\mathbb{E}(W|X,S)-\mathbb{E}(W|S))

\displaystyle-\tau\left(\pi(S)(\mathbb{E}(D(0)|X,S)-\mathbb{E}(D(0)|S))+\left(1-\pi(S)\right)(\mathbb{E}(D(1)|X,S)-\mathbb{E}(D(1)|S))\right)\biggr].

Then, we have

\displaystyle Y(D(1))-\tau D(1)=\pi(S)\underline{\Xi}_{1}(\mathcal{D},S)+\mathbb{E}(W-\tau D(1)|S)+\Gamma(X,S),

\displaystyle Y(D(0))-\tau D(0)=-(1-\pi(S))\underline{\Xi}_{0}(\mathcal{D},S)+\mathbb{E}(Z-\tau D(0)|S)+\Gamma(X,S).

This implies

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(Y(D(1))-\tau D(1))(g_{1}(\mathcal{Y}(1)|S)+g_{s}(S))

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\pi(S)\underline{\Xi}_{1}(\mathcal{D},S)g_{1}(\mathcal{Y}(1)|S)+\mathbb{E}\Gamma(X,S)g_{1}(\mathcal{Y}(1)|S)+\mathbb{E}(\mathbb{E}(W-\tau D(1)|S)g_{s}(S)),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(Y(D(0))-\tau D(0))(g_{0}(\mathcal{Y}(0)|S)+g_{s}(S))

\displaystyle=-\mathbb{E}(1-\pi(S))\Xi_{0}(\mathcal{D},S)g_{0}(\mathcal{Y}(0)|S)+\mathbb{E}\Gamma(X,S)g_{0}(\mathcal{Y}(0)|S)+\mathbb{E}(\mathbb{E}(Z-\tau D(0)|S)g_{s}(S)),

where we have used

,

and

for

. Then

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\tau(\theta)}{\partial\theta}\bigg|_{\theta=\theta^{*}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbb{E}\pi(S)\underline{\Xi}_{1}(\mathcal{D},S)g_{1}(\mathcal{Y}(1)|S)}{H}+\frac{\mathbb{E}(1-\pi(S))\underline{\Xi}_{0}(\mathcal{D},S)g_{0}(\mathcal{Y}(0)|S)}{H}+\frac{\mathbb{E}g_{s}(S)\underline{\Xi}_{2}(S)}{H}

\displaystyle+\frac{\mathbb{E}\Gamma(X,S)(g_{1}(\mathcal{Y}(1)|S)-g_{0}(\mathcal{Y}(0)|S))}{H}

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbb{E}\pi(S)\underline{\Xi}_{1}(\mathcal{D},S)g_{1}(\mathcal{Y}(1)|S)}{H}+\frac{\mathbb{E}(1-\pi(S))\underline{\Xi}_{0}(\mathcal{D},S)g_{0}(\mathcal{Y}(0)|S)}{H}+\frac{\mathbb{E}g_{s}(S)\underline{\Xi}_{2}(S)}{H}.

(G.6)

where the last equality is due to (
G.1
).

On the other hand, we note that

\displaystyle\langle\tilde{\Psi},\Psi\rangle_{\bar{\mathbb{P}}}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}\left[\frac{\mathbb{E}\mathinner{\bigl[g_{s}(S_{i})\underline{\Xi}_{2}(S_{i})\bigr]}}{H}+\frac{\mathbb{E}\mathinner{\bigl[A_{i}\underline{\Xi}_{1}(\mathcal{D}_{i},S_{i})g_{1}(\mathcal{Y}_{i}(1)|S_{i})\bigr]}}{H}+\frac{\mathbb{E}\mathinner{\bigl[(1-A_{i})\underline{\Xi}_{0}(\mathcal{D}_{i},S_{i})g_{0}(\mathcal{Y}_{i}(0)|S_{i})\bigr]}}{H}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{\partial\tau(\theta)}{\partial\theta}\bigg|_{\theta=\theta^{*}}+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}\left[\frac{\mathbb{E}\mathinner{\bigl[(A_{i}-\pi(S_{i}))\underline{\Xi}_{1}(\mathcal{D}_{i},S_{i})g_{1}(\mathcal{Y}_{i}(1)|S_{i})\bigr]}}{H}-\frac{\mathbb{E}\mathinner{\bigl[(A_{i}-\pi(S_{i}))\underline{\Xi}_{0}(\mathcal{D}_{i},S_{i})g_{0}(\mathcal{Y}_{i}(0)|S_{i})\bigr]}}{H}\right].

In addition, by Assumption
10
, we have, for some constant

,
that

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}\left[\frac{\mathbb{E}(A_{i}-\pi(S_{i}))\underline{\Xi}_{1}(\mathcal{D}_{i},S_{i})g_{1}(\mathcal{Y}_{i}(1)|S_{i})}{H}-\frac{\mathbb{E}(A_{i}-\pi(S_{i}))\underline{\Xi}_{0}(\mathcal{D}_{i},S_{i})g_{0}(\mathcal{Y}_{i}(0)|S_{i})}{H}\right]\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{n}\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\mathbb{E}|B_{n}(s)|=o(1),

where the inequality is by law of iterated expectation and Assumption

(iii) and the last equality is due to

.
17
17

17

Since

.
This implies

\displaystyle\langle\tilde{\Psi},\Psi\rangle_{\bar{\mathbb{P}}}=\frac{\partial\tau(\theta)}{\partial\theta}\bigg|_{\theta=\theta^{*}}+o(1).

(G.7)
