\displaystyle\langle\tilde{\Psi},\tilde{\Psi}\rangle_{\bar{\mathbb{P}}}\rightarrow\underline{\sigma}^{2}.

Appendix H
Proof of Theorem
5.1

The proof is divided into two steps. In the first step, we show Assumption

3
(i). In the second step, we establish Assumptions

3
(ii) and
3
(iii).

Step 1.
Recall

\displaystyle\Delta^{Y}(a,s,X_{i})=\hat{\mu}^{Y}(a,s,X_{i})-\overline{\mu}^{Y}(a,s,X_{i})=\Lambda_{a,s}^{Y}(X_{i},\hat{\theta}_{a,s})-\Lambda_{a,s}^{Y}(X_{i},\theta_{a,s}),

and
\{X_{i}^{s}\}_{i\in[n]}
is generated independently from the
distribution of
X_{i}
given
S_{i}=s
, and so is independent of

\{A_{i},S_{i}\}_{i\in[n]}
. Let
M_{a,s}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\mathbb{E}[\Lambda_{a,s}^{Y}(X_{i},\theta_{1})-\Lambda_{a,s}^{Y}(X_{i},\theta_{2})|S_{i}=s]=\mathbb{E}[\Lambda_{a,s}^{Y}(X_{i}^{s},\theta_{1})-\Lambda_{a,s}^{Y}(X_{i}^{s},\theta_{2})]
. We have

\displaystyle\biggl|\frac{\sum_{i\in I_{1}(s)}\Delta^{Y}(a,s,X_{i})}{n_{1}(s)}-\frac{\sum_{i\in I_{0}(s)}\Delta^{Y}(a,s,X_{i})}{n_{0}(s)}\biggr|

\displaystyle\leq\mathinner{\!\biggl\lvert\frac{\sum_{i\in I_{1}(s)}[\Delta^{Y}(a,s,X_{i})-M_{a,s}(\hat{\theta}_{a,s},\theta_{a,s})]}{n_{1}(s)}\biggr\rvert}+\mathinner{\!\biggl\lvert\frac{\sum_{i\in I_{0}(s)}[\Delta^{Y}(a,s,X_{i})-M_{a,s}(\hat{\theta}_{a,s},\theta_{a,s})]}{n_{0}(s)}\biggr\rvert}

\displaystyle=o_{p}(n^{-1/2}).

(H.1)

To see the last equality, we note that, for any
\varepsilon>0
, with
probability approaching one (w.p.a.1), we have

\displaystyle\max_{s\in\mathcal{S}}||\hat{\theta}_{a,s}-\theta_{a,s}||_{2}\leq\varepsilon.

Therefore, on the event
\mathcal{A}_{n}(\varepsilon)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\{\max_{s\in\mathcal{S}}||\hat{\theta}_{a,s}-\theta_{a,s}||_{2}\leq\varepsilon,\min_{s\in\mathcal{S}}n_{1}(s)\geq\varepsilon n\}
we have

\displaystyle\mathinner{\!\biggl\lvert\frac{\sum_{i\in I_{1}(s)}[\Delta^{Y}(a,s,X_{i})-M_{a,s}(\hat{\theta}_{a,s},\theta_{a,s})]}{n_{1}(s)}\biggr\rvert}\biggl|\{A_{i},S_{i}\}_{i\in[n]}

\displaystyle\overset{d}{=}\mathinner{\!\biggl\lvert\frac{\sum_{i=N(s)+1}^{N(s)+n_{1}(s)}[\Delta^{Y}(a,s,X_{i}^{s})-M_{a,s}(\hat{\theta}_{a,s},\theta_{a,s})]}{n_{1}(s)}\biggr\rvert}\biggl|\{A_{i},S_{i}\}_{i\in[n]}\leq||\mathbb{P}_{n_{1}(s)}-\mathbb{P}||_{\mathcal{F}}\biggl|\{A_{i},S_{i}\}_{i\in[n]},

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}=\{\Lambda_{a,s}^{Y}(X_{i}^{s},\theta_{1})-\Lambda_{a,s}^{Y}(X_{i}^{s},\theta_{2})-M_{a,s}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}||\theta_{1}-\theta_{2}||_{2}\leq\varepsilon\}.

Therefore, for any
\delta>0
we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\mathinner{\biggl(\mathinner{\!\biggl\lvert\frac{\sum_{i\in I_{1}(s)}[\Delta^{Y}(a,s,X_{i})-M_{a,s}(\hat{\theta}_{a,s},\theta_{a,s})]}{n_{1}(s)}\biggr\rvert}\geq\delta n^{-1/2}\biggr)}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\mathinner{\biggl(\mathinner{\!\biggl\lvert\frac{\sum_{i\in I_{1}(s)}[\Delta^{Y}(a,s,X_{i})-M_{a,s}(\hat{\theta}_{a,s},\theta_{a,s})]}{n_{1}(s)}\biggr\rvert}\geq\delta n^{-1/2},\mathcal{A}_{n}(\varepsilon)\biggr)}+\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{A}_{n}^{c}(\varepsilon))

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\mathinner{\biggl[\mathbb{P}\mathinner{\biggl(\mathinner{\!\biggl\lvert\frac{\sum_{i\in I_{1}(s)}[\Delta^{Y}(a,s,X_{i})-M_{a,s}(\hat{\theta}_{a,s},\theta_{a,s})]}{n_{1}(s)}\biggr\rvert}\geq\delta n^{-1/2},\mathcal{A}_{n}(\varepsilon)\biggl|\{A_{i},S_{i}\}_{i\in[n]}\biggr)}\biggr]}+\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{A}_{n}^{c}(\varepsilon))

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{P}\left(||\mathbb{P}_{n_{1}(s)}-\mathbb{P}||_{\mathcal{F}}\geq\delta n^{-1/2}\biggl|\{A_{i},S_{i}\}_{i\in[n]}\right)1\{n_{1}(s)\geq n\varepsilon\}\right]+\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{A}_{n}^{c}(\varepsilon))

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\mathbb{E}\left\{\frac{n^{1/2}\mathbb{E}\left[||\mathbb{P}_{n_{1}(s)}-\mathbb{P}||_{\mathcal{F}}|\{A_{i},S_{i}\}_{i\in[n]}\right]1\{n_{1}(s)\geq n\varepsilon\}}{\delta}\right\}+\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{A}_{n}^{c}(\varepsilon)).

By Assumption
4
,
\mathcal{F}
is a VC-class with a fixed VC index
and envelope
L_{i}
such that
\mathbb{E}(L_{i}^{q}|\{A_{i},S_{i}\}_{i\in[n]})\leq C<\infty
. This implies
\mathbb{E}\max_{i\in[n_{1}(s)]}L_{i}^{2}\leq Cn_{1}^{2/q}(s)
. In addition,

\displaystyle\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\mathbb{P}f^{2}\leq\mathbb{E}L_{i}^{2}(\theta_{1}-\theta_{2})^{2}\leq C\varepsilon^{2}.

Invoke
Chernozhukov et al. 2014
with
A
and
\nu
being fixed constants,
and
\sigma^{2}
,
F
,
M
being
C\varepsilon^{2}
,
L
,
\max_{1\leq i\leq n_{1}(s)}L_{i}
, respectively, in our setting. We have

\displaystyle n^{1/2}\mathbb{E}\left[||\mathbb{P}_{n_{1}(s)}-\mathbb{P}||_{\mathcal{F}}|\{A_{i},S_{i}\}_{i\in[n]}\right]1\{n_{1}(s)\geq n\varepsilon\}

\displaystyle\leq C\mathinner{\biggl(\sqrt{\frac{n}{n_{1}(s)}\varepsilon^{2}\log(1/\varepsilon)}+n^{1/2}n_{1}^{1/q-1}(s)\log(1/\varepsilon)\biggr)}1\{n_{1}(s)\geq n\varepsilon\}

\displaystyle\leq C(\varepsilon^{1/2}\log^{1/2}(1/\varepsilon)+n^{1/q-1/2}\varepsilon^{1/q-1}\log(1/\varepsilon)).

Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left\{\frac{n^{1/2}\mathbb{E}\mathinner{\bigl[||\mathbb{P}_{n_{1}(s)}-\mathbb{P}||_{\mathcal{F}}|\{A_{i},S_{i}\}_{i\in[n]}\bigr]}1\{n_{1}(s)\geq n\varepsilon\}}{\delta}\right\}\leq C\mathbb{E}\left(\varepsilon^{1/2}\log^{1/2}(1/\varepsilon)+n^{1/q-1/2}\varepsilon^{1/q-1}\log(1/\varepsilon)\right)/\delta.

By letting
n\rightarrow\infty
followed by
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
, we
have

\displaystyle\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb{P}\mathinner{\biggl(\mathinner{\!\biggl\lvert\frac{\sum_{i\in I_{1}(s)}[\Delta^{Y}(a,s,X_{i})-M_{a,s}(\hat{\theta}_{a,s},\theta_{a,s})]}{n_{1}(s)}\biggr\rvert}\geq\delta n^{-1/2}\biggr)}=0,

Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathinner{\!\biggl\lvert\frac{\sum_{i\in I_{1}(s)}[\Delta^{Y}(a,s,X_{i})-M_{a,s}(\hat{\theta}_{a,s},\theta_{a,s})]}{n_{1}(s)}\biggr\rvert}=o_{p}(n^{-1/2}).

For the same reason, we have

\displaystyle\mathinner{\!\biggl\lvert\frac{\sum_{i\in I_{0}(s)}[\Delta^{Y}(a,s,X_{i})-M_{a,s}(\hat{\theta}_{a,s},\theta_{a,s})]}{n_{0}(s)}\biggr\rvert}=o_{p}(n^{-1/2}),

and (
H.1
) holds.

Step 2
. We have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Delta^{Y,2}(a,S_{i},X_{i})=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}1\{S_{i}=s\}(\Lambda_{a,s}^{Y}(X_{i},\hat{\theta}_{a,s})-\Lambda_{a,s}^{Y}(X_{i},\theta_{a,s}))^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\mathinner{\biggl(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}L_{i}^{2}\biggr)}C\max_{s\in\mathcal{S}}||\hat{\theta}_{a,s}-\theta_{a,s}||_{2}^{2}=o_{p}(1).

This verifies Assumption
3
(ii). Assumption
3
(iii)
holds by Assumption
4
(ii).

Appendix I
Proof of Theorem
5.2

Let

\displaystyle\nu^{Y}(a,S_{i},X_{i})=\mathbb{E}(Y_{i}(D_{i}(a))|S_{i},X_{i})-\mathbb{E}(Y_{i}(D_{i}(a))|S_{i})\quad\text{and}

\displaystyle\nu^{D}(a,S_{i},X_{i})=\mathbb{E}(D_{i}(a)|S_{i},X_{i})-\mathbb{E}(D_{i}(a)|S_{i}).

(I.1)

Also recall that
W_{i}=Y_{i}(D_{i}(1))
,

,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\pi(S_{i})\Xi_{1}^{2}(\mathcal{D}_{i},S_{i})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left\{\frac{\left(W_{i}-\mu^{Y}(1,S_{i},X_{i})-\tau(D_{i}(1)-\mu^{D}(1,S_{i},X_{i}))\right)^{2}}{\pi(S_{i})}\right\}

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}\biggl\{\pi(S_{i})\biggl[\frac{\nu^{Y}(1,S_{i},X_{i})-\tilde{\mu}^{Y}(1,S_{i},X_{i})-\tau(\nu^{D}(1,S_{i},X_{i})-\tilde{\mu}^{D}(1,S_{i},X_{i}))}{\pi(S_{i})}

\displaystyle+\tilde{\mu}^{Y}(1,S_{i},X_{i})-\tilde{\mu}^{Y}(0,S_{i},X_{i})-\tau(\tilde{\mu}^{D}(1,S_{i},X_{i})-\tilde{\mu}^{D}(0,S_{i},X_{i}))\biggr]^{2}\biggr\}.

Similarly, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(1-\pi(S_{i}))\Xi_{0}^{2}(\mathcal{D}_{i},S_{i})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left\{\frac{\left(Z_{i}-\mu^{Y}(0,S_{i},X_{i})-\tau(D_{i}(0)-\mu^{D}(0,S_{i},X_{i}))\right)^{2}}{1-\pi(S_{i})}\right\}

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}\biggl\{(1-\pi(S_{i}))\biggl[\frac{\nu^{Y}(0,S_{i},X_{i})-\tilde{\mu}^{Y}(0,S_{i},X_{i})-\tau(\nu^{D}(0,S_{i},X_{i})-\tilde{\mu}^{D}(0,S_{i},X_{i}))}{1-\pi(S_{i})}

\displaystyle-\left(\tilde{\mu}^{Y}(1,S_{i},X_{i})-\tilde{\mu}^{Y}(0,S_{i},X_{i})-\tau(\tilde{\mu}^{D}(1,S_{i},X_{i})-\tilde{\mu}^{D}(0,S_{i},X_{i}))\right)\biggr]^{2}\biggr\}.

Last, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Xi_{2}^{2}(S_{i})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}(\mu^{Y}(1,S_{i},X_{i})-\mu^{Y}(0,S_{i},X_{i})-\tau(\mu^{D}(1,S_{i},X_{i})-\mu^{D}(0,S_{i},X_{i})))^{2}

\displaystyle-\mathbb{E}(\nu^{Y}(1,S_{i},X_{i})-\nu^{Y}(0,S_{i},X_{i})-\tau(\nu^{D}(1,S_{i},X_{i})-\nu^{D}(0,S_{i},X_{i})))^{2}

Let

\displaystyle\sigma_{\ast}^{2}

\displaystyle=(\mathbb{P}(D_{i}(1)>D_{i}(0)))^{-2}\biggl\{\mathbb{E}\mathinner{\biggl[\frac{\mathinner{\bigl(W_{i}-\mu^{Y}(1,S_{i},X_{i})-\tau(D_{i}(1)-\mu^{D}(1,S_{i},X_{i}))\bigr)}^{2}}{\pi(S_{i})}\biggr]}

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}\mathinner{\biggl[\frac{\mathinner{\bigl(Z_{i}-\mu^{Y}(0,S_{i},X_{i})-\tau[D_{i}(0)-\mu^{D}(0,S_{i},X_{i})]\bigr)}^{2}}{1-\pi(S_{i})}\biggr]}

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}\mathinner{\Bigl(\mu^{Y}(1,S_{i},X_{i})-\mu^{Y}(0,S_{i},X_{i})-\tau[\mu^{D}(1,S_{i},X_{i})-\mu^{D}(0,S_{i},X_{i})]\Bigr)}^{2}\biggr\},

which does not depend on the working models
\overline{\mu}^{b}(a,S_{i},X_{i})
for
a=0,1
and
b=D,Y
.
Then, we have

\displaystyle\sigma^{2}((t_{a,s},b_{a,s})_{a=0,1,s\in\mathcal{S}})=\frac{\sigma_{*}^{2}+V((t_{a,s},b_{a,s})_{a=0,1,s\in\mathcal{S}})}{\mathbb{P}(D_{i}(1)>D_{i}(0))^{2}},

where
\sigma_{*}^{2}
does not depend on
(t_{a,s},b_{a,s})_{a=0,1,s\in\mathcal{S}}
and

\displaystyle V((t_{a,s},b_{a,s})_{a=0,1,s\in\mathcal{S}})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\mathinner{\biggl(\sqrt{\frac{\pi(S_{i})}{1-\pi(S_{i})}}A_{0}(S_{i},X_{i})+\sqrt{\frac{1-\pi(S_{i})}{\pi(S_{i})}}A_{1}(S_{i},X_{i})\biggr)}^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}p(s)\mathbb{E}\mathinner{\biggl[\mathinner{\biggl(\sqrt{\frac{\pi(s)}{1-\pi(s)}}A_{0}(s,X_{i})+\sqrt{\frac{1-\pi(s)}{\pi(s)}}A_{1}(s,X_{i})\biggr)}^{2}\biggl|S_{i}=s\biggr]}

where for
a=0,1
,

\displaystyle A_{a}(s,x)

\displaystyle=\nu^{Y}(a,s,x)-\tilde{\mu}^{Y}(a,s,x)-\tau(\nu^{D}(a,s,x)-\tilde{\mu}^{D}(a,s,x))

\displaystyle=(\nu^{Y}(a,s,x)-\tau\nu^{D}(a,s,x))-\tilde{\Psi}_{i,s}^{\top}(t_{a,s}-\tau b_{a,s}),

and
(\tilde{\mu}^{Y}(a,s,x),\tilde{\mu}^{D}(a,s,x))
and
(\nu^{Y}(a,s,x),\nu^{D}(a,s,x))
are defined in (
3.5
) and
(
I.1
), respectively. Specifically, we have

\displaystyle\tilde{\mu}^{Y}(a,s,x)=\tilde{\Psi}_{i,s}^{\top}t_{a,s},\quad\tilde{\mu}^{D}(a,s,x)=\tilde{\Psi}_{i,s}^{\top}b_{a,s},\quad\text{and}\quad\tilde{\Psi}_{i,s}=\Psi_{i,s}-\mathbb{E}(\Psi_{i,s}|S_{i}=s).

In order to minimize
V((t_{a,s},b_{a,s})_{a=0,1,s\in\mathcal{S}})
, it
suffices to minimize

\mathbb{E}\mathinner{\biggl[\mathinner{\biggl(\sqrt{\frac{\pi(s)}{1-\pi(s)}}A_{0}(s,X_{i})+\sqrt{\frac{1-\pi(s)}{\pi(s)}}A_{1}(s,X_{i})\biggr)}^{2}\biggl|S_{i}=s\biggr]}

for each
s\in\mathcal{S}
. In addition, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\mathinner{\biggl[\mathinner{\biggl(\sqrt{\frac{\pi(s)}{1-\pi(s)}}A_{0}(s,X_{i})+\sqrt{\frac{1-\pi(s)}{\pi(s)}}A_{1}(s,X_{i})\biggr)}^{2}\biggl|S_{i}=s\biggr]}=\mathbb{E}\left((\overline{y}_{i,s}-\tilde{\Psi}_{i,s}^{\top}\gamma_{s})^{2}\biggl|S_{i}=s\right),

where

\displaystyle\overline{y}_{i,s}=\sqrt{\frac{1-\pi(s)}{\pi(s)}}(\nu^{Y}(1,s,X_{i})-\tau\nu^{D}(1,s,X_{i}))+\sqrt{\frac{\pi(s)}{1-\pi(s)}}(\nu^{Y}(0,s,X_{i})-\tau\nu^{D}(0,s,X_{i}))

and

\displaystyle\gamma_{s}=\sqrt{\frac{1-\pi(s)}{\pi(s)}}(t_{1,s}-\tau b_{1,s})+\sqrt{\frac{\pi(s)}{1-\pi(s)}}(t_{0,s}-\tau b_{0,s}).

By solving the first order condition, we find that

\displaystyle\Theta^{*}

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}&(\theta_{a,s}^{*},\beta_{a,s}^{*})_{a=0,1,s\in\mathcal{S}}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\\
&\sqrt{\frac{1-\pi(s)}{\pi(s)}}(\theta_{1,s}^{*}-\tau\beta_{1,s}^{*})+\sqrt{\frac{\pi(s)}{1-\pi(s)}}(\theta_{0,s}^{*}-\tau\beta_{0,s}^{*})=\mathbb{E}(\tilde{\Psi}_{i,s}\tilde{\Psi}_{i,s}^{\top}|S_{i}=s)^{-1}\mathbb{E}(\tilde{\Psi}_{i,s}\overline{y}_{i,s}|S_{i}=s)\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}&(\theta_{a,s}^{*},\beta_{a,s}^{*})_{a=0,1,s\in\mathcal{S}}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\\
&\sqrt{\frac{1-\pi(s)}{\pi(s)}}(\theta_{1,s}^{*}-\tau\beta_{1,s}^{*})+\sqrt{\frac{\pi(s)}{1-\pi(s)}}(\theta_{0,s}^{*}-\tau\beta_{0,s}^{*})\\
&=\sqrt{\frac{1-\pi(s)}{\pi(s)}}(\theta_{1,s}^{L}-\tau\beta_{1,s}^{L})+\sqrt{\frac{\pi(s)}{1-\pi(s)}}(\theta_{0,s}^{L}-\tau\beta_{0,s}^{L}).\end{pmatrix},

where

\displaystyle\theta_{a,s}^{L}

\displaystyle=[\mathbb{E}(\tilde{\Psi}_{i,s}\tilde{\Psi}_{i,s}^{\top}|S_{i}=s)]^{-1}[\mathbb{E}(\tilde{\Psi}_{i,s}\nu^{Y}(a,s,X_{i})|S_{i}=s)]

\displaystyle=[\mathbb{E}(\tilde{\Psi}_{i,s}\tilde{\Psi}_{i,s}^{\top}|S_{i}=s)]^{-1}[\mathbb{E}(\tilde{\Psi}_{i,s}\mathbb{E}(Y_{i}(D_{i}(a))|S_{i},X_{i})|S_{i}=s)]

\displaystyle=[\mathbb{E}(\tilde{\Psi}_{i,s}\tilde{\Psi}_{i,s}^{\top}|S_{i}=s)]^{-1}[\mathbb{E}(\tilde{\Psi}_{i,s}Y_{i}(D_{i}(a))|S_{i}=s)].

Similarly, we have

\displaystyle\beta_{a,s}^{L}

\displaystyle=[\mathbb{E}(\tilde{\Psi}_{i,s}\tilde{\Psi}_{i,s}^{\top}|S_{i}=s)]^{-1}[\mathbb{E}(\tilde{\Psi}_{i,s}D_{i}(a)|S_{i}=s)].

This concludes the proof.

Appendix J
Proof of Theorem
5.3

In order to verify Assumption
3
, by Theorem
5.1
, it
suffices to show that
\hat{\theta}_{a,s}^{L}\overset{p}{\longrightarrow}\theta_{a,s}^{L}
and
\hat{\beta}_{a,s}^{L}\overset{p}{\longrightarrow}\beta_{a,s}^{L}
. We focus on the former with
a=1
. Let
\{W_{i}^{s},X_{i}^{s}\}_{i\in[n]}
be generated independently from the joint
distribution of
(Y_{i}(D_{i}(1)),X_{i})
given
S_{i}=s
and denote

\Psi_{i,s}^{s}=\Psi_{s}(X_{i}^{s})
,
\tilde{\Psi}_{i,s}^{s}=\Psi_{s}(X_{i}^{s})-\mathbb{E}\Psi_{s}(X_{i}^{s})
,
\dot{\Psi}_{i,1,s}^{s}=\Psi_{s}(X_{i}^{s})-\frac{1}{n_{1}(s)}\sum_{i=N(s)+1}^{N(s)+n_{1}(s)}\Psi_{s}(X_{i}^{s})
, and
\dot{\Psi}_{i,0,s}^{s}=\Psi_{s}(X_{i}^{s})-\frac{1}{n_{0}(s)}\sum_{i=N(s)+n_{1}(s)+1}^{N(s)+n(s)}\Psi_{s}(X_{i}^{s})
.
Then, we have

\displaystyle\hat{\theta}_{1,s}^{L}\overset{d}{=}\mathinner{\biggl(\frac{1}{n_{1}(s)}\sum_{i=N(s)+1}^{N(s)+n_{1}(s)}\dot{\Psi}_{i,1,s}^{s}\dot{\Psi}_{i,1,s}^{s,\top}\biggr)}^{-1}\mathinner{\biggl(\frac{1}{n_{1}(s)}\sum_{i=N(s)+1}^{N(s)+n_{1}(s)}\dot{\Psi}_{i,1,s}^{s}W_{i}^{s}\biggr)}.

As
\frac{1}{n_{1}(s)}\sum_{i=N(s)+1}^{N(s)+n_{1}(s)}\Psi_{i,s}^{s}\overset{p}{\longrightarrow}\mathbb{E}\Psi_{i,s}^{s}=\mathbb{E}(\Psi_{s}(X_{i}^{s}))=\mathbb{E}(\Psi_{s}(X_{i})|S_{i}=s)
by the standard LLN,
we have

\displaystyle\mathinner{\biggl(\frac{1}{n_{1}(s)}\sum_{i=N(s)+1}^{N(s)+n_{1}(s)}\dot{\Psi}_{i,1,s}^{s}\dot{\Psi}_{i,1,s}^{s,\top}\biggr)}=\mathinner{\biggl(\frac{1}{n_{1}(s)}\sum_{i=N(s)+1}^{N(s)+n_{1}(s)}\tilde{\Psi}_{i,s}^{s}\tilde{\Psi}_{i,s}^{s,\top}\biggr)}+o_{p}(1),

\displaystyle\mathinner{\biggl(\frac{1}{n_{1}(s)}\sum_{i=N(s)+1}^{N(s)+n_{1}(s)}\dot{\Psi}_{i,1,s}^{s}W_{i}^{s}\biggr)}=\mathinner{\biggl(\frac{1}{n_{1}(s)}\sum_{i=N(s)+1}^{N(s)+n_{1}(s)}\tilde{\Psi}_{i,s}^{s}W_{i}^{s}\biggr)}+o_{p}(1).

In addition, by the standard LLN,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n_{1}(s)}\sum_{i=N(s)+1}^{N(s)+n_{1}(s)}\tilde{\Psi}_{i,s}^{s}\tilde{\Psi}_{i,s}^{s,\top}\overset{p}{\longrightarrow}\mathbb{E}\tilde{\Psi}_{i,s}^{s}\tilde{\Psi}_{i,s}^{s,\top}=\mathbb{E}(\tilde{\Psi}_{i,s}\tilde{\Psi}_{i,s}^{\top}|S_{i}=s),

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n_{1}(s)}\sum_{i=N(s)+1}^{N(s)+n_{1}(s)}\tilde{\Psi}_{i,s}^{s}W_{i}^{s}\overset{p}{\longrightarrow}\mathbb{E}\tilde{\Psi}_{i,s}^{s}W_{i}^{s}=\mathbb{E}(\tilde{\Psi}_{i,s}Y_{i}(D_{i}(1))|S_{i}=s).

Last, Assumption
5
implies
\mathbb{E}(\tilde{\Psi}_{i,s}\tilde{\Psi}_{i,s}^{\top}|S_{i}=s)
is invertible, this means

\displaystyle\hat{\theta}_{1,s}^{L}\overset{p}{\longrightarrow}\left[\mathbb{E}(\tilde{\Psi}_{i,s}\tilde{\Psi}_{i,s}^{\top}|S_{i}=s)\right]^{-1}\mathbb{E}(\tilde{\Psi}_{i,s}Y_{i}(D_{i}(1))|S_{i}=s)=\theta_{1,s}^{L}.

Similarly, we can show that
\hat{\theta}_{0,s}^{L}\overset{p}{\longrightarrow}\theta_{0,s}^{L}
and
\hat{\beta}_{a,s}^{L}\overset{p}{\longrightarrow}\beta_{a,s}^{L}
for
a=0,1
and
s\in\mathcal{S}
. Therefore, Assumption
3
holds, and thus, all
the results in Theorem
3.1
hold for
\hat{\tau}_{L}
. Then, the
optimality result in the second half of Theorem
5.3
is a direct
consequence of Theorem
5.2
.

Last, we compare the asymptotic variances of TSLS estimator and the estimator with the optimal linear adjustment with
\pi(s)=\pi
for
s\in\mathcal{S}
and
\Psi_{i,s}=X_{i}
. In this special case, we first note that the asymptotic variance of the estimator with the optimal linear adjustment is

\displaystyle\frac{\sigma_{1}^{2}+\sigma_{0}^{2}+\sigma_{2}^{2}}{[\mathbb{E}(D(1)-D(0))]^{2}},

where

\displaystyle\sigma_{0}^{2}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}(1-\pi)\Xi_{0}^{2}(\mathcal{D}_{i},S_{i})

\displaystyle\Xi_{0}(\mathcal{D}_{i},S_{i})

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\left[\left(\frac{1}{1-\pi}-1\right)\tilde{X}_{i}^{\top}\theta_{0s}+\tilde{X}_{i}^{\top}\theta_{1s}-\frac{\tilde{Z}_{i}}{1-\pi}\right]-\tau\left[\left(\frac{1}{1-\pi}-1\right)\tilde{X}_{i}^{\top}\beta_{0s}+\tilde{X}_{i}^{\top}\beta_{1s}-\frac{\tilde{D}_{i}(0)}{1-\pi}\right]

\displaystyle\sigma_{1}^{2}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\pi\Xi_{1}^{2}(\mathcal{D}_{i},S_{i}),

\displaystyle\Xi_{1}(\mathcal{D}_{i},S_{i})

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\left[\left(1-\frac{1}{\pi}\right)\tilde{X}_{i}^{\top}\theta_{1s}-\tilde{X}_{i}^{\top}\theta_{0s}+\frac{\tilde{W}_{i}}{\pi}\right]-\tau\left[\left(1-\frac{1}{\pi}\right)\tilde{X}_{i}^{\top}\beta_{1s}-\tilde{X}_{i}^{\top}\beta_{0s}+\frac{\tilde{D}_{i}(1)}{\pi}\right],

\displaystyle\sigma_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\mathinner{\bigl[Y(D(1))-Y(D(0))-(D(1)-D(0))\tau|S_{i}\bigr]}\right]^{2},

with

\displaystyle\theta_{as}

\displaystyle=\left[\mathbb{E}(\tilde{X}_{is}\tilde{X}_{is}^{\top}|S_{i}=s)\right]^{-1}\left[\mathbb{E}(\tilde{X}_{is}Y_{i}(D_{i}(a))|S_{i}=s)\right]\quad\text{and}

\displaystyle\beta_{as}

\displaystyle=\left[\mathbb{E}(\tilde{X}_{is}\tilde{X}_{is}^{\top}|S_{i}=s)\right]^{-1}\left[\mathbb{E}(\tilde{X}_{is}D_{i}(a)|S_{i}=s)\right],\quad a=0,1.

(J.1)

Observe that
\sigma_{TSLS,1}^{2}+\sigma_{TSLS,0}^{2}
can also be written as

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\pi(S_{i})\Xi_{1}(\mathcal{D}_{i},S_{i})^{2}+(1-\pi(S_{i}))\Xi_{0}(\mathcal{D}_{i},S_{i})^{2}\right],

(J.2)

where

\displaystyle\Xi_{1}(\mathcal{D}_{i},S_{i})

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\left[\left(1-\frac{1}{\pi(S_{i})}\right)\tilde{X}_{i}^{\top}\theta_{1s}-\tilde{X}_{i}^{\top}\theta_{0s}+\frac{\tilde{W}_{i}}{\pi(S_{i})}\right]

\displaystyle-\tau\left[\left(1-\frac{1}{\pi(S_{i})}\right)\tilde{X}_{i}^{\top}\beta_{1s}-\tilde{X}_{i}^{\top}\beta_{0s}+\frac{\tilde{D}_{i}(1)}{\pi(S_{i})}\right],

\displaystyle\Xi_{0}(\mathcal{D}_{i},S_{i})

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\left[\left(\frac{1}{1-\pi(S_{i})}-1\right)\tilde{X}_{i}^{\top}\theta_{0s}+\tilde{X}_{i}^{\top}\theta_{1s}-\frac{\tilde{Z}_{i}}{1-\pi(S_{i})}\right]

\displaystyle-\tau\left[\left(\frac{1}{1-\pi(S_{i})}-1\right)\tilde{X}_{i}^{\top}\beta_{0s}+\tilde{X}_{i}^{\top}\beta_{1s}-\frac{\tilde{D}_{i}(0)}{1-\pi(S_{i})}\right],

with

\theta_{1s}=\theta_{0s}
,
\beta_{1s}=\beta_{0s}
and
\theta_{1s}-\tau\beta_{1s}=\lambda_{x}^{*}
, where
\lambda_{x}^{*}
is the first
d_{x}
coefficients of
\lambda^{*}
defined in Theorem
2.1
where
d_{x}
is the dimension of
X_{i}
. By Theorem
5.2
, we achieve the optimal linear adjustment when
\theta_{a,s}
and
\beta_{a,s}
satisfy (
J.1
), which implies

\displaystyle\sigma_{1}^{2}+\sigma_{0}^{2}\leq\sigma_{TSLS,1}^{2}+\sigma_{TSLS,0}^{2}.

In addition, we have
\sigma_{2}^{2}=\sigma_{TSLS,2}^{2}
and
0\leq\sigma_{TSLS,3}^{2}
,
which implies the desired result.

Appendix K
Proof of Theorem
5.4

Let
\{D_{i}^{s}(1),X_{i}^{s}\}_{i\in[n]}
be generated independently from
the joint distribution of
(D_{i}(1),X_{i})
given
S_{i}=s
,
\Psi_{i,s}^{s}=\Psi_{s}(X_{i}^{s})
, and
\mathring{\Psi}_{i,s}^{s}=(1,\Psi_{i,s}^{s,\top})^{\top}
. Then, we have, pointwise in
b
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n_{1}(s)}\sum_{i\in I_{1}(s)}\left[D_{i}\log(\lambda(\mathring{\Psi}_{i,s}^{\top}b))+(1-D_{i})\log(1-\lambda(\mathring{\Psi}_{i,s}^{\top}b))\right]

\displaystyle\overset{d}{=}\frac{1}{n_{1}(s)}\sum_{i=N(s)+1}^{N(s)+n_{1}(s)}\left[D_{i}^{s}(1)\log(\lambda(\mathring{\Psi}_{i,s}^{s,\top}b))+(1-D_{i}^{s}(1))\log(1-\lambda(\mathring{\Psi}_{i,s}^{s,\top}b))\right]

\displaystyle\overset{p}{\longrightarrow}\mathbb{E}\left[D_{i}^{s}(1)\log(\lambda(\mathring{\Psi}_{i,s}^{s,\top}b))+(1-D_{i}^{s}(1))\log(1-\lambda(\mathring{\Psi}_{i,s}^{s,\top}b))\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[D_{i}(1)\log(\lambda(\mathring{\Psi}_{i,s}^{\top}b))+(1-D_{i}(1))\log(1-\lambda(\mathring{\Psi}_{i,s}^{\top}b))|S_{i}=s\right].

As the logistic likelihood function is concave in
b
, the pointwise
convergence in
b
implies uniform convergence, i.e.,

\displaystyle\sup_{b}\biggl|

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n_{1}(s)}\sum_{i\in I_{1}(s)}\left[D_{i}\log(\lambda(\mathring{\Psi}_{i,s}^{\top}b))+(1-D_{i})\log(1-\lambda(\mathring{\Psi}_{i,s}^{\top}b))\right]

\displaystyle-\mathbb{E}\left[D_{i}(1)\log(\lambda(\mathring{\Psi}_{i,s}^{\top}b))+(1-D_{i}(1))\log(1-\lambda(\mathring{\Psi}_{i,s}^{\top}b))|S_{i}=s\right]\biggr|\overset{p}{\longrightarrow}0.

Then, by the standard proof for the extremum estimation, we have
\hat{\beta}_{a,s}^{MLE}\overset{p}{\longrightarrow}\beta_{a,s}^{MLE}
. Similarly, we
can show that
\hat{\theta}_{a,s}^{OLS}\overset{p}{\longrightarrow}\theta_{a,s}^{OLS}
. The verifies Assumption
4
(i). Assumptions

4
(ii) and
4
(iii) follow from Assumption

6
(ii). Then, the desired results hold due to Theorem

5.1
.

Appendix L
Proof of Theorem
5.5

We note that the adjustments proposed in Theorem
5.5
are still
parametric. Specifically, we have

\displaystyle\overline{\mu}^{Y}(a,s,X_{i})=\Lambda_{a,s}^{Y}(X_{i},\{\beta_{1,s}^{MLE},\beta_{0,s}^{MLE},\theta_{a,s}^{F}\}),

\displaystyle\overline{\mu}^{D}(a,s,X_{i})=\Lambda_{a,s}^{D}(X_{i},\{\beta_{1,s}^{MLE},\beta_{0,s}^{MLE},\beta_{a,s}^{F}\}),

\displaystyle\hat{\mu}^{Y}(a,s,X_{i})=\Lambda_{a,s}^{Y}(X_{i},\{\hat{\beta}_{1,s}^{MLE},\hat{\beta}_{0,s}^{MLE},\hat{\theta}_{a,s}^{F}\}),\quad\text{and}

\displaystyle\hat{\mu}^{D}(a,s,X_{i})=\Lambda_{a,s}^{D}(X_{i},\{\hat{\beta}_{1,s}^{MLE},\hat{\beta}_{0,s}^{MLE},\hat{\beta}_{a,s}^{F}\}),

where

\displaystyle\Lambda_{a,s}^{Y}(X_{i},\{b_{1},b_{0},t_{a}^{*}\})=\begin{pmatrix}\Psi_{i,s}^{\top}\\
\lambda(\mathring{\Psi}_{i,s}^{\top}b_{1})\\
\lambda(\mathring{\Psi}_{i,s}^{\top}b_{0})\end{pmatrix}^{\top}t_{a}^{*}\quad\text{and}\quad\Lambda_{a,s}^{D}(X_{i},\{b_{1},b_{0},b_{a}^{*}\})=\begin{pmatrix}\Psi_{i,s}^{\top}\\
\lambda(\mathring{\Psi}_{i,s}^{\top}b_{1})\\
\lambda(\mathring{\Psi}_{i,s}^{\top}b_{0})\end{pmatrix}^{\top}b_{a}^{*}.

Therefore, in view of Theorem
5.1
, to verify Assumption

3
, it suffices to show that
\hat{\theta}_{a,s}^{F}\overset{p}{\longrightarrow}\theta_{a,s}^{F}
and
\hat{\beta}_{a,s}^{F}\overset{p}{\longrightarrow}\beta_{a,s}^{F}
, as we have already shown the
consistency of
\hat{\beta}_{a,s}^{MLE}
in the proof of Theorem

5.4
. We focus on
\hat{\theta}_{a,s}^{F}
. Define
\dot{\Phi}_{i,a,s}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\Phi_{i,s}-\frac{1}{n_{a}(s)}\sum_{i\in I_{a}(s)}\Phi_{i,s}
,
where

\displaystyle\Phi_{i,s}=\begin{pmatrix}\Psi_{i,s}\\
\lambda(\mathring{\Psi}_{i,s}^{\top}\beta_{1,s}^{MLE})\\
\lambda(\mathring{\Psi}_{i,s}^{\top}\beta_{0,s}^{MLE})\end{pmatrix}.

We first show that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n_{a}(s)}\sum_{i\in I_{a}(s)}\breve{\Phi}_{i,a,s}\breve{\Phi}_{i,a,s}^{\top}=\frac{1}{n_{a}(s)}\sum_{i\in I_{a}(s)}\dot{\Phi}_{i,a,s}\dot{\Phi}_{i,a,s}^{\top}+o_{p}(1).

(L.1)

Let
v,u\in\Re^{d_{\Psi}+2}
be two arbitrary vectors such that

||u||_{2}=||v||_{2}=1
. Then, we have

\displaystyle\mathinner{\!\biggl\lvert v^{\top}\mathinner{\biggl[\frac{1}{n_{a}(s)}\sum_{i\in I_{a}(s)}\left(\breve{\Phi}_{i,a,s}\breve{\Phi}_{i,a,s}^{\top}-\dot{\Phi}_{i,a,s}\dot{\Phi}_{i,a,s}^{\top}\right)\biggr]}u\biggr\rvert}

\displaystyle=\mathinner{\!\biggl\lvert\frac{1}{n_{a}(s)}\sum_{i\in I_{a}(s)}\left[(v^{\top}\breve{\Phi}_{i,a,s})(u^{\top}\breve{\Phi}_{i,a,s})-(v^{\top}\dot{\Phi}_{i,a,s})(u^{\top}\dot{\Phi}_{i,a,s})\right]\biggr\rvert}

\displaystyle=\mathinner{\!\biggl\lvert\frac{1}{n_{a}(s)}\sum_{i\in I_{a}(s)}\left[v^{\top}(\breve{\Phi}_{i,a,s}-\dot{\Phi}_{i,a,s})(u^{\top}\breve{\Phi}_{i,a,s})+(v^{\top}\dot{\Phi}_{i,a,s})u^{\top}(\breve{\Phi}_{i,a,s}-\dot{\Phi}_{i,a,s})\right]\biggr\rvert}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n_{a}(s)}\sum_{i\in I_{a}(s)}||\breve{\Phi}_{i,a,s}-\dot{\Phi}_{i,a,s}||_{2}(||\breve{\Phi}_{i,a,s}||_{2}+||\dot{\Phi}_{i,a,s}||_{2})

(L.2)

where the first inequality is due to Cauchy-Schwarz inequality. We now show
(
L.2
) is
o_{p}(1)
. First note that

\displaystyle||\breve{\Phi}_{i,a,s}-\dot{\Phi}_{i,a,s}||_{2}\leq\sum_{a^{\prime}=0,1}\|B_{a^{\prime}}\|_{2}

where

\displaystyle B_{a^{\prime}}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\lambda(\mathring{\Psi}_{i,s}^{\top}\hat{\beta}_{a^{\prime},s}^{MLE})-\lambda(\mathring{\Psi}_{i,s}^{\top}\beta_{a^{\prime},s}^{MLE})-\frac{1}{n_{a}(s)}\sum_{i\in I_{a}(s)}\mathinner{\bigl[\lambda(\mathring{\Psi}_{i,s}^{\top}\hat{\beta}_{a^{\prime},s}^{MLE})-\lambda(\mathring{\Psi}_{i,s}^{\top}\beta_{a^{\prime},s}^{MLE})\bigr]}.

Note that

\displaystyle\lambda(\mathring{\Psi}_{i,s}^{\top}\hat{\beta}_{a^{\prime},s}^{MLE})-\lambda(\mathring{\Psi}_{i,s}^{\top}\beta_{a^{\prime},s}^{MLE})

\displaystyle=\frac{\partial\lambda(\mathring{\Psi}_{i,s}^{\top}\tilde{\beta}_{a^{\prime},s}^{MLE})}{\partial\beta_{a^{\prime},s}}(\hat{\beta}_{a^{\prime},s}^{MLE}-\beta_{a^{\prime},s}^{MLE})

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n_{a}(s)}\sum_{i\in I_{a}(s)}\lambda(\mathring{\Psi}_{i,s}^{\top}\hat{\beta}_{a^{\prime},s}^{MLE})-\lambda(\mathring{\Psi}_{i,s}^{\top}\beta_{a^{\prime},s}^{MLE})

\displaystyle=\mathinner{\biggl[\frac{1}{n_{a}(s)}\sum_{i\in I_{a}(s)}\frac{\partial\lambda(\mathring{\Psi}_{i,s}^{\top}\tilde{\beta}_{a^{\prime},s}^{MLE})}{\partial\beta_{a^{\prime},s}}\biggr]}(\hat{\beta}_{a^{\prime},s}^{MLE}-\beta_{a^{\prime},s}^{MLE})

where
\tilde{\beta}_{a^{\prime},s}^{MLE}
is a mid-point of
\hat{\beta}_{a^{\prime},s}^{MLE}
and
\beta_{a^{\prime},s}^{MLE}
. Hence

\displaystyle\|B_{a^{\prime}}\|_{2}=\mathinner{\!\biggl\lVert\frac{\partial\lambda(\mathring{\Psi}_{i,s}^{\top}\tilde{\beta}_{a^{\prime},s}^{MLE})}{\partial\beta_{a^{\prime},s}}-\frac{1}{n_{a}(s)}\sum_{i\in I_{a}(s)}\frac{\partial\lambda(\mathring{\Psi}_{i,s}^{\top}\tilde{\beta}_{a^{\prime},s}^{MLE})}{\partial\beta_{a^{\prime},s}}\biggr\rVert}_{2}\|\hat{\beta}_{a^{\prime},s}^{MLE}-\beta_{a^{\prime},s}^{MLE}\|_{2}.

Since
\partial\lambda(u)/\partial u\leq 1
,

\displaystyle\mathinner{\!\biggl\lVert\frac{\partial\lambda(\mathring{\Psi}_{i,s}^{\top}\tilde{\beta}_{a^{\prime},s}^{MLE})}{\partial\beta_{a^{\prime},s}}\biggr\rVert}_{2}=\mathinner{\!\biggl\lVert\left.\frac{\partial\lambda(u)}{\partial u}\right|_{u=\mathring{\Psi}_{i,s}^{\top}\tilde{\beta}_{a^{\prime},s}^{MLE}}\cdot\mathring{\Psi}_{i,s}^{\top}\biggr\rVert}_{2}\leq\|\mathring{\Psi}_{i,s}\|_{2}.

Thus,

\displaystyle\|B_{a^{\prime}}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\mathinner{\biggl(\|\mathring{\Psi}_{i,s}\|_{2}+\frac{1}{n_{a}(s)}\sum_{i\in I_{a}(s)}\|\mathring{\Psi}_{i,s}\|_{2}\biggr)}\|\hat{\beta}_{a^{\prime},s}^{MLE}-\beta_{a^{\prime},s}^{MLE}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\mathinner{\biggl(2+\|\Psi_{i,s}\|_{2}+\frac{1}{n_{a}(s)}\sum_{i\in I_{a}(s)}\|\Psi_{i,s}\|_{2}\biggr)}\|\hat{\beta}_{a^{\prime},s}^{MLE}-\beta_{a^{\prime},s}^{MLE}\|_{2},

\displaystyle||\breve{\Phi}_{i,a,s}-\dot{\Phi}_{i,a,s}||_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\mathinner{\biggl(2+\|\Psi_{i,s}\|_{2}+\frac{1}{n_{a}(s)}\cdot\sum_{i\in I_{a}(s)}\|\Psi_{i,s}\|_{2}\biggr)}\sum_{a^{\prime}=0,1}\|\hat{\beta}_{a^{\prime},s}^{MLE}-\beta_{a^{\prime},s}^{MLE}\|_{2}.

(L.3)

Moreover, we can show

\displaystyle||\breve{\Phi}_{i,a,s}||_{2}+||\dot{\Phi}_{i,a,s}||_{2}\leq 2\mathinner{\biggl(4+||\Psi_{i,s}||_{2}+\frac{1}{n_{a}(s)}\sum_{i\in I_{a}(s)}\|\Psi_{i,s}\|_{2}\biggr)}.

(L.4)

Substituting (
L.3
), (
L.4
) and the fact that
||\hat{\beta}_{a,s}^{MLE}-\beta_{a,s}^{MLE}||_{2}=o_{p}(1)
into (
L.2
), we show
that (
L.2
) is
o_{p}(1)
. As it holds for arbitrary
u,v
, it implies
(
L.1
). Similarly, we can show that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n_{a}(s)}\sum_{i\in I_{a}(s)}\breve{\Phi}_{i,a,s}Y_{i}=\frac{1}{n_{a}(s)}\sum_{i\in I_{a}(s)}\dot{\Phi}_{i,a,s}Y_{i}+o_{p}(1).

(L.5)

Following the same argument in the proof of Theorem
5.3
, we can
show that

\displaystyle\mathinner{\biggl[\frac{1}{n_{a}(s)}\sum_{i\in I_{a}(s)}\dot{\Phi}_{i,a,s}\dot{\Phi}_{i,a,s}^{\top}\biggr]}^{-1}\mathinner{\biggl[\frac{1}{n_{a}(s)}\sum_{i\in I_{a}(s)}\dot{\Phi}_{i,a,s}Y_{i}\biggr]}\overset{p}{\longrightarrow}\theta_{a,s}^{F}.

In addition, by Assumption
7
, with probability approaching
one, there exists a constant
c>0
such that

\displaystyle\lambda_{\min}\mathinner{\biggl(\frac{1}{n_{a}(s)}\sum_{i\in I_{a}(s)}\dot{\Phi}_{i,a,s}\dot{\Phi}_{i,a,s}^{\top}\biggr)}\geq c.

(L.6)

Combining (
L.1
), (
L.5
), and (
L.6
), we can show that

\displaystyle\hat{\theta}_{a,s}^{F}

\displaystyle=\mathinner{\biggl[\frac{1}{n_{a}(s)}\sum_{i\in I_{a}(s)}\breve{\Phi}_{i,a,s}\breve{\Phi}_{i,a,s}^{\top}\biggr]}^{-1}\mathinner{\biggl[\frac{1}{n_{a}(s)}\sum_{i\in I_{a}(s)}\breve{\Phi}_{i,a,s}Y_{i}\biggr]}

\displaystyle=\mathinner{\biggl[\frac{1}{n_{a}(s)}\sum_{i\in I_{a}(s)}\dot{\Phi}_{i,a,s}\dot{\Phi}_{i,a,s}^{\top}\biggr]}^{-1}\mathinner{\biggl[\frac{1}{n_{a}(s)}\sum_{i\in I_{a}(s)}\dot{\Phi}_{i,a,s}Y_{i}\biggr]}+o_{p}(1)\overset{p}{\longrightarrow}\theta_{a,s}^{F}.

Similarly, we have
\hat{\beta}_{a,s}^{F}\overset{p}{\longrightarrow}\beta_{a,s}^{F}
, which implies all the results in Theorem
3.1
hold
for
\hat{\tau}_{F}
. The optimality result in the second half of the theorem
is a direct consequence of Theorem
5.2
.

Appendix M
Proof of Theorem
5.6

We focus on verifying Assumption
3
for
\hat{\mu}^{D}(a,s,X_{i})
. The proof for
\hat{\mu}^{Y}(a,s,X_{i})
is similar and hence
omitted. Following the proof of Theorem
5.4
, we note that, for
each
a=0,1
and
s\in\mathcal{S}
, the data in cell
I_{a}(s)
, denoted

\{D_{i}^{s}(a),X_{i}^{s}\}_{i\in[n]}
, can be viewed as i.i.d. following the
joint distribution of
(D_{i}(a),X_{i})
given
S_{i}=s
conditionally on

\{A_{i},S_{i}\}_{i\in[n]}
. Then following the standard logistic sieve
regression in
Hirano
et al. 2003
, we have

\displaystyle\max_{a=0,1,s\in\mathcal{S}}||\hat{\beta}_{a,s}^{NP}-\beta_{a,s}^{NP}||_{2}=O_{p}\left(\sqrt{h_{n}/n_{a}(s)}\right).

Then we have

\displaystyle\biggl|\frac{\sum_{i\in I_{1}(s)}\Delta^{D}(a,s,X_{i})}{n_{1}(s)}-\frac{\sum_{i\in I_{0}(s)}\Delta^{D}(a,s,X_{i})}{n_{0}(s)}\biggr|

\displaystyle\leq\biggl|\frac{\sum_{i\in I_{1}(s)}\mathinner{\bigl(\lambda(\mathring{\Psi}_{i,n}^{\top}\hat{\beta}_{a,s}^{NP})-\lambda(\mathring{\Psi}_{i,n}^{\top}\beta_{a,s}^{NP})\bigr)}}{n_{1}(s)}-\frac{\sum_{i\in I_{0}(s)}\mathinner{\bigl(\lambda(\mathring{\Psi}_{i,n}^{\top}\hat{\beta}_{a,s}^{NP})-\lambda(\mathring{\Psi}_{i,n}^{\top}\beta_{a,s}^{NP})\bigr)}}{n_{0}(s)}\biggr|

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathinner{\!\biggl\lvert\frac{1}{n_{1}(s)}\sum_{i\in I_{1}(s)}\mathinner{\Bigl(R^{D}(a,s,X_{i})-\mathbb{E}[R^{D}(a,s,X_{i})|S_{i}=s]\Bigr)}\biggr\rvert}

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathinner{\!\biggl\lvert\frac{1}{n_{0}(s)}\sum_{i\in I_{0}(s)}\mathinner{\Bigl(R^{D}(a,s,X_{i})-\mathbb{E}[R^{D}(a,s,X_{i})|S_{i}=s]\Bigr)}\biggr\rvert}=\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}I+II+III.

(M.1)

To bound term
I
in (
M.1
), we define
M_{a,s}(\beta_{1},\beta_{2})\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\mathbb{E}\mathinner{\bigl[\lambda(\mathring{\Psi}_{i,n}^{\top}\beta_{1})-\lambda(\mathring{\Psi}_{i,n}^{\top}\beta_{2})|S_{i}=s\bigr]}=\mathbb{E}[\lambda(\mathring{\Psi}_{i,n}^{s,\top}\beta_{1})-\lambda(\mathring{\Psi}_{i,n}^{s,\top}\beta_{2})]
, where
\mathring{\Psi}_{i,n}^{s}=\mathring{\Psi}(X_{i}^{s})
. Then we have

\displaystyle I

\displaystyle\leq\biggl|\frac{\sum_{i\in I_{1}(s)}\mathinner{\bigl[\lambda(\mathring{\Psi}_{i,n}^{\top}\hat{\beta}_{a,s}^{NP})-\lambda(\mathring{\Psi}_{i,n}^{\top}\beta_{a,s}^{NP})-M_{a,s}(\hat{\beta}_{a,s}^{NP},\beta_{a,s}^{NP})\bigr]}}{n_{1}(s)}\biggr|

\displaystyle\qquad+\biggl|\frac{\sum_{i\in I_{0}(s)}\mathinner{\bigl[\lambda(\mathring{\Psi}_{i,n}^{\top}\hat{\beta}_{a,s}^{NP})-\lambda(\mathring{\Psi}_{i,n}^{\top}\beta_{a,s}^{NP})-M_{a,s}(\hat{\beta}_{a,s}^{NP},\beta_{a,s}^{NP})\bigr]}}{n_{0}(s)}\biggr|=\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}I_{1}+I_{2}.

Following the argument in the proof of Theorem
5.1
, in order to show

I_{1}=o_{p}(n^{-1/2})
, we only need to show

n^{1/2}\mathbb{E}\mathinner{\bigl[||\mathbb{P}_{n_{1}(s)}-\mathbb{P}||_{\mathcal{F}}|\{A_{i},S_{i}\}_{i\in[n]}\bigr]}1\{n_{1}(s)\geq n\varepsilon,n_{0}(s)\geq n\varepsilon\}=o(1),

where
\varepsilon
is an arbitrary but fixed constant, and

\mathcal{F}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\mathinner{\Bigl\{\lambda(\mathring{\Psi}_{i,n}^{\top}\beta_{1})-\lambda(\mathring{\Psi}_{i,n}^{\top}\beta_{a,s}^{NP})\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\beta_{1}\in\Re^{h_{n}},||\beta_{1}-\beta_{a,s}^{NP}||_{2}\leq C\sqrt{h_{n}/n_{a}(s)}\Bigr\}},

for some constant
C>0
. Furthermore, we note that
\mathcal{F}
has a bounded
envelope, is of the VC-type with VC-index upper bounded by
Ch_{n}
,
18
18

18

See
van der Vaart and
Wellner 1996
for the calculation of the VC
index.
and has

\displaystyle\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\mathbb{E}\mathinner{\bigl[f^{2}|\{A_{i},S_{i}\}_{i\in[n]}\bigr]}\leq\frac{Ch_{n}}{n_{a}(s)}.

Invoking
Chernozhukov et al. 2014
with
A
being a constant,
\nu=Ch_{n}
,

\sigma^{2}=Ch_{n}/n_{a}(s)
, and
F
and
M
being
2h_{n}
, we have

\displaystyle n^{1/2}\mathbb{E}\left[||\mathbb{P}_{n_{1}(s)}-\mathbb{P}||_{\mathcal{F}}|\{A_{i},S_{i}\}_{i\in[n]}\right]1\{n_{1}(s)\geq n\varepsilon,n_{0}(s)\geq n\varepsilon\}

\displaystyle\leq C\sqrt{\frac{n}{n_{1}(s)}}\mathinner{\biggl(\sqrt{\frac{h_{n}^{2}\log n}{n_{a}(s)}}+\frac{h_{n}\log n}{\sqrt{n_{1}(s)}}\biggr)}1\{n_{1}(s)\geq n\varepsilon,n_{0}(s)\geq n\varepsilon\}

\displaystyle\leq C\sqrt{\frac{1}{\varepsilon}}\mathinner{\biggl(\sqrt{\frac{h_{n}^{2}\log n}{n\varepsilon}}+\frac{h\log n}{\sqrt{n\varepsilon}}\biggr)}\rightarrow 0,

as
n\to\infty
.

Similarly, we can show
I_{2}=o_{p}(n^{-1/2})
. In addition, we note that

\displaystyle II\overset{d}{=}\mathinner{\!\biggl\lvert\frac{1}{n_{1}(s)}\sum_{i=N(s)+1}^{N(s)+n_{1}(s)}\mathinner{\Bigl(R^{D}(a,s,X_{i}^{s})-\mathbb{E}[R^{D}(a,s,X_{i}^{s})]\Bigr)}\biggr\rvert}=o_{p}(n^{-1/2})

by the Chebyshev’s inequality as
\mathbb{E}R^{D,2}(a,s,X_{i}^{s})=\mathbb{E}[R^{D,2}(a,s,X_{i})|S_{i}=s]=o(1)
by Assumption
8
(ii).
Similarly we have
III=o_{p}(n^{-1/2})
. Combining the bounds of
I
,
II
,

III
with (
M.1
), we have

\displaystyle\biggl|\frac{\sum_{i\in I_{1}(s)}\Delta^{D}(a,s,X_{i})}{n_{1}(s)}-\frac{\sum_{i\in I_{0}(s)}\Delta^{D}(a,s,X_{i})}{n_{0}(s)}\biggr|=o_{p}(n^{-1/2}),

which verifies Assumption
3
(i).

To verify Assumption
3
(ii), we note that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Delta^{D,2}(a,s,X_{i})\leq\frac{2}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathinner{\bigl(\lambda(\mathring{\Psi}_{i,n}^{\top}\hat{\beta}_{a,s}^{NP})-\lambda(\mathring{\Psi}_{i,n}^{\top}\beta_{a,s}^{NP})\bigr)}^{2}+\frac{2}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}R^{D,2}(a,S_{i},X_{i})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|\mathring{\Psi}_{i,n}\|_{2}^{2}\|\hat{\beta}_{a,s}^{NP}-\beta_{a,s}^{NP}\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{2}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}R^{D,2}(a,S_{i},X_{i})

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|\mathring{\Psi}_{i,n}\|_{2}^{2}\|\hat{\beta}_{a,s}^{NP}-\beta_{a,s}^{NP}\|_{2}^{2}+o_{p}(1)\leq 2\max_{i}\|\mathring{\Psi}_{i,n}\|_{2}^{2}\max_{s}\|\hat{\beta}_{a,s}^{NP}-\beta_{a,s}^{NP}\|_{2}^{2}+o_{p}(1)

\displaystyle=O_{p}\mathinner{\bigl(\zeta^{2}(h_{n})h_{n}/n_{a}(s)\bigr)}+o_{p}(1)=o_{p}(1)

where the first equality is due to Assumption
8
(ii), and the second
equality is due to Assumption
8
(iv).

Last, Assumption
3
(iii) is implied by Assumption

1
(vi) via Jensen’s inequality.

Appendix N
Proof of Theorem
5.7

We focus on verifying Assumption
3
for
\hat{\mu}^{D}(a,s,X_{i})
. The proof for
\hat{\mu}^{Y}(a,s,X_{i})
is similar and hence

5.4
, we note that, for
each
a=0,1
and

, the data in cell

, denoted

\{D_{i}^{s}(a),X_{i}^{s}\}_{i\in[n]}

(D_{i}(a),X_{i})
given
S_{i}=s

\{A_{i},S_{i}\}_{i\in[n]}
. Then following the standard logistic Lasso
