, we have

\displaystyle\max_{a=0,1,s\in\mathcal{S}}||\hat{\beta}_{a,s}^{R}-\beta_{a,s}^{R}||_{2}=O_{p}\left(\sqrt{h_{n}\log p_{n}/n_{a}(s)}\right)\quad\text{and}\quad\max_{a=0,1,s\in\mathcal{S}}||\hat{\beta}_{a,s}^{R}||_{0}=O_{p}(h_{n}).

Then, we have

\displaystyle\biggl|\frac{\sum_{i\in I_{1}(s)}\Delta^{D}(a,s,X_{i})}{n_{1}(s)}-\frac{\sum_{i\in I_{0}(s)}\Delta^{D}(a,s,X_{i})}{n_{0}(s)}\biggr|

\displaystyle\leq\biggl|\frac{\sum_{i\in I_{1}(s)}\mathinner{\bigl(\lambda(\mathring{\Psi}_{i,n}^{\top}\hat{\beta}_{a,s}^{R})-\lambda(\mathring{\Psi}_{i,n}^{\top}\beta_{a,s}^{R})\bigr)}}{n_{1}(s)}-\frac{\sum_{i\in I_{0}(s)}\mathinner{\bigl(\lambda(\mathring{\Psi}_{i,n}^{\top}\hat{\beta}_{a,s}^{R})-\lambda(\mathring{\Psi}_{i,n}^{\top}\beta_{a,s}^{R})\bigr)}}{n_{0}(s)}\biggr|

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathinner{\!\biggl\lvert\frac{1}{n_{1}(s)}\sum_{i\in I_{1}(s)}\mathinner{\Bigl(R^{D}(a,s,X_{i})-\mathbb{E}[R^{D}(a,s,X_{i})|S_{i}=s]\Bigr)}\biggr\rvert}

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathinner{\!\biggl\lvert\frac{1}{n_{0}(s)}\sum_{i\in I_{0}(s)}\mathinner{\Bigl(R^{D}(a,s,X_{i})-\mathbb{E}[R^{D}(a,s,X_{i})|S_{i}=s]\Bigr)}\biggr\rvert}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=I+II+III.

(N.1)

To bound term
I
in (
M.1
), we define
M_{a,s}(\beta_{1},\beta_{2})\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\mathbb{E}\mathinner{\bigl[\lambda(\mathring{\Psi}_{i,n}^{\top}\beta_{1})-\lambda(\mathring{\Psi}_{i,n}^{\top}\beta_{2})|S_{i}=s\bigr]}=\mathbb{E}[\lambda(\mathring{\Psi}_{i,n}^{s,\top}\beta_{1})-\lambda(\mathring{\Psi}_{i,n}^{s,\top}\beta_{2})]
, where
\mathring{\Psi}_{i,n}^{s}=\mathring{\Psi}(X_{i}^{s})
. Then we have

\displaystyle I

\displaystyle\leq\biggl|\frac{\sum_{i\in I_{1}(s)}\mathinner{\bigl[\lambda(\mathring{\Psi}_{i,n}^{\top}\hat{\beta}_{a,s}^{R})-\lambda(\mathring{\Psi}_{i,n}^{\top}\beta_{a,s}^{R})-M_{a,s}(\hat{\beta}_{a,s}^{R},\beta_{a,s}^{R})\bigr]}}{n_{1}(s)}\biggr|

\displaystyle\qquad+\biggl|\frac{\sum_{i\in I_{0}(s)}\mathinner{\bigl[\lambda(\mathring{\Psi}_{i,n}^{\top}\hat{\beta}_{a,s}^{R})-\lambda(\mathring{\Psi}_{i,n}^{\top}\beta_{a,s}^{R})-M_{a,s}(\hat{\beta}_{a,s}^{R},\beta_{a,s}^{R})\bigr]}}{n_{0}(s)}\biggr|=\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}I_{1}+I_{2}.

Following the argument in the proof of Theorems
5.1
and

5.6
, in order to show
I_{1}=o_{p}(n^{-1/2})
, we only need to
show

n^{1/2}\mathbb{E}\mathinner{\bigl[||\mathbb{P}_{n_{1}(s)}-\mathbb{P}||_{\mathcal{F}}|\{A_{i},S_{i}\}_{i\in[n]}\bigr]}1\{n_{1}(s)\geq n\varepsilon,n_{0}(s)\geq n\varepsilon\}=o(1),

where
\varepsilon
is an arbitrary but fixed constant, and

\mathcal{F}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\mathinner{\Bigl\{\lambda(\mathring{\Psi}_{i,n}^{\top}\beta_{1})-\lambda(\mathring{\Psi}_{i,n}^{\top}\beta_{a,s}^{R})\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\beta_{1}\in\Re^{h_{n}},||\beta_{1}-\beta_{a,s}^{R}||_{2}\leq C\sqrt{h_{n}\log(p_{n})/n_{a}(s)},||\beta_{1}||_{0}\leq Ch_{n}\Bigr\}},

for some constant
C>0
. Furthermore, we note that
\mathcal{F}
has a bounded
envelope and

\displaystyle\sup_{Q}N(\mathcal{F},e_{Q},\varepsilon||F||_{Q,2})\leq\left(\frac{c_{1}p_{n}}{\varepsilon}\right)^{c_{2}h_{n}},

where
c_{1},c_{2}
are two fixed constants,
N(\cdot)
is the covering
number,
e_{Q}(f,g)=\sqrt{Q|f-g|^{2}}
, and the supremum is taken over all
discrete probability measures
Q
. Last, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\mathbb{E}\mathinner{\bigl[f^{2}|\{A_{i},S_{i}\}_{i\in[n]}\bigr]}\leq\frac{Ch_{n}\log p_{n}}{n_{a}(s)}.

Invoking
Chernozhukov et al. 2014
with
A=Cp_{n}
,
\nu=Ch_{n}
,

\sigma^{2}=Ch_{n}\log(p_{n})/n_{a}(s)
, and
F
and
M
being
2
, we
have

\displaystyle n^{1/2}\mathbb{E}\left[||\mathbb{P}_{n_{1}(s)}-\mathbb{P}||_{\mathcal{F}}|\{A_{i},S_{i}\}_{i\in[n]}\right]1\{n_{1}(s)\geq n\varepsilon,n_{0}(s)\geq n\varepsilon\}

\displaystyle\leq C\sqrt{\frac{n}{n_{1}(s)}}\left(\sqrt{h_{n}\frac{h_{n}\log p_{n}}{n_{a}(s)}\log\mathinner{\biggl(\frac{p_{n}}{\sqrt{\frac{h_{n}\log p_{n}}{n_{a}(s)}}}\biggr)}}+\frac{h_{n}}{\sqrt{n_{1}(s)}}\log\mathinner{\biggl(\frac{p_{n}}{\sqrt{\frac{h_{n}\log p_{n}}{n_{a}(s)}}}\biggr)}\right)1\{n_{1}(s)\geq n\varepsilon,n_{0}(s)\geq n\varepsilon\}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\sqrt{\frac{n}{n_{1}(s)}}\right)\left(\frac{h_{n}\log(p_{n})}{\sqrt{n_{1}(s)\wedge n_{0}(s)}}\right)1\{n_{1}(s)\geq n\varepsilon,n_{0}(s)\geq n\varepsilon\}\rightarrow 0.

The bounds for
I_{2}
,
II
and
III
can be established following the same
argument as in the proof of Theorem
5.6
. We omit the detail for
brevity. This leads to Assumption
3
(i).

To verify Assumption
3
(ii), we note that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Delta^{D,2}(a,s,X_{i})\leq\frac{2}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathinner{\bigl(\lambda(\mathring{\Psi}_{i,n}^{\top}\hat{\beta}_{a,s}^{R})-\lambda(\mathring{\Psi}_{i,n}^{\top}\beta_{a,s}^{R})\bigr)}^{2}+\frac{2}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}R^{D,2}(a,S_{i},X_{i})

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathinner{\bigl(\lambda(\mathring{\Psi}_{i,n}^{\top}\hat{\beta}_{a,s}^{R})-\lambda(\mathring{\Psi}_{i,n}^{\top}\beta_{a,s}^{R})\bigr)}^{2}+o_{p}(1)=o_{p}(1),

where the first equality is due to Assumption
9
(iii) and the second
equality is by Assumption
9
(vi) and the fact that

\displaystyle\frac{2}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathinner{\bigl(\lambda(\mathring{\Psi}_{i,n}^{\top}\hat{\beta}_{a,s}^{R})-\lambda(\mathring{\Psi}_{i,n}^{\top}\beta_{a,s}^{R})\bigr)}^{2}\lesssim\frac{(\hat{\beta}_{a,s}^{R}-\beta_{a,s}^{R})^{\top}}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathring{\Psi}_{i,n}\mathring{\Psi}_{i,n}^{\top}(\hat{\beta}_{a,s}^{R}-\beta_{a,s}^{R})\lesssim||\hat{\beta}_{a,s}^{R}-\beta_{a,s}^{R}||_{2}^{2}=o_{p}(1),

where the first probability inequality is due to the fact that
\lambda(\cdot)
is Lipschitz continuous with Lipschitz constant 1. Last, Assumption

3
(iii) is implied by Assumption
1
(vi) via
Jensen’s inequality.

Appendix O
Proof of Theorem
B.1

Some part of the proof of part (i) is due to
Ansel
et al. 2018
while some part of the
proof is original. Let
U_{i}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=(1,X_{i}^{\top})^{\top}
and
\hat{\lambda}_{as}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=(\hat{\gamma}_{as}^{b},\hat{\nu}_{as}^{b,\top})^{\top}
for
a=0,1

and
b=Y,D
. Consider
\hat{\lambda}_{0s}^{D}
as an example; note that

\hat{\lambda}_{0s}^{D}=\mathinner{\biggl(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(1-A_{i})1\{S_{i}=s\}U_{i}U_{i}^{\top}\biggr)}^{-1}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(1-A_{i})1\{S_{i}=s\}U_{i}D_{i}.

Consider the denominator of
\hat{\lambda}_{0s}^{D}
:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(1-A_{i})1\{S_{i}=s\}U_{i}U_{i}^{\top}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\pi(s)-A_{i})1\{S_{i}=s\}U_{i}U_{i}^{\top}+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(1-\pi(s))1\{S_{i}=s\}U_{i}U_{i}^{\top}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\pi(s)-A_{i})1\{S_{i}=s\}\mathinner{\bigl(U_{i}U_{i}^{\top}-\mathbb{E}[U_{i}U_{i}^{\top}|S_{i}]\bigr)}+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\pi(s)-A_{i})1\{S_{i}=s\}\mathbb{E}[U_{i}U_{i}^{\top}|S_{i}]

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(1-\pi(s))1\{S_{i}=s\}U_{i}U_{i}^{\top}.

(O.1)

Consider the first term of (
O.1
). Note that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\mathinner{\biggl[\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\pi(s)-A_{i})1\{S_{i}=s\}\mathinner{\bigl(U_{i}U_{i}^{\top}-\mathbb{E}[U_{i}U_{i}^{\top}|S_{i}]\bigr)}|A^{(n)},S^{(n)}\biggr]}=0.

Invoking the conditional Chebyshev’s inequality, we have, for any
a>0
,

1\leq k,\ell\leq\text{dim}(U_{i})
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\mathinner{\biggl(\mathinner{\!\biggl\lvert\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\pi(s)-A_{i})1\{S_{i}=s\}\mathinner{\bigl(U_{ik}U_{i\ell}-\mathbb{E}[U_{ik}U_{i\ell}|S_{i}]\bigr)}\biggr\rvert}\geq a|A^{(n)},S^{(n)}\biggr)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{a^{2}}\var\mathinner{\biggl(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\pi(s)-A_{i})1\{S_{i}=s\}\mathinner{\bigl(U_{ik}U_{i\ell}-\mathbb{E}[U_{ik}U_{i\ell}|S_{i}]\bigr)}|A^{(n)},S^{(n)}\biggr)}

\displaystyle=\frac{\sum_{i,j\in[n]}(\pi(s)-A_{i})(\pi(s)-A_{j})1\{S_{i}=s\}1\{S_{j}=s\}\mathbb{E}\mathinner{\Bigl[\mathinner{\bigl(U_{ik}U_{i\ell}-\mathbb{E}[U_{ik}U_{i\ell}|S_{i}]\bigr)}\mathinner{\bigl(U_{jk}U_{j\ell}-\mathbb{E}[U_{jk}U_{j\ell}|S_{j}]\bigr)}|A^{(n)},S^{(n)}\Bigr]}}{a^{2}n^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\sum_{i\in[n]}(\pi(s)-A_{i})^{2}1\{S_{i}=s\}\mathbb{E}\mathinner{\Bigl[\mathinner{\bigl(U_{ik}U_{i\ell}-\mathbb{E}[U_{ik}U_{i\ell}|S_{i}]\bigr)}^{2}|A^{(n)},S^{(n)}\Bigr]}}{a^{2}n^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sum_{i\in[n]}(\pi(s)-A_{i})^{2}1\{S_{i}=s\}\mathbb{E}\mathinner{\Bigl[U_{ik}^{2}U_{i\ell}^{2}|S_{i}\Bigr]}}{a^{2}n^{2}}\leq\frac{\sum_{i\in[n]}\mathbb{E}\mathinner{\Bigl[U_{ik}^{2}U_{i\ell}^{2}|S_{i}=s\Bigr]}}{a^{2}n^{2}}=o(1)

(O.2)

where the second equality is due to

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\mathinner{\Bigl[\mathinner{\bigl(U_{ik}U_{i\ell}-\mathbb{E}[U_{ik}U_{i\ell}|S_{i}]\bigr)}\mathinner{\bigl(U_{jk}U_{j\ell}-\mathbb{E}[U_{jk}U_{j\ell}|S_{j}]\bigr)}|A^{(n)},S^{(n)}\Bigr]}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\mathinner{\Bigl[\mathinner{\bigl(U_{ik}U_{i\ell}-\mathbb{E}[U_{ik}U_{i\ell}|S_{i}]\bigr)}\mathinner{\bigl(U_{jk}U_{j\ell}-\mathbb{E}[U_{jk}U_{j\ell}|S_{j}]\bigr)}|S^{(n)}\Bigr]}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\mathinner{\Bigl[U_{ik}U_{i\ell}-\mathbb{E}[U_{ik}U_{i\ell}|S_{i}]|S^{(n)}\Bigr]}\mathbb{E}\mathinner{\Bigl[U_{jk}U_{j\ell}-\mathbb{E}[U_{jk}U_{j\ell}|S_{j}]|S^{(n)}\Bigr]}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\mathinner{\Bigl[U_{ik}U_{i\ell}-\mathbb{E}[U_{ik}U_{i\ell}|S_{i}]|S_{i}\Bigr]}\mathbb{E}\mathinner{\Bigl[U_{jk}U_{j\ell}-\mathbb{E}[U_{jk}U_{j\ell}|S_{j}]|S_{j}\Bigr]}=0

for
i\neq j
, where the second equality is due to that
U_{ik}U_{i\ell}-\mathbb{E}[U_{ik}U_{i\ell}|S_{i}]
and
U_{jk}U_{j\ell}-\mathbb{E}[U_{jk}U_{j\ell}|S_{j}]
are independent conditional on
S^{(n)}
. From (
O.2
), we deduce that the first term of (
O.1
) is

o_{p}(1)
. Consider the second term of (
O.1
).

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\pi(s)-A_{i})1\{S_{i}=s\}\mathbb{E}[U_{i}U_{i}^{\top}|S_{i}]=\mathbb{E}[UU^{\top}|S=s]\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\pi(s)-A_{i})1\{S_{i}=s\}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[UU^{\top}|S=s]\frac{1}{n}B_{n}(s)=o_{p}(1).

Consider the third term of (
O.1
).

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(1-\pi(s))1\{S_{i}=s\}U_{i}U_{i}^{\top}=(1-\pi(s))\frac{n(s)}{n}\frac{1}{n(s)}\sum_{i=1}^{n}1\{S_{i}=s\}U_{i}U_{i}^{\top}

\displaystyle\qquad\xrightarrow{p}(1-\pi(s))p(s)\mathbb{E}[UU^{\top}|S=s].

We hence have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(1-A_{i})1\{S_{i}=s\}U_{i}U_{i}^{\top}\xrightarrow{p}(1-\pi(s))p(s)\mathbb{E}[UU^{\top}|S=s].

Similarly, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(1-A_{i})1\{S_{i}=s\}U_{i}D_{i}\xrightarrow{p}(1-\pi(s))\hat{p}(s)\mathbb{E}[UD(0)|S=s]

\displaystyle\hat{\lambda}_{0s}^{D}\xrightarrow{p}\mathinner{\Bigl(\mathbb{E}[UU^{\top}|S=s]\Bigr)}^{-1}\mathbb{E}[UD(0)|S=s]

\displaystyle\hat{\lambda}_{1s}^{D}\xrightarrow{p}\mathinner{\Bigl(\mathbb{E}[UU^{\top}|S=s]\Bigr)}^{-1}\mathbb{E}[UD(1)|S=s].

Thus, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\hat{p}(s)(\hat{\gamma}_{1s}^{D}-\hat{\gamma}_{0s}^{D}+(\hat{\nu}_{1s}^{D}-\hat{\nu}_{0s}^{D})^{\top}\bar{X}_{s})=\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}(\hat{\lambda}_{1s}^{D}-\hat{\lambda}_{0s}^{D})^{\top}\left(\begin{array}[c]{c}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}1\{S_{i}=s\}\\
\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}X_{i}1\{S_{i}=s\}\end{array}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad=\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\frac{n(s)}{n}\frac{1}{n(s)}\sum_{i\in[n]}1\{S_{i}=s\}U_{i}^{\top}(\hat{\lambda}_{1s}^{D}-\hat{\lambda}_{0s}^{D})

\displaystyle\qquad\xrightarrow{p}\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}p(s)\mathbb{E}[U^{\top}|S=s]\mathinner{\Bigl(\mathbb{E}[UU^{\top}|S=s]\Bigr)}^{-1}\mathbb{E}\mathinner{\Bigl[U\mathinner{\bigl(D(1)-D(0)\bigr)}|S=s\Bigr]}

\displaystyle\qquad=\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}p(s)\mathbb{E}\mathinner{\bigl[D(1)-D(0)|S=s\bigr]}=\mathbb{E}\mathinner{\bigl[D(1)-D(0)\bigr]}

where the second last equality is due to
\mathbb{E}[U^{\top}|S=s]\mathinner{\bigl(\mathbb{E}[UU^{\top}|S=s]\bigr)}^{-1}=(1,0,\ldots,0)
(
Ansel
et al. 2018

p290). Thus, the denominator of
\sqrt{n}(\hat{\tau}_{S}-\tau)
converges in
probability to
\mathbb{E}[D(1)-D(0)]
.

We now consider the numerator of
\sqrt{n}(\hat{\tau}_{S}-\tau)
. Relying on a
similar argument, we have

\displaystyle\hat{\lambda}_{1s}^{Y}=\mathinner{\biggl(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}A_{i}1\{S_{i}=s\}U_{i}U_{i}^{\top}\biggr)}^{-1}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}A_{i}1\{S_{i}=s\}U_{i}Y_{i}(D_{i}(1))

\displaystyle\qquad\xrightarrow{p}\mathinner{\Bigl(\mathbb{E}[UU^{\top}|S=s]\Bigr)}^{-1}\mathbb{E}[UY(D(1))|S=s]

\displaystyle\hat{\lambda}_{0s}^{Y}=\mathinner{\biggl(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(1-A_{i})1\{S_{i}=s\}U_{i}U_{i}^{\top}\biggr)}^{-1}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(1-A_{i})1\{S_{i}=s\}U_{i}Y_{i}(D_{i}(0))

\displaystyle\qquad\xrightarrow{p}\mathinner{\Bigl(\mathbb{E}[UU^{\top}|S=s]\Bigr)}^{-1}\mathbb{E}[UY(D(0))|S=s]

\displaystyle\hat{\eta}_{1s}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\hat{\lambda}_{1s}^{Y}-\tau\hat{\lambda}_{1s}^{D}\xrightarrow{p}\mathinner{\Bigl(\mathbb{E}[UU^{\top}|S=s]\Bigr)}^{-1}\mathbb{E}\mathinner{\Bigl[U\mathinner{\bigl[Y(D(1))-\tau D(1)\bigr]}|S=s\Bigr]}=\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\eta_{1s}

\displaystyle\hat{\eta}_{0s}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\hat{\lambda}_{0s}^{Y}-\tau\hat{\lambda}_{0s}^{D}\xrightarrow{p}\mathinner{\Bigl(\mathbb{E}[UU^{\top}|S=s]\Bigr)}^{-1}\mathbb{E}\mathinner{\Bigl[U\mathinner{\bigl[Y(D(0))-\tau D(0)\bigr]}|S=s\Bigr]}=\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\eta_{0s}.

The numerator of
\sqrt{n}(\hat{\tau}_{S}-\tau)
could be written as

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\hat{p}(s)(\hat{\gamma}_{1s}^{Y}-\hat{\gamma}_{0s}^{Y}+(\hat{\nu}_{1s}^{Y}-\hat{\nu}_{0s}^{Y})^{\top}\bar{X}_{s})-\sqrt{n}\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\hat{p}(s)(\hat{\gamma}_{1s}^{D}-\hat{\gamma}_{0s}^{D}+(\hat{\nu}_{1s}^{D}-\hat{\nu}_{0s}^{D})^{\top}\bar{X}_{s})\tau

\displaystyle=\sqrt{n}\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\hat{p}(s)\frac{1}{n(s)}\sum_{i\in[n]}1\{S_{i}=s\}U_{i}^{\top}\mathinner{\bigl[\hat{\lambda}_{1s}^{Y}-\tau\hat{\lambda}_{1s}^{D}-(\hat{\lambda}_{0s}^{Y}-\tau\hat{\lambda}_{0s}^{D})\bigr]}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{n}\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\hat{p}(s)\bar{U}_{s}^{\top}(\hat{\eta}_{1s}-\eta_{1s})-\sqrt{n}\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\hat{p}(s)\bar{U}_{s}^{\top}(\hat{\eta}_{0s}-\eta_{0s})+\sqrt{n}\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\hat{p}(s)\bar{U}_{s}^{\top}(\eta_{1s}-\eta_{0s})

(O.3)

where
\bar{U}_{s}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\frac{1}{n(s)}\sum_{i\in[n]}1\{S_{i}=s\}U_{i}\xrightarrow{p}\mathbb{E}[U|S=s]
. Consider the first term of (
O.3
).

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\hat{p}(s)\bar{U}_{s}^{\top}(\hat{\eta}_{1s}-\eta_{1s})

\displaystyle=\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\hat{p}(s)\bar{U}_{s}^{\top}\mathinner{\biggl(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}A_{i}1\{S_{i}=s\}U_{i}U_{i}^{\top}\biggr)}^{-1}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}A_{i}1\{S_{i}=s\}U_{i}\mathinner{\bigl[Y_{i}(D_{i}(1))-\tau D_{i}(1)-U_{i}^{\top}\eta_{1s}\bigr]}

\displaystyle=\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\hat{p}(s)\mathbb{E}[U^{\top}|S=s]\mathinner{\biggl(\pi(s)\hat{p}(s)\mathbb{E}[UU^{\top}|S=s]\biggr)}^{-1}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}A_{i}1\{S_{i}=s\}U_{i}\mathinner{\bigl[Y_{i}(D_{i}(1))-\tau D_{i}(1)-U_{i}^{\top}\eta_{1s}\bigr]}+o_{p}(1)

\displaystyle=\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\frac{1}{\pi(s)}\mathbb{E}[U^{\top}|S=s]\mathinner{\biggl(\mathbb{E}[UU^{\top}|S=s]\biggr)}^{-1}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}A_{i}1\{S_{i}=s\}U_{i}\mathinner{\bigl[Y_{i}(D_{i}(1))-\tau D_{i}(1)-U_{i}^{\top}\eta_{1s}\bigr]}+o_{p}(1)

\displaystyle=\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\frac{1}{\pi(s)}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}A_{i}1\{S_{i}=s\}\mathinner{\bigl[Y_{i}(D_{i}(1))-\tau D_{i}(1)-U_{i}^{\top}\eta_{1s}\bigr]}+o_{p}(1)

(O.4)

where the second equality is based on that

n^{-1/2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}A_{i}1\{S_{i}=s\}U_{i}\mathinner{\bigl[Y_{i}(D_{i}(1))-\tau D_{i}(1)-U_{i}^{\top}\eta_{1s}\bigr]}=O_{p}(1),

which is implied by the asymptotic normality of (
O.7
), which we will prove shortly, and the last equality is due to
\mathbb{E}[U^{\top}|S=s]\mathinner{\bigl(\mathbb{E}[UU^{\top}|S=s]\bigr)}^{-1}=(1,0,\ldots,0)

(
Ansel
et al. 2018
p290). Likewise, the second term of (
O.3
)

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\hat{p}(s)\bar{U}_{s}^{\top}(\hat{\eta}_{0s}-\eta_{0s})

\displaystyle=\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\frac{1}{1-\pi(s)}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(1-A_{i})1\{S_{i}=s\}\mathinner{\bigl[Y_{i}(D_{i}(0))-\tau D_{i}(0)-U_{i}^{\top}\eta_{0s}\bigr]}+o_{p}(1).

(O.5)

Note that

\displaystyle\eta_{as}=\left(\begin{array}[c]{c}\mathbb{E}\mathinner{\bigl[Y(D(a))-\tau D(a)|S=s\bigr]}-\mathbb{E}[X^{\top}\nu_{as}^{YD}|S=s]\\
\nu_{as}^{YD}\end{array}\right)

for
a=0,1
via the Frisch-Waugh Theorem. Hence

\displaystyle U_{i}^{\top}\eta_{as}=\mathbb{E}\mathinner{\bigl[Y(D(a))-\tau D(a)|S=s\bigr]}+X_{i}^{\top}\nu_{as}^{YD}-\mathbb{E}[X^{\top}\nu_{as}^{YD}|S=s].

(O.6)

Substituting (
O.4
), (
O.5
) and (
O.6
) into (
O.3
), we could
write the numerator of
\sqrt{n}(\hat{\tau}_{S}-\tau)
as

\displaystyle\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\frac{1}{\pi(s)}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}A_{i}1\{S_{i}=s\}\mathinner{\bigl[Y_{i}(D_{i}(1))-\tau D_{i}(1)-U_{i}^{\top}\eta_{1s}\bigr]}

\displaystyle\hskip 17.00024pt-\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\frac{1}{1-\pi(s)}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(1-A_{i})1\{S_{i}=s\}\mathinner{\bigl[Y_{i}(D_{i}(0))-\tau D_{i}(0)-U_{i}^{\top}\eta_{0s}\bigr]}

(O.7)

\displaystyle\hskip 17.00024pt+\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i\in[n]}1\{S_{i}=s\}U_{i}^{\top}(\eta_{1s}-\eta_{0s})+o_{p}(1)

\displaystyle=\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\frac{1}{\pi(s)}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}A_{i}1\{S_{i}=s\}\mathinner{\biggl[Y_{i}(D_{i}(1))-\tau D_{i}(1)-\mathbb{E}\mathinner{\bigl[Y(D(1))-\tau D(1)|S=s\bigr]}-\mathinner{\bigl(X_{i}^{\top}\nu_{1s}^{YD}-\mathbb{E}[X^{\top}\nu_{1s}^{YD}|S=s]\bigr)}\biggr]}

\displaystyle\hskip 8.50012pt-\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\frac{1}{1-\pi(s)}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(1-A_{i})1\{S_{i}=s\}\mathinner{\biggl[Y_{i}(D_{i}(0))-\tau D_{i}(0)-\mathbb{E}\mathinner{\bigl[Y(D(0))-\tau D(0)|S=s\bigr]}-\mathinner{\bigl(X_{i}^{\top}\nu_{0s}^{YD}-\mathbb{E}[X^{\top}\nu_{0s}^{YD}|S=s]\bigr)}\biggr]}

\displaystyle\hskip 8.50012pt+\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i\in[n]}1\{S_{i}=s\}\mathbb{E}\mathinner{\Bigl[Y(D(1))-Y(D(0))-\tau(D(1)-D(0))|S=s\Bigr]}

\displaystyle\hskip 8.50012pt+\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i\in[n]}1\{S_{i}=s\}\mathinner{\Bigl(X_{i}^{\top}(\nu_{1s}^{YD}-\nu_{0s}^{YD})-\mathbb{E}[X^{\top}(\nu_{1s}^{YD}-\nu_{0s}^{YD})|S=s]\Bigr)}+o_{p}(1)

\displaystyle=\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}A_{i}1\{S_{i}=s\}\mathinner{\biggl[\frac{Y_{i}(D_{i}(1))-\tau D_{i}(1)-X_{i}^{\top}\nu_{1s}^{YD}-\mathbb{E}\mathinner{\bigl[Y(D(1))-\tau D(1)-X^{\top}\nu_{1s}^{YD}|S=s\bigr]}}{\pi(s)}\biggr]}

\displaystyle\hskip 8.50012pt+\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i\in[n]}A_{i}1\{S_{i}=s\}\mathinner{\Bigl(X_{i}^{\top}(\nu_{1s}^{YD}-\nu_{0s}^{YD})-\mathbb{E}[X^{\top}(\nu_{1s}^{YD}-\nu_{0s}^{YD})|S=s]\Bigr)}

\displaystyle\hskip 8.50012pt-\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(1-A_{i})1\{S_{i}=s\}\mathinner{\biggl[\frac{Y_{i}(D_{i}(0))-\tau D_{i}(0)-X_{i}^{\top}\nu_{0s}^{YD}-\mathbb{E}\mathinner{\bigl[Y(D(0))-\tau D(0)-X^{\top}\nu_{0s}^{YD}|S=s\bigr]}}{1-\pi(s)}\biggr]}

\displaystyle\hskip 8.50012pt+\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i\in[n]}(1-A_{i})1\{S_{i}=s\}\mathinner{\Bigl(X_{i}^{\top}(\nu_{1s}^{YD}-\nu_{0s}^{YD})-\mathbb{E}[X^{\top}(\nu_{1s}^{YD}-\nu_{0s}^{YD})|S=s]\Bigr)}

\displaystyle\hskip 8.50012pt+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i\in[n]}\mathbb{E}\mathinner{\Bigl[Y(D(1))-Y(D(0))-\tau(D(1)-D(0))|S\Bigr]}+o_{p}(1).

(O.8)

Define

\displaystyle\rho_{is}(1)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\frac{Y_{i}(D_{i}(1))-\tau D_{i}(1)-X_{i}^{\top}\nu_{1s}^{YD}}{\pi(s)}+X_{i}^{\top}(\nu_{1s}^{YD}-\nu_{0s}^{YD})

\displaystyle\rho_{is}(0)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\frac{Y_{i}(D_{i}(0))-\tau D_{i}(0)-X_{i}^{\top}\nu_{0s}^{YD}}{1-\pi(s)}-X_{i}^{\top}(\nu_{1s}^{YD}-\nu_{0s}^{YD}).

Then the first four terms of (
O.8
) could be written compactly as

\displaystyle R_{n,1}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}A_{i}1\{S_{i}=s\}\mathinner{\bigl[\rho_{is}(1)-\mathbb{E}[\rho_{is}(1)|S_{i}=s]\bigr]}

\displaystyle\qquad-\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(1-A_{i})1\{S_{i}=s\}\mathinner{\bigl[\rho_{is}(0)-\mathbb{E}[\rho_{is}(0)|S_{i}=s]\bigr]}.

Define
R_{n,2}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i\in[n]}\mathbb{E}\mathinner{\bigl[Y(D(1))-Y(D(0))-\tau(D(1)-D(0))|S\bigr]}
. To establish the asymptotic
distribution of (
O.8
), we first argue that

\displaystyle(R_{n,1},R_{n,2})\overset{d}{=}(R_{n,1}^{*},R_{n,2})+o_{p}(1)

for a random variable
R_{n,1}^{*}
that satisfies
R_{n,1}^{*}\perp\!\!\!\perp R_{n,2}
. Conditional on
\{S^{(n)},A^{(n)}\}
, the distribution
of
R_{n,1}
is the same as the distribution of the same quantity where units
are ordered by strata and then ordered by
A_{i}=1
first and
A_{i}=0
second
within strata. To this end, define
N(s)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}1\{S_{i}<s\}
and

F(s)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\mathbb{P}(S_{i}<s)
. Furthermore, independently for each

s\in\mathcal{S}
and independently of
\{S^{(n)},A^{(n)}\}
, let

\mathinner{\bigl\{Y_{i}(1)^{s},Y_{i}(0)^{s},D_{i}(1)^{s},D_{i}(0)^{s},X_{i}^{s}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}1\leq i\leq n\bigr\}}
be i.i.d.
over
i
with distribution equal to that of
(Y(1),Y(0),D(1),D(0),X)|S=s
.
Define

\displaystyle\tilde{\rho}_{is}(a)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\rho_{is}(a)-\mathbb{E}[\rho_{is}(a)|S_{i}=s],\qquad\tilde{\rho}_{is}^{s}(a)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\rho_{is}^{s}(a)-\mathbb{E}[\rho_{is}^{s}(a)|S_{i}=s],

where

\displaystyle\rho_{is}^{s}(1)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\frac{Y_{i}^{s}(D_{i}^{s}(1))-\tau D_{i}^{s}(1)-X_{i}^{s,\top}\nu_{1s}^{YD}}{\pi(s)}+X_{i}^{s,\top}(\nu_{1s}^{YD}-\nu_{0s}^{YD})

\displaystyle\rho_{is}^{s}(0)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\frac{Y_{i}^{s}(D_{i}^{s}(0))-\tau D_{i}^{s}(0)-X_{i}^{s,\top}\nu_{0s}^{YD}}{1-\pi(s)}-X_{i}^{s,\top}(\nu_{1s}^{YD}-\nu_{0s}^{YD}).

Then we have

\displaystyle R_{n,1}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}1\{S_{i}=s\}\mathinner{\Bigl[A_{i}\tilde{\rho}_{is}(1)-(1-A_{i})\tilde{\rho}_{is}(0)\Bigr]}.

Define

\displaystyle\tilde{R}_{n,1}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\mathinner{\biggl[\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=n\frac{N(s)}{n}+1}^{n\mathinner{\bigl(\frac{N(s)}{n}+\frac{n_{1}(s)}{n}\bigr)}}\tilde{\rho}_{is}^{s}(1)-\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{n\mathinner{\bigl(\frac{N(s)}{n}+\frac{n_{1}(s)}{n}\bigr)}+1}^{n\mathinner{\bigl(\frac{N(s)}{n}+\frac{n(s)}{n}\bigr)}}\tilde{\rho}_{is}^{s}(0)\biggr]}

\displaystyle R_{n,1}^{*}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\mathinner{\biggl[\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=\lfloor nF(s)\rfloor+1}^{\lfloor n(F(s)+\pi(s)p(s))\rfloor}\tilde{\rho}_{is}^{s}(1)-\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=\lfloor n(F(s)+\pi(s)p(s))\rfloor+1}^{\lfloor n(F(s)+p(s))\rfloor}\tilde{\rho}_{is}^{s}(0)\biggr]}.

Thus
R_{n,1}|S^{(n)},A^{(n)}\overset{d}{=}\tilde{R}_{n,1}|S^{(n)},A^{(n)}

(and as a by-product
R_{n,1}\overset{d}{=}\tilde{R}_{n,1}
). Since
R_{n,2}

is a function of
\{S^{(n)},A^{(n)}\}
, we have, arguing along the line of a
joint distribution being the product of a conditional distribution and a
marginal distribution,
(R_{n,1},R_{n,2})\overset{d}{=}(\tilde{R}_{n,1},R_{n,2})
. Define the following partial sum process

\displaystyle g_{n}(u)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{\lfloor nu\rfloor}\tilde{\rho}^{s}_{is}(1).

Under our assumptions,
g_{n}(u)
converges weakly to a suitably scaled
Brownian motion. Next, by elementary properties of Brownian motion, we have
that

\displaystyle g_{n}\mathinner{\bigl(F(s)+\pi(s)p(s)\bigr)}-g_{n}\mathinner{\bigl(F(s)\bigr)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=\lfloor nF(s)\rfloor+1}^{\lfloor n(F(s)+\pi(s)p(s))\rfloor}\tilde{\rho}^{s}_{is}(1)\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{N}\mathinner{\biggl(0,\pi(s)p(s)\var\mathinner{\bigl(\rho_{s}(1)|S=s\bigr)}\biggr)}.

(O.9)

Furthermore, since

\displaystyle\mathinner{\biggl(\frac{N(s)}{n},\frac{n_{1}(s)}{n}\biggr)}\xrightarrow{p}\mathinner{\bigl(F(s),\pi(s)p(s)\bigr)},

it follows that

\displaystyle g_{n}\mathinner{\biggl(\frac{N(s)+n_{1}(s)}{n}\biggr)}-g_{n}\mathinner{\biggl(\frac{N(s)}{n}\biggr)}-\mathinner{\biggl[g_{n}\mathinner{\bigl(F(s)+\pi(s)p(s)\bigr)}-g_{n}\mathinner{\bigl(F(s)\bigr)}\biggr]}\xrightarrow{p}0

(O.10)

where the convergence follows from the stochastic equicontinuity of the
partial sum process. Using (
O.9
) and (
O.10
), we have:

\displaystyle(R_{n,1},R_{n,2})

\displaystyle\overset{d}{=}(\tilde{R}_{n,1},R_{n,2})=(R^{*}_{n,1},R_{n,2})+o_{p}(1)

(O.11)

\displaystyle R^{*}_{n,1}

\displaystyle\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{N}\mathinner{\biggl(0,\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\mathinner{\biggl[\pi(s)p(s)\var\mathinner{\bigl(\rho_{s}(1)|S=s\bigr)}+\mathinner{\bigl[1-\pi(s)\bigr]}p(s)\var\mathinner{\bigl(\rho_{s}(0)|S=s\bigr)}\biggr]}\biggr)}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{N}\mathinner{\biggl(0,\mathbb{E}\mathinner{\Bigl[\pi(S)\mathinner{\bigl(\rho_{S}(1)-\mathbb{E}[\rho_{S}(1)|S]\bigr)}^{2}+(1-\pi(S))\mathinner{\bigl(\rho_{S}(0)-\mathbb{E}[\rho_{S}(0)|S]\bigr)}^{2}\Bigr]}\biggr)}

\displaystyle=\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\zeta_{1}

where the convergence in distribution is due to an analogous argument for

\tilde{\rho}^{s}_{is}(0)
and the independence of

\mathinner{\bigl\{Y_{i}(1)^{s},Y_{i}(0)^{s},D_{i}(1)^{s},D_{i}(0)^{s},X_{i}^{s}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}1\leq i\leq n,s\in\mathcal{S}\bigr\}}

across both
i
and
s
. Moreover, since
R_{n,1}^{*}
is a function of

\mathinner{\bigl\{Y_{i}(1)^{s},Y_{i}(0)^{s},D_{i}(1)^{s},D_{i}(0)^{s},X_{i}^{s}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}1\leq i\leq n,s\in\mathcal{S}\bigr\}}

\perp\!\!\!\perp S^{(n)},A^{(n)}
, and
R_{n,2}
is a function of

\{S^{(n)},A^{(n)}\}
, we see that
R_{n,1}^{*}\perp\!\!\!\perp R_{n,2}
.
Thus (
O.11
) implies

\displaystyle(R_{n,1},R_{n,2})\overset{d}{=}(R^{*}_{n,1},R_{n,2})+o_{p}(1)\rightsquigarrow\mathinner{\bigl(\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}\bigr)}

where
\zeta_{1}
and
\zeta_{2}
are independent, with

\displaystyle\zeta_{2}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\mathcal{N}\mathinner{\biggl(0,\mathbb{E}\mathinner{\biggl[\mathinner{\Bigl(\mathbb{E}\mathinner{\bigl[Y(D(1))-Y(D(0))-\tau(D(1)-D(0))|S\bigr]}\Bigr)}^{2}\biggr]}\biggr)}.

We hence show that the asymptotic distribution of the numerator of
\sqrt{n}(\hat{\tau}_{S}-\tau)
is
\zeta_{1}+\zeta_{2}
. This completes the proof of part (i). The proof of part (ii), available upon request, is omitted in the interest of space as it is quite similar to that of part (ii) of Theorem
3.1
.

Appendix P
Technical Lemmas Used in the Proof of Theorem
3.1

Lemma P.1
.

Suppose assumptions in Theorem
3.1
hold. Then, we have

\displaystyle R_{n,1}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathinner{\biggl(1-\frac{1}{\pi(S_{i})}\biggr)}A_{i}\tilde{\mu}^{Y}(1,S_{i},X_{i})+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(1-A_{i})\tilde{\mu}^{Y}(1,S_{i},X_{i})+o_{p}(1),

\displaystyle R_{n,2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathinner{\biggl(\frac{1}{1-\pi(S_{i})}-1\biggr)}(1-A_{i})\tilde{\mu}^{Y}(0,S_{i},X_{i})-\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}A_{i}\tilde{\mu}^{Y}(0,S_{i},X_{i})+o_{p}(1),

\displaystyle R_{n,3}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{1}{\pi(S_{i})}\tilde{W}_{i}A_{i}-\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{1-A_{i}}{1-\pi(S_{i})}\tilde{Z}_{i}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathinner{\bigl(\mathbb{E}[W_{i}-Z_{i}|S_{i}]-\mathbb{E}[W_{i}-Z_{i}]\bigr)}+o_{p}(1),

where for
a=0,1
,

\displaystyle\tilde{\mu}^{Y}(a,S_{i},X_{i})\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\overline{\mu}^{Y}(a,S_{i},X_{i})-\overline{\mu}^{Y}(a,S_{i}),\quad\overline{\mu}^{Y}(a,S_{i})\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\mathbb{E}\mathinner{\bigl[\overline{\mu}^{Y}(a,S_{i},X_{i})|S_{i}\bigr]},

\displaystyle W_{i}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=Y_{i}(1)D_{i}(1)+Y_{i}(0)(1-D_{i}(1)),\quad Z_{i}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=Y_{i}(1)D_{i}(0)+Y_{i}(0)(1-D_{i}(0)),

\displaystyle\tilde{W}_{i}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=W_{i}-\mathbb{E}[W_{i}|S_{i}],\quad\text{and}\quad\tilde{Z}_{i}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=Z_{i}-\mathbb{E}[Z_{i}|S_{i}].

Proof.

We have

\displaystyle R_{n,1}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathinner{\biggl[\hat{\mu}^{Y}(1,S_{i},X_{i})-\frac{A_{i}\hat{\mu}^{Y}(1,S_{i},X_{i})}{\hat{\pi}(S_{i})}\biggr]}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{A_{i}-\hat{\pi}(S_{i})}{\hat{\pi}(S_{i})}\hat{\mu}^{Y}(1,S_{i},X_{i})

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{A_{i}-\hat{\pi}(S_{i})}{\hat{\pi}(S_{i})}\mathinner{\bigl[\hat{\mu}^{Y}(1,S_{i},X_{i})-\overline{\mu}^{Y}(1,S_{i},X_{i})+\overline{\mu}^{Y}(1,S_{i},X_{i})\bigr]}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{A_{i}-\hat{\pi}(S_{i})}{\hat{\pi}(S_{i})}\Delta^{Y}(1,S_{i},X_{i})-\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{A_{i}}{\hat{\pi}(S_{i})}\overline{\mu}^{Y}(1,S_{i},X_{i})+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\overline{\mu}^{Y}(1,S_{i},X_{i})

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{A_{i}-\hat{\pi}(S_{i})}{\hat{\pi}(S_{i})}\Delta^{Y}(1,S_{i},X_{i})-\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{A_{i}}{\hat{\pi}(S_{i})}\tilde{\mu}^{Y}(1,S_{i},X_{i})+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\tilde{\mu}^{Y}(1,S_{i},X_{i}),

(P.1)

where the last equality is due to

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{A_{i}}{\hat{\pi}(S_{i})}\overline{\mu}^{Y}(1,S_{i})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\overline{\mu}^{Y}(1,S_{i}).

Consider the first term of (
P.1
).

\displaystyle\mathinner{\!\biggl\lvert\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{A_{i}-\hat{\pi}(S_{i})}{\hat{\pi}(S_{i})}\Delta^{Y}(1,S_{i},X_{i})\biggr\rvert}=\mathinner{\!\biggl\lvert\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{A_{i}-\hat{\pi}(s)}{\hat{\pi}(s)}\Delta^{Y}(1,s,X_{i})1\{S_{i}=s\}\biggr\rvert}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\mathinner{\!\biggl\lvert\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\frac{1}{\hat{\pi}(s)}\sum_{i=1}^{n}A_{i}\Delta^{Y}(1,s,X_{i})1\{S_{i}=s\}-\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Delta^{Y}(1,s,X_{i})1\{S_{i}=s\}\biggr\rvert}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\mathinner{\!\biggl\lvert\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\sum_{i\in I_{1}(s)}\Delta^{Y}(1,s,X_{i})\frac{n(s)}{n_{1}(s)}-\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\sum_{i\in I_{0}(s)\cup I_{1}(s)}\Delta^{Y}(1,s,X_{i})\biggr\rvert}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\mathinner{\!\biggl\lvert\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\sum_{i\in I_{1}(s)}\Delta^{Y}(1,s,X_{i})\frac{n_{0}(s)}{n_{1}(s)}-\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\sum_{i\in I_{0}(s)}\Delta^{Y}(1,s,X_{i})\biggr\rvert}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\mathinner{\!\biggl\lvert\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}n_{0}(s)\mathinner{\biggl[\frac{\sum_{i\in I_{1}(s)}\Delta^{Y}(1,s,X_{i})}{n_{1}(s)}-\frac{\sum_{i\in I_{0}(s)}\Delta^{Y}(1,s,X_{i})}{n_{0}(s)}\biggr]}\biggr\rvert}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}n_{0}(s)\mathinner{\!\biggl\lvert\frac{\sum_{i\in I_{1}(s)}\Delta^{Y}(1,s,X_{i})}{n_{1}(s)}-\frac{\sum_{i\in I_{0}(s)}\Delta^{Y}(1,s,X_{i})}{n_{0}(s)}\biggr\rvert}=o_{p}(1)

where the last equality is due to Assumption
3
. Thus

\displaystyle R_{n,1}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{A_{i}}{\hat{\pi}(S_{i})}\tilde{\mu}^{Y}(1,S_{i},X_{i})+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\tilde{\mu}^{Y}(1,S_{i},X_{i})+o_{p}(1)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{A_{i}}{\hat{\pi}(S_{i})}\tilde{\mu}^{Y}(1,S_{i},X_{i})+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}A_{i}\tilde{\mu}^{Y}(1,S_{i},X_{i})+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(1-A_{i})\tilde{\mu}^{Y}(1,S_{i},X_{i})+o_{p}(1)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathinner{\biggl(1-\frac{1}{\hat{\pi}(S_{i})}\biggr)}A_{i}\tilde{\mu}^{Y}(1,S_{i},X_{i})+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(1-A_{i})\tilde{\mu}^{Y}(1,S_{i},X_{i})+o_{p}(1).

(P.2)

In addition, we note that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathinner{\biggl(1-\frac{1}{\hat{\pi}(S_{i})}\biggr)}A_{i}\tilde{\mu}^{Y}(1,S_{i},X_{i})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathinner{\biggl(1-\frac{1}{\pi(S_{i})}\biggr)}A_{i}\tilde{\mu}^{Y}(1,S_{i},X_{i})

\displaystyle+\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\mathinner{\biggl(\frac{1}{\pi(s)}-\frac{1}{\hat{\pi}(s)}\biggr)}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}A_{i}\tilde{\mu}^{Y}(1,s,X_{i})1\{S_{i}=s\}.

Note that under Assumption
1
(i), conditional on
\{S^{(n)},A^{(n)}\}
, the distribution of

\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}A_{i}\tilde{\mu}^{Y}(1,s,X_{i})1\{S_{i}=s\}

is the same as the distribution of the same quantity where units are ordered by strata and then ordered by
A_{i}=1
first and
A_{i}=0
second within strata. To this end, define
N(s)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}1\{S_{i}<s\}
and
F(s)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\mathbb{P}(S_{i}<s)
. Furthermore, independently for each
s\in\mathcal{S}
and independently of
\{S^{(n)},A^{(n)}\}
, let
\mathinner{\bigl\{X_{i}^{s}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}1\leq i\leq n\bigr\}}
be i.i.d with marginal distribution equal to the distribution of
X_{i}|S=s
. Define

\displaystyle\tilde{\mu}^{b}(a,s,X_{i}^{s})

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\overline{\mu}^{b}(a,s,X_{i}^{s})-\mathbb{E}\mathinner{\bigl[\overline{\mu}^{b}(a,s,X_{i}^{s})|S_{i}=s\bigr]}

Then, we have, for
s\in\mathcal{S}
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}A_{i}\tilde{\mu}^{Y}(1,s,X_{i})1\{S_{i}=s\}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=N(s)+1}^{N(s)+n_{1}(s)}\tilde{\mu}^{Y}(1,s,X_{i}^{s}).

In addition, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=N(s)+1}^{N(s)+n_{1}(s)}\tilde{\mu}^{Y}(1,s,X_{i}^{s})\right)^{2}\biggl|S^{(n)},A^{(n)}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{n_{1}(s)}{n}\mathbb{E}\mathinner{\bigl[\tilde{\mu}^{Y,2}(a,s,X_{i}^{s})|S^{(n)}\bigr]}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{n_{1}(s)}{n}E\left[\overline{\mu}^{Y,2}(a,s,X_{i})|S_{i}=s\right]=O_{p}(1),

which implies

\displaystyle\max_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\biggl|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=N(s)+1}^{N(s)+n_{1}(s)}\tilde{\mu}^{Y}(1,s,X_{i}^{s})\biggr|=O_{p}(1).

Combining this with the facts that
\max_{s\in\mathcal{S}}|\hat{\pi}(s)-\pi(s)|=o_{p}(1)
and
\min_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\pi(s)>c>0
for some constant
c
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\mathinner{\biggl(\frac{1}{\pi(s)}-\frac{1}{\hat{\pi}(s)}\biggr)}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}A_{i}\tilde{\mu}^{Y}(1,s,X_{i})1\{S_{i}=s\}=o_{p}(1)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathinner{\biggl(1-\frac{1}{\hat{\pi}(S_{i})}\biggr)}A_{i}\tilde{\mu}^{Y}(1,S_{i},X_{i})=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathinner{\biggl(1-\frac{1}{\pi(S_{i})}\biggr)}A_{i}\tilde{\mu}^{Y}(1,S_{i},X_{i})+o_{p}(1).

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle R_{n,1}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathinner{\biggl(1-\frac{1}{\pi(S_{i})}\biggr)}A_{i}\tilde{\mu}^{Y}(1,S_{i},X_{i})+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(1-A_{i})\tilde{\mu}^{Y}(1,S_{i},X_{i})+o_{p}(1).

The linear expansion of
R_{n,2}
can be established in the same manner.
For
R_{n,3}
, note that

\displaystyle Y_{i}

\displaystyle=Y_{i}(1)\mathinner{\bigl[D_{i}(1)A_{i}+D_{i}(0)(1-A_{i})\bigr]}+Y_{i}(0)\mathinner{\bigl[1-D_{i}(1)A_{i}-D_{i}(0)(1-A_{i})\bigr]}

\displaystyle=\mathinner{\bigl[Y_{i}(1)D_{i}(1)-Y_{i}(0)D_{i}(1)\bigr]}A_{i}+\mathinner{\bigl[Y_{i}(1)D_{i}(0)-Y_{i}(0)D_{i}(0)\bigr]}(1-A_{i})+Y_{i}(0).

Then

\displaystyle A_{i}Y_{i}

\displaystyle=\mathinner{\bigl[Y_{i}(1)D_{i}(1)+Y_{i}(0)(1-D_{i}(1))\bigr]}A_{i},

\displaystyle(1-A_{i})Y_{i}

\displaystyle=\mathinner{\bigl[Y_{i}(1)D_{i}(0)+Y_{i}(0)(1-D_{i}(0))\bigr]}(1-A_{i}),

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{A_{i}Y_{i}}{\hat{\pi}(S_{i})}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{1}{\hat{\pi}(S_{i})}\mathinner{\bigl[Y_{i}(1)D_{i}(1)+Y_{i}(0)(1-D_{i}(1))\bigr]}A_{i}=\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{1}{\hat{\pi}(S_{i})}W_{i}A_{i},

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{(1-A_{i})Y_{i}}{1-\hat{\pi}(S_{i})}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\mathinner{\bigl[Y_{i}(1)D_{i}(0)+Y_{i}(0)(1-D_{i}(0))\bigr]}(1-A_{i})}{1-\hat{\pi}(S_{i})}=\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{Z_{i}(1-A_{i})}{1-\hat{\pi}(S_{i})}.

Thus we have

\displaystyle R_{n,3}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{A_{i}Y_{i}}{\hat{\pi}(S_{i})}-\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{(1-A_{i})Y_{i}}{1-\hat{\pi}(S_{i})}-\sqrt{n}G

\displaystyle=\left\{\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{1}{\hat{\pi}(S_{i})}\tilde{W}_{i}A_{i}-\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{1-A_{i}}{1-\hat{\pi}(S_{i})}\tilde{Z}_{i}\right\}

\displaystyle+\left\{\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{1}{\hat{\pi}(S_{i})}\mathbb{E}[W_{i}|S_{i}]A_{i}-\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{1-A_{i}}{1-\hat{\pi}(S_{i})}\mathbb{E}[Z_{i}|S_{i}]-\sqrt{n}G\right\}.

(P.3)

We now consider the second term on the RHS of (
P.3
). First note that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{1}{\hat{\pi}(S_{i})}\mathbb{E}[W_{i}|S_{i}]A_{i}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{1}{\pi(S_{i})}\mathbb{E}[W_{i}|S_{i}]A_{i}-\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\hat{\pi}(S_{i})-\pi(S_{i})}{\hat{\pi}(S_{i})\pi(S_{i})}\mathbb{E}[W_{i}|S_{i}]A_{i},

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{1}{\pi(S_{i})}\mathbb{E}[W_{i}|S_{i}]A_{i}=\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{1}{\pi(s)}\mathbb{E}[W_{i}|S_{i}=s]A_{i}1\{S_{i}=s\}

\displaystyle=\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\mathbb{E}[W_{i}|S_{i}=s]}{\pi(s)}(A_{i}-\pi(s))1\{S_{i}=s\}+\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{1}{\pi(s)}\mathbb{E}[W_{i}|S_{i}=s]\pi(s)1\{S_{i}=s\}

\displaystyle=\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\frac{\mathbb{E}[W|S=s]}{\pi(s)\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(A_{i}-\pi(s))1\{S_{i}=s\}+\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\frac{\mathbb{E}[W|S=s]}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}1\{S_{i}=s\}

\displaystyle=\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\frac{\mathbb{E}[W|S=s]}{\pi(s)\sqrt{n}}B_{n}(s)+\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\frac{\mathbb{E}[W|S=s]}{\sqrt{n}}n(s),

(P.4)

and

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\hat{\pi}(S_{i})-\pi(S_{i})}{\hat{\pi}(S_{i})\pi(S_{i})}\mathbb{E}[W_{i}|S_{i}]A_{i}=\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\hat{\pi}(s)-\pi(s)}{\hat{\pi}(s)\pi(s)}\mathbb{E}[W_{i}|S_{i}=s]A_{i}1\{S_{i}=s\}

\displaystyle=\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{B_{n}(s)}{n(s)\hat{\pi}(s)\pi(s)}\mathbb{E}[W_{i}|S_{i}=s]A_{i}1\{S_{i}=s\}

\displaystyle=\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\frac{B_{n}(s)\mathbb{E}[W|S=s]}{\sqrt{n}n(s)\hat{\pi}(s)\pi(s)}\sum_{i=1}^{n}A_{i}1\{S_{i}=s\}=\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\frac{B_{n}(s)\mathbb{E}[W|S=s]}{\sqrt{n}n(s)\hat{\pi}(s)\pi(s)}n_{1}(s)

\displaystyle=\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\frac{B_{n}(s)\mathbb{E}[W|S=s]}{\sqrt{n}\pi(s)}.

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{1}{\hat{\pi}(S_{i})}\mathbb{E}[W_{i}|S_{i}]A_{i}=\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\frac{\mathbb{E}[W|S=s]}{\sqrt{n}}n(s).

Similarly, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{1-A_{i}}{1-\hat{\pi}(S_{i})}\mathbb{E}[Z_{i}|S_{i}]=\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\frac{\mathbb{E}[Z|S=s]}{\sqrt{n}}n(s)

Then, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{1}{\hat{\pi}(S_{i})}\mathbb{E}[W_{i}|S_{i}]A_{i}-\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{1-A_{i}}{1-\hat{\pi}(S_{i})}\mathbb{E}[Z_{i}|S_{i}]-\sqrt{n}G

\displaystyle=\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\frac{\mathbb{E}[W|S=s]}{\sqrt{n}}n(s)-\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\frac{\mathbb{E}[Z|S=s]}{\sqrt{n}}n(s)-\sqrt{n}G

\displaystyle=\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\sqrt{n}\mathinner{\biggl(\frac{n(s)}{n}-p(s)\biggr)}\mathbb{E}[W-Z|S=s]+\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\sqrt{n}p(s)\mathbb{E}[W-Z|S=s]-\sqrt{n}G

\displaystyle=\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\sqrt{n}\mathinner{\biggl(\frac{n(s)}{n}-p(s)\biggr)}\mathbb{E}[W-Z|S=s]+\sqrt{n}\mathbb{E}[W-Z]-\sqrt{n}G

\displaystyle=\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\frac{n(s)}{\sqrt{n}}\mathbb{E}[W-Z|S=s]-\sqrt{n}\mathbb{E}[W-Z]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathinner{\Bigl(1\{S_{i}=s\}\mathbb{E}[W_{i}-Z_{i}|S_{i}=s]\Bigr)}-\sqrt{n}\mathbb{E}[W-Z]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}[W_{i}-Z_{i}|S_{i}]-\sqrt{n}\mathbb{E}[W-Z]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathinner{\bigl(\mathbb{E}[W_{i}-Z_{i}|S_{i}]-\mathbb{E}[W_{i}-Z_{i}]\bigr)}.

(P.5)

Combining (
P.3
) and (
P.5
), we have

\displaystyle R_{n,3}

\displaystyle=\left\{\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{1}{\hat{\pi}(S_{i})}\tilde{W}_{i}A_{i}-\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{1-A_{i}}{1-\hat{\pi}(S_{i})}\tilde{Z}_{i}\right\}+\left\{\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathinner{\bigl(\mathbb{E}[W_{i}-Z_{i}|S_{i}]-\mathbb{E}[W_{i}-Z_{i}]\bigr)}\right\}

\displaystyle=\left\{\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{1}{\pi(S_{i})}\tilde{W}_{i}A_{i}-\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{1-A_{i}}{1-\pi(S_{i})}\tilde{Z}_{i}\right\}

\displaystyle+\left\{\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathinner{\bigl(\mathbb{E}[W_{i}-Z_{i}|S_{i}]-\mathbb{E}[W_{i}-Z_{i}]\bigr)}\right\}+o_{p}(1),

where the second equality holds because

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{\pi(s)}-\frac{1}{\hat{\pi}(s)}\right)\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\tilde{W}_{i}A_{i}1\{S_{i}=s\}=o_{p}(1)\quad\text{and}

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{\pi(s)}-\frac{1}{\hat{\pi}(s)}\right)\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\tilde{Z}_{i}(1-A_{i})1\{S_{i}=s\}=o_{p}(1)

due to the same argument used in the proofs of
R_{n,1}
.
∎

Lemma P.2
.

Under the assumptions in Theorem
3.1
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Xi_{1}(\mathcal{D}_{i},S_{i})A_{i}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{N}\left(0,\mathbb{E}\pi(S_{i})\Xi_{1}^{2}(\mathcal{D}_{i},S_{i})\right),

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Xi_{0}(\mathcal{D}_{i},S_{i})(1-A_{i})\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{N}\left(0,\mathbb{E}(1-\pi(S_{i}))\Xi_{0}^{2}(\mathcal{D}_{i},S_{i})\right),\quad\text{and}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Xi_{2}(S_{i})\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{N}(0,\mathbb{E}\Xi_{2}^{2}(S_{i})),

and the three terms are asymptotically independent.

Proof.

Note that under Assumption
1
(i), conditional on
\{S^{(n)},A^{(n)}\}
, the distribution of

\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Xi_{1}(\mathcal{D}_{i},S_{i})A_{i},~\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Xi_{0}(\mathcal{D}_{i},S_{i})(1-A_{i})\right)

is the same as the distribution of the same quantity where units are ordered by strata and then ordered by
A_{i}=1
first and
A_{i}=0
second within strata. To this end, define
N(s)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}1\{S_{i}<s\}
and
F(s)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\mathbb{P}(S_{i}<s)
. Furthermore, independently for each
s\in\mathcal{S}
and independently of
\{S^{(n)},A^{(n)}\}
, let
\mathinner{\bigl\{\mathcal{D}_{i}^{s}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}1\leq i\leq n\bigr\}}
be i.i.d with marginal distribution equal to the distribution of
\mathcal{D}|S=s
. Then, we have

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Xi_{1}(\mathcal{D}_{i},S_{i})A_{i},~\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Xi_{0}(\mathcal{D}_{i},S_{i})(1-A_{i})\right)\biggl|S^{(n)},A^{(n)}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\sum_{i=N(s)+1}^{N(s)+n_{1}(s)}\Xi_{1}(\mathcal{D}_{i}^{s},s),~\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\sum_{N(s)+n_{1}(s)+1}^{N(s)+n(s)}\Xi_{0}(\mathcal{D}_{i}^{s},s)\right)\biggl|S^{(n)},A^{(n)}.

In addition, since
\Xi_{2}(S_{i})
is a function of
\{S^{(n)},A^{(n)}\}
, we have, arguing along the line of a joint distribution being the product of a conditional distribution and a marginal distribution,

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Xi_{1}(\mathcal{D}_{i},S_{i})A_{i},~\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Xi_{0}(\mathcal{D}_{i},S_{i})(1-A_{i}),~\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Xi_{2}(S_{i})\right)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\sum_{i=N(s)+1}^{N(s)+n_{1}(s)}\Xi_{1}(\mathcal{D}_{i}^{s},s),~\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\sum_{N(s)+n_{1}(s)+1}^{N(s)+n(s)}\Xi_{0}(\mathcal{D}_{i}^{s},s),~\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Xi_{2}(S_{i})\right).

Define
\Gamma_{a,n}(u,s)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{\lfloor un\rfloor}\Xi_{a}(\mathcal{D}_{i}^{s},s)
for
a=0,1,s\in\mathcal{S}
. We have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\sum_{i=N(s)+1}^{N(s)+n_{1}(s)}\Xi_{1}(\mathcal{D}_{i}^{s},s)=\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\left[\Gamma_{1,n}\left(\frac{N(s)+n_{1}(s)}{n},s\right)-\Gamma_{1,n}\left(\frac{N(s)}{n},s\right)\right],

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\sum_{N(s)+n_{1}(s)+1}^{N(s)+n(s)}\Xi_{0}(\mathcal{D}_{i}^{s},s)=\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\left[\Gamma_{0,n}\left(\frac{N(s)+n(s)}{n},s\right)-\Gamma_{0,n}\left(\frac{N(s)+n_{1}(s)}{n},s\right)\right].

In addition, the partial sum process (w.r.t.
u\in[0,1]
) is stochastic equicontinuous and

\displaystyle\mathinner{\biggl(\frac{N(s)}{n},\frac{n_{1}(s)}{n}\biggr)}\xrightarrow{p}\mathinner{\bigl(F(s),\pi(s)p(s)\bigr)}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\sum_{i=N(s)+1}^{N(s)+n_{1}(s)}\Xi_{1}(\mathcal{D}_{i}^{s},s),~\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\sum_{N(s)+n_{1}(s)+1}^{N(s)+n(s)}\Xi_{0}(\mathcal{D}_{i}^{s},s),~\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Xi_{2}(S_{i})\right)

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}&\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\left[\Gamma_{1,n}\left(F(s)+p(s)\pi(s),s\right)-\Gamma_{1,n}\left(F(s),s\right)\right],\\
&\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\left[\Gamma_{0,n}\left(F(s)+p(s),s\right)-\Gamma_{0,n}\left(F(s)+\pi(s)p(s),s\right)\right],\\
&~\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Xi_{2}(S_{i})\end{pmatrix}+o_{p}(1)

and by construction,

\displaystyle\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\left[\Gamma_{1,n}\left(F(s)+p(s)\pi(s),s\right)-\Gamma_{1,n}\left(F(s),s\right)\right],

\displaystyle\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\left[\Gamma_{0,n}\left(F(s)+p(s),s\right)-\Gamma_{0,n}\left(F(s)+p(s)\pi(s),s\right)\right],

\displaystyle\text{and}\quad\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Xi_{2}(S_{i})

are independent. Last, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\left[\Gamma_{1,n}\left(F(s)+p(s)\pi(s),s\right)-\Gamma_{1,n}\left(F(s),s\right)\right]\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{N}\left(0,\mathbb{E}\pi(S_{i})\Xi_{1}^{2}(\mathcal{D}_{i},S_{i})\right)

\displaystyle\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\left[\Gamma_{0,n}\left(F(s)+p(s),s\right)-\Gamma_{0,n}\left(F(s)+p(s)\pi(s),s\right)\right]\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{N}\left(0,\mathbb{E}(1-\pi(S_{i}))\Xi_{0}^{2}(\mathcal{D}_{i},S_{i})\right)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Xi_{2}(S_{i})\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{N}\left(0,\mathbb{E}\Xi_{2}^{2}(S_{i})\right).

This implies the desired result.
∎

Lemma P.3
.

Suppose assumptions in Theorem
3.1
hold. Then,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}A_{i}\hat{\Xi}_{1}^{2}(\mathcal{D}_{i},S_{i})\overset{p}{\longrightarrow}\sigma_{1}^{2},\quad\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(1-A_{i})\hat{\Xi}_{0}^{2}(\mathcal{D}_{i},S_{i})\overset{p}{\longrightarrow}\sigma_{0}^{2},\quad\text{and}\quad\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\hat{\Xi}_{2}^{2}(\mathcal{D}_{i},S_{i})\overset{p}{\longrightarrow}\sigma_{2}^{2}.

Proof.

To derive the limit of
\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}A_{i}\hat{\Xi}_{1}^{2}(\mathcal{D}_{i},S_{i})
, we first define

\displaystyle\tilde{\Xi}_{1}^{*}(\mathcal{D}_{i},s)

\displaystyle=\left[\left(1-\frac{1}{\pi(s)}\right)\overline{\mu}^{Y}(1,s,X_{i})-\overline{\mu}^{Y}(0,s,X_{i})+\frac{Y_{i}}{\pi(s)}\right]

\displaystyle-\tau\left[\left(1-\frac{1}{\pi(s)}\right)\overline{\mu}^{D}(1,s,X_{i})-\overline{\mu}^{D}(0,s,X_{i})+\frac{D_{i}}{\pi(s)}\right]\quad\text{and}

\displaystyle\breve{\Xi}_{1}(\mathcal{D}_{i},s)

\displaystyle=\left[\left(1-\frac{1}{\hat{\pi}(s)}\right)\overline{\mu}^{Y}(1,s,X_{i})-\overline{\mu}^{Y}(0,s,X_{i})+\frac{Y_{i}}{\hat{\pi}(s)}\right]

\displaystyle-\hat{\tau}\left[\left(1-\frac{1}{\hat{\pi}(s)}\right)\overline{\mu}^{D}(1,s,X_{i})-\overline{\mu}^{D}(0,s,X_{i})+\frac{D_{i}}{\hat{\pi}(s)}\right]

Then, we have

\displaystyle\left[\frac{1}{n_{1}(s)}\sum_{i\in I_{1}(s)}(\tilde{\Xi}_{1}^{*}(\mathcal{D}_{i},s)-\tilde{\Xi}_{1}(\mathcal{D}_{i},s))^{2}\right]^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\left[\frac{1}{n_{1}(s)}\sum_{i\in I_{1}(s)}(\tilde{\Xi}_{1}^{*}(\mathcal{D}_{i},s)-\breve{\Xi}_{1}(\mathcal{D}_{i},s))^{2}\right]^{1/2}+\left[\frac{1}{n_{1}(s)}\sum_{i\in I_{1}(s)}(\tilde{\Xi}_{1}(\mathcal{D}_{i},s)-\breve{\Xi}_{1}(\mathcal{D}_{i},s))^{2}\right]^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{|\hat{\pi}(s)-\pi(s)|}{\hat{\pi}(s)\pi(s)}\biggl\{\mathinner{\biggl[\frac{1}{n_{1}(s)}\sum_{i\in I_{1}(s)}\overline{\mu}^{Y,2}(1,s,X_{i})\biggr]}^{1/2}+\mathinner{\biggl[\frac{1}{n_{1}(s)}\sum_{i\in I_{1}(s)}W_{i}^{2}\biggr]}^{1/2}\biggr\}

\displaystyle\quad+\left(|\hat{\tau}-\tau|+\frac{|\tau\hat{\pi}(s)-\hat{\tau}\pi(s)|}{\hat{\pi}(s)\pi(s)}\right)\biggl\{\mathinner{\biggl[\frac{1}{n_{1}(s)}\sum_{i\in I_{1}(s)}\overline{\mu}^{D,2}(1,s,X_{i})\biggr]}^{1/2}+\mathinner{\biggl[\frac{1}{n_{1}(s)}\sum_{i\in I_{1}(s)}D_{i}^{2}(1)\biggr]}^{1/2}\biggr\}

\displaystyle\quad+|\hat{\tau}-\tau|\mathinner{\biggl[\frac{1}{n_{1}(s)}\sum_{i\in I_{1}(s)}\overline{\mu}^{D,2}(0,s,X_{i})\biggr]}^{1/2}

\displaystyle\quad+\left(\frac{1}{\hat{\pi}(s)}-1\right)\biggl\{\mathinner{\biggl[\frac{1}{n_{1}(s)}\sum_{i\in I_{1}(s)}\Delta^{Y,2}(1,s,X_{i})\biggr]}^{1/2}+|\hat{\tau}|\mathinner{\biggl[\frac{1}{n_{1}(s)}\sum_{i\in I_{1}(s)}\Delta^{D,2}(1,s,X_{i})\biggr]}^{1/2}\biggr\}

\displaystyle\quad+\mathinner{\biggl[\frac{1}{n_{1}(s)}\sum_{i\in I_{1}(s)}\Delta^{Y,2}(0,s,X_{i})\biggr]}^{1/2}+|\hat{\tau}|\mathinner{\biggl[\frac{1}{n_{1}(s)}\sum_{i\in I_{1}(s)}\Delta^{D,2}(0,s,X_{i})\biggr]}^{1/2}=o_{p}(1),

where the second inequality holds by the triangle inequality and the fact that when
i\in I_{1}(s)
,
A_{i}=1
,
Y_{i}=W_{i}
, and
D_{i}=D_{i}(1)
, and the last equality is due to Assumption
3
(ii) and the facts that
\hat{\pi}(s)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\longrightarrow}}\pi(s)
and
\hat{\tau}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\longrightarrow}}\tau
. This further implies

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n_{1}(s)}\sum_{i\in I_{1}(s)}(\tilde{\Xi}_{1}^{*}(\mathcal{D}_{i},s)-\tilde{\Xi}_{1}(\mathcal{D}_{i},s))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\longrightarrow}}0,

by the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and thus,

\displaystyle\left[\frac{1}{n_{1}(s)}\sum_{i\in I_{1}(s)}\hat{\Xi}_{1}^{2}(\mathcal{D}_{i},s)\right]^{1/2}\leq\left[\frac{1}{n_{1}(s)}\sum_{i\in I_{1}(s)}\left(\tilde{\Xi}_{1}^{*}(\mathcal{D}_{i},s)-\frac{1}{n_{1}}\sum_{i\in I_{1}(s)}\tilde{\Xi}_{1}^{*}(\mathcal{D}_{i},s)\right)^{2}\right]^{1/2}+o_{p}(1).

Next, following the same argument in the proof of Lemma
P.2
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n_{1}(s)}\sum_{i\in I_{1}(s)}\tilde{\Xi}_{1}^{*}(\mathcal{D}_{i},s)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\frac{1}{n_{1}(s)}\sum_{i=N(s)+1}^{N(s)+n_{1}(s)}\biggl\{\left[\left(1-\frac{1}{\pi(s)}\right)\overline{\mu}^{Y}(1,s,X_{i}^{s})-\overline{\mu}^{Y}(0,s,X_{i}^{s})+\frac{W_{i}^{s}}{\pi(s)}\right]

\displaystyle\qquad-\tau\left[\left(1-\frac{1}{\pi(s)}\right)\overline{\mu}^{D}(1,s,X_{i}^{s})-\overline{\mu}^{D}(0,s,X_{i}^{s})+\frac{D_{i}^{s}(1)}{\pi(s)}\right]\biggr\}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathbb{E}\biggl\{\left[\left(1-\frac{1}{\pi(S_{i})}\right)\overline{\mu}^{Y}(1,S_{i},X_{i})-\overline{\mu}^{Y}(0,S_{i},X_{i})+\frac{W_{i}}{\pi(S_{i})}\right]

\displaystyle\qquad-\tau\left[\left(1-\frac{1}{\pi(S_{i})}\right)\overline{\mu}^{D}(1,S_{i},X_{i})-\overline{\mu}^{D}(0,S_{i},X_{i})+\frac{D_{i}(1)}{\pi(S_{i})}\right]|S_{i}=s\biggr\},

This implies

\displaystyle\left[\frac{1}{n_{1}(s)}\sum_{i\in I_{1}(s)}\left(\tilde{\Xi}_{1}^{*}(\mathcal{D}_{i},s)-\frac{1}{n_{1}}\sum_{i\in I_{1}(s)}\tilde{\Xi}_{1}^{*}(\mathcal{D}_{i},s)\right)^{2}\right]^{1/2}

\displaystyle=\biggl[\frac{1}{n_{1}(s)}\sum_{i\in I_{1}(s)}\biggl(\tilde{\Xi}_{1}^{*}(\mathcal{D}_{i},s)-\mathbb{E}\biggl\{\left[\left(1-\frac{1}{\pi(S_{i})}\right)\overline{\mu}^{Y}(1,S_{i},X_{i})-\overline{\mu}^{Y}(0,S_{i},X_{i})+\frac{W_{i}}{\pi(S_{i})}\right]

\displaystyle\qquad-\tau\left[\left(1-\frac{1}{\pi(S_{i})}\right)\overline{\mu}^{D}(1,S_{i},X_{i})-\overline{\mu}^{D}(0,S_{i},X_{i})+\frac{D_{i}(1)}{\pi(S_{i})}\right]|S_{i}=s\biggr\}\biggr)^{2}\biggr]^{1/2}+o_{p}(1)

\displaystyle=\biggl[\frac{1}{n_{1}(s)}\sum_{i\in I_{1}(s)}\biggl(\left[\left(1-\frac{1}{\pi(S_{i})}\right)\tilde{\mu}^{Y}(1,S_{i},X_{i})-\tilde{\mu}^{Y}(0,S_{i},X_{i})+\frac{\tilde{W}_{i}}{\pi(S_{i})}\right]

\displaystyle\qquad-\tau\left[\left(1-\frac{1}{\pi(S_{i})}\right)\tilde{\mu}^{D}(1,S_{i},X_{i})-\tilde{\mu}^{D}(0,S_{i},X_{i})+\frac{\tilde{D}_{i}(1)}{\pi(S_{i})}\right]\biggr)^{2}\biggr]^{1/2}+o_{p}(1).

Last, following the same argument in the proof of Lemma
P.2
, we have

\displaystyle\biggl[\frac{1}{n_{1}(s)}\sum_{i\in I_{1}(s)}\biggl(\left[\left(1-\frac{1}{\pi(S_{i})}\right)\tilde{\mu}^{Y}(1,S_{i},X_{i})-\tilde{\mu}^{Y}(0,S_{i},X_{i})+\frac{\tilde{W}_{i}}{\pi(S_{i})}\right]

\displaystyle-\tau\left[\left(1-\frac{1}{\pi(S_{i})}\right)\tilde{\mu}^{D}(1,S_{i},X_{i})-\tilde{\mu}^{D}(0,S_{i},X_{i})+\frac{\tilde{D}_{i}(1)}{\pi(S_{i})}\right]\biggr)^{2}\biggr]^{1/2}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\biggl[\frac{1}{n_{1}(s)}\sum_{i=N(s)+1}^{N(s)+n_{1}(s)}\biggl(\left[\left(1-\frac{1}{\pi(s)}\right)\tilde{\mu}^{Y}(1,s,X_{i}^{s})-\tilde{\mu}^{Y}(0,s,X_{i}^{s})+\frac{\tilde{W}_{i}^{s}}{\pi(s)}\right]

\displaystyle-\tau\left[\left(1-\frac{1}{\pi(s)}\right)\tilde{\mu}^{D}(1,s,X_{i}^{s})-\tilde{\mu}^{D}(0,s,X_{i}^{s})+\frac{\tilde{D}_{i}^{s}(1)}{\pi(s)}\right]\biggr)^{2}\biggr]^{1/2}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\longrightarrow}}\left[\mathbb{E}(\Xi_{1}^{2}(\mathcal{D}_{i},S_{i})|S_{i}=s)\right]^{1/2},

where
\tilde{W}_{i}^{s}=W_{i}^{s}-\mathbb{E}(W_{i}|S_{i}=s)
and
\tilde{D}_{i}^{s}(1)=D_{i}^{s}(1)-\mathbb{E}(D_{i}(1)|S_{i}=s)
and the last convergence is due to the fact that conditionally on
S^{(n)},A^{(n)}
,
\{X_{i}^{s},\tilde{W}_{i}^{s},\tilde{D}_{i}^{s}(1)\}_{i\in I_{1}(s)}
is a sequence of i.i.d. random variables so that the standard LLN is applicable. Combining all the results above, we have shown that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n_{1}(s)}\sum_{i\in I_{1}(s)}\hat{\Xi}_{1}^{2}(\mathcal{D}_{i},S_{i})

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{p}\mathbb{E}(\Xi_{1}^{2}(\mathcal{D}_{i},S_{i})|S_{i}=s)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}A_{i}\hat{\Xi}_{1}^{2}(\mathcal{D}_{i},S_{i})

\displaystyle=\sum_{s\in S}\frac{n_{1}(s)}{n}\mathinner{\biggl(\frac{1}{n_{1}(s)}\sum_{i\in I_{1}(s)}\hat{\Xi}_{1}^{2}(\mathcal{D}_{i},S_{i})\biggr)}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\longrightarrow}}\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}p(s)\pi(s)\mathbb{E}(\Xi_{1}^{2}(\mathcal{D}_{i},S_{i})|S_{i}=s)=\mathbb{E}\mathinner{\Bigl[\pi(S_{i})\mathbb{E}(\Xi_{1}^{2}(\mathcal{D}_{i},S_{i})|S_{i})\Bigr]}=\sigma_{1}^{2}.

For the same reason, we can show that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(1-A_{i})\hat{\Xi}_{0}^{2}(\mathcal{D}_{i},S_{i})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\longrightarrow}}\sigma_{0}^{2}.

Last, by the similar argument, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\hat{\Xi}_{2}^{2}(S_{i})

\displaystyle=\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\frac{n(s)}{n}\hat{\Xi}_{2}^{2}(s)

\displaystyle=\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\frac{n(s)}{n}(\mathbb{E}(W_{i}-\tau D_{i}(1)|S_{i}=s)-\mathbb{E}(Z_{i}-\tau D_{i}(0)|S_{i}=s))^{2}+o_{p}(1)

\displaystyle=\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\frac{n(s)}{n}\Xi_{2}^{2}(s)+o_{p}(1)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\longrightarrow}}\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}p(s)\Xi_{2}^{2}(s)=\mathbb{E}\Xi_{2}^{2}(S_{i})=\sigma_{2}^{2}.

∎

Appendix Q
An Additional Simulation

In this section, we use an additional simulation to demonstrate that when probabilities of treatment assignment
\{\pi(s)\}
are heterogeneous across strata, the TSLS estimator could be inconsistent. The data generating process we consider here, denoted DGP(iv), is almost the same as DGP(i) in Section
6
; the only difference is in
Y_{i}(a)
:

\displaystyle Y_{i}(1)=

\displaystyle 2+S_{i}^{2}+0.7X_{1,i}^{2}+X_{2,i}+4Z_{i}+\varepsilon_{1,i}

\displaystyle Y_{i}(0)=

\displaystyle 1+0.7X_{1,i}^{2}+X_{2,i}+4Z_{i}+\varepsilon_{2,i}.

The rationale for specifying this DGP is to allow a difference between the probabilistic limit of
\hat{\tau}_{TSLS}
and
\tau
. We consider randomization schemes SRS and SBR with
(\pi(1),\pi(2),\pi(3),\pi(4))=(0.2,0.2,0.2,0.5)
. We do not consider randomization scheme WEI or BCD because for these two,
\pi(s)=0.5
for all
s\in\mathcal{S}
. The rest of the simulation setting is the same as DGP(i) in Section
6
. Table
6
presents the empirical sizes. We see that all estimators, except the TSLS estimator, have the empirical sizes converging to 0.05 as sample size increases.

NA, TSLS, L, S, NL, F, NP, SNP, R stand for the unadjusted estimator, TSLS estimator, optimally linearly adjusted estimator,
Ansel
et al. 2018
’s S estimator with
X_{i}
as regressor, nonlinearly (logistic) adjusted estiamtor, further efficiency improving estimator, nonparametrically adjusted estimator,
Ansel
et al. 2018
’s S estimator with
\mathring{\Psi}_{i,n}
defined in (
6.1
) as regressor, and estimator with regularized adjustments, respectively.
